## 0. Actors and the functions that identify them

| Actor | How the code identifies it | Function |
|---|---|---|
| Anonymous | No check | none |
| Signed-in login | Cookie `thakeen_session` to `session:h:{sha256(token)}`, or the legacy `session:{token}` of a session written before 2026-10-12 (rule 1345; `{email, at, imp?}`) to the LOGIN it names: an owner record `user:{email}` or a member record `member:{email}` (the owner record wins should both exist). It always returns the OWNER's `User`, which is the account, with a runtime-only `actor` saying who is acting, plus `brandId` and `scope`. An owner who has not verified the address resolves to `null` here (the verification gate, 0.1 item 10). The refusals are in 0.1. Since 2026-10-18 (account-id R1, rule 1984) the session also carries `pid` and `acct` and resolves only while `login:{email}` names that person and `acct` is the login's workspace; the account is read by id (`getAccount`), the address only finds the login (`getLogin`, guarded fallback to `user:{email}` for accounts from before R1). | `currentUser()` and `resolveSession` in `src/lib/auth.ts` |
| Account owner | The login whose `user:{email}` record is the account: actor `{kind:"owner", role:"owner"}`. Created by signup; an owner counts only once `emailVerifiedAt` is set (2026-10-08, rule 751). The only role that can pay, delete the account, write governance, mint the MCP token, approve OAuth apps or manage admins (0.1). | `currentUser()` |
| Member of an account (role `admin` or `member`) | A login at `member:{email}` whose `memberOf` names the owner, created only by accepting an invite. Since multi-workspace M1 (2026-10-19, rules 2115 and 2125) a role belongs to a membership, `mbr:{aid}:{pid}` listed on the roster `members:{aid}`; the legacy record's single `memberOf` is now the person's HOME membership, still written beside its row so an older build reads it, and the session's active account decides which membership acts. In M1 every member holds one membership (accepting an invite with an address that already signs in is refused while `MULTI_ORG` is off). Resolves to the OWNER's account: every read and write uses the owner's workspace. Actor `{kind:"member", email: the member's own address, role}`. Never in `users:all`, never given credits, a referral code or signup tracking. | `currentUser()`; records in `src/lib/team.ts` |
| Person (2026-10-19, multi-workspace M1) | The human behind a session: `person:{pid}`, else the legacy owner or member record (`getPerson`, rule 2134). A session written from R1 on names its person (`pid`) and its ACTIVE account (`acct`); `resolveSession` re-checks the person (index, suspension, stamps) and the person's role in that account (`roleIn`, `membershipHolds`) on every request. Every actor carries the person's `pid` (`actor.pid`; an owner's and a token's is `ownerOf(u)`) and `home` (the account is the person's legacy home). A suspended person resolves in no account and through no token (rule 2131). Caveat: a session written before R1 (no `pid`) resolves through `resolveLegacy`, which checks the account's and the member's suspension but not the person's, so person suspension is not checked for pid-less sessions until M3/M4 adds it to that path (before any writer of person suspension ships; nothing writes it in M1; `flows.md` section 93.1, "Legacy sessions and person suspension"). The account's owner is the person `ownerOf(u)` names (`ownerPid`, else the legacy owner); the acting login's own password, address and birth are the PERSON's (`actingLogin`, `ownPasswordMatches`), and every credential write goes through `updatePerson` (`flows.md` section 5 "Credential writes"). | `currentUser()`; `currentPerson()` for the person without an account (the workspaces flow, M2; no caller in M1) |
| Person with no workspace (multi-workspace M2, only with `MULTI_ORG` on) | A `p_` person whose last membership was left, removed or deleted, or who holds only paused ones: `getLogin` answers a member login with `owner` and `acct` null, sign-in writes a session with `pid` and no `acct`, `currentUser()` is `null` and `currentPerson()` is the person, so the app layout sends it to `/{lang}/app/workspaces` (rules 2124, 2126). The address stays taken (a sign-up answers 409). | `currentPerson()` |
| Account of the resource | Every workspace reader and writer takes the caller's scope `sc(u)` (`src/lib/auth.ts`), built from the OWNER email and the acting login's active brand, so ids from the request only resolve inside the account's own workspace. The owner and the account's members share that workspace; nobody outside the account can be given access to it. | `sc(u)` plus the scoped getters in `src/lib/brand.ts`, `files.ts`, `conversations.ts`, `connectors/store.ts` |
| Founder impersonating an account | A session written by the console's `impersonate` action carries `imp` (the founder's email, or `"key"` for the console key): actor `{kind:"founder", role:"owner", impersonating}`. Refused every action in `IMPERSONATION_BLOCKED` (0.1) and the founder console itself. | `currentUser()`, `can()` |
| Owner who has not verified the address (2026-10-08) | The same session and the same owner record, with no `emailVerifiedAt`: `currentUser()` answers `null` (signed out everywhere), `currentUserUnverified()` returns the owner with actor `{kind:"owner", role:"owner"}`. Only the app layout, the verify page and the three `auth/verify/*` routes may call it (rule 752). A founder impersonation (the founder login's or the console key's) and the MCP token of a verified owner are not in this row. A member of such an owner resolves in NEITHER resolver (rule 767): the account spends nothing until the owner verifies, and a member cannot prove the owner's address. | `currentUserUnverified()` and `emailVerified(u)` in `src/lib/auth.ts` |
| Staff (console) (2026-10-13, rules 1630-1654, `claude/staff-identity`, phase 1, expand) | Cookie `__Host-thakeen_staff` (a random id; `HttpOnly`, `Secure`, `SameSite=Strict`, `Path=/`, no `Domain`; `thakeen_staff_dev` only on plain http to a loopback host, rule 1633) to `staffsession:{sha256(id)}` (`{email, at, seen}`: 15 minutes idle, 12 hours at most, renewed at most once a minute with `SET XX`, sign-out deletes it, rule 1634) to `staff:{email}` (name, role, bcrypt cost-12 password hash, the authenticator secret sealed under `CONNECT_SECRET` with its `kid`, hashed recovery codes, `sessionsAfter`). A different record type from any customer `User`: never `currentUser()`, never `sc(u)`, and a customer session for the same address is never a staff actor (rule 1630). Opens only after the password AND a code from the person's own authenticator (rule 1632). Role `owner` writes; any other role is read-only (403 `read_only`), an unknown role fails closed (rule 1637). Resolved first by `adminActor` (after the `x-admin-key` header): actor `{who: email, via: "staff", role}` | `readStaffSession()` in `src/lib/staff-session.ts`; `adminActor(req)`, `consoleIdentity()` in `src/lib/auth.ts` |
| Founder admin | `WAITLIST_ADMIN_KEY` in header `x-admin-key` (30 tries per IP per 15 min, bucket `adminhdr:{ip}`) or a key session opened by the key form (cookie `nashr_admin` = random id, record `adminsession:{sha256(id)}` holding a fingerprint of the key, so rotating the key ends it and logout deletes it; it ends after 15 minutes of inactivity and 12 h after the key entry at most), never in the URL; or a session whose actor is the owner login itself (`kind:"owner"`, actor email equal to the account email) with that email in `ADMIN_EMAILS`. A member of a founder's account and a founder impersonating never pass. Since the staff identity (2026-10-13, phase 1): the key form needs the console code too once `admin:2fa` is enrolled, and a key session is break-glass with a banner (rule 1645); the header stays for scripts and never opens a browser session (rule 1646); the `ADMIN_EMAILS` customer-session path is EXPAND ONLY (rule 1649, `via: "founder-session"`, banner), a staff owner can switch it off at runtime (rule 1650, `staff:config.customerPathOff`), and the contract release removes it (rule 1651, not built). | `adminActor(req)` and `isAdmin(req)` (APIs), `isAdminCookie()` (pages), `isFounderActor(u)`, all in `src/lib/auth.ts` |
| Cron | Header `Authorization: Bearer ${CRON_SECRET}`; denied when the variable is unset. Since 2026-10-20 (rules 2500-2509) the bearer opens the route, not any account: each per-user item acts only for the account and scope its roster entry carries (`jobRef`), read again when it starts (`runAsAccount`, `src/lib/job-account.ts`); a gone, suspended or unverified account, or a brand not active, is skipped before any write, charge or email | inline in each cron route; `src/lib/job-account.ts` |
| MCP bearer | Header `Authorization: Bearer thakeen_...` to `mcptoken:{sha256(token)}` to `user:{email}` (a token stored before 2026-10-05 under its raw value is found once and renamed to its hash); an OAuth token also requires its grant `mcpgrant:{email}:{id}` to exist and list the token's hash (rule 551); refuses a `suspended` account, an owner who has not verified the address (rule 753) and a token whose email is a member login (even should an owner record exist at the same address); sets `scope` from the owner's active brand like a session, only while that brand is one of the account's active brands, else the default brand (rule 3030); actor `{kind:"token", role:"owner"}` | `userFromBearer(req)` `src/lib/mcp.ts` |
| Staging visitor (the staging site only, `APP_ENV=staging`) | Cookie `thakeen_staging` whose value is `HMAC-SHA256(STAGING_ACCESS_KEY, "staging-access-v1")` in hex, compared with `sameSecret`. The cookie never holds the code, and changing the key signs everyone out. Without a valid cookie, or with `STAGING_ACCESS_KEY` missing or empty, only `/ar/staging-access`, `/en/staging-access`, `/api/staging-access` and `/robots.txt` answer (exact match: `/ar/staging-access/`, `/AR/staging-access` and `/ar/staging-access/x` stay locked); every other path is a bare 404, and every staging response carries `X-Robots-Tag: noindex, nofollow` (rules 674 and 677). The cookie only opens the door: it is not a login, and every handler behind it still enforces the rows above. Not applied on the live site, where the proxy checks nothing. | `hasAccess(cookie)` in `src/lib/staging-access.ts`, called by `proxy` in `src/proxy.ts:27` |

There is no global authentication guard. `src/proxy.ts` does not authenticate (`src/proxy.ts:22-56`); every handler enforces its own rule, and a handler without a check is public. Two checks run in the proxy before any handler. On the staging site only (`APP_ENV=staging`), the proxy answers 404 to every path but four without the staging cookie (the last row above); on the live site that branch never runs. Everywhere, since 2026-10-08, the cross-site check on `/api` (`src/proxy.ts:33-37`, `src/lib/csrf.ts`, rules 690-693; item 23 of the known gaps): a POST, PUT, PATCH or DELETE that another site started is refused with 403 `{"error":"cross_site"}` before the handler runs. It reads who started the request, never a session, so it decides nothing about who may act. Exempt (the proxy runs on them, after the staging lock, but skips the check): `/api/mcp`, `/api/oauth/token`, `/api/oauth/register`, `/api/media/hook/*`, `/api/secretary/booking/*`, `/api/billing/paymob/*` and `/api/cron/*`.

### 0.1 Roles, members and seats (2026-09-29)

Files: `src/lib/roles.ts` (the table), `src/lib/team.ts` (member logins, invites, seats), `src/lib/auth.ts` (resolution; it re-exports `PLAN_SEATS` and `seatLimit`), `src/lib/pricing.ts` (the plan table: `PLAN_SEATS`, `seatLimit`, since 2026-09-30). Design: `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-29-teams-design.md`.

1. Three roles. `owner` belongs to the `user:` record only and can never be assigned, removed or transferred. `admin` and `member` belong to member logins (`Member.role`): chosen at invite, changed only by the owner. Since multi-workspace M1 (rules 2125 and 2132; `flows.md` 91), a role belongs to one membership (`mbr:{aid}:{pid}`, read with `getMembership`), and the legacy `Member.role` is written beside it for the home membership. The owner is the person `ownerOf(account)` names. An admin of one account removes only plain members of that account, invites only plain members, and holds nothing in another account: its actor there fails `actorStillHolds`. The owner cannot leave.
2. One table, `MIN_ROLE` in `src/lib/roles.ts`, names the minimum role of each action. `can(u, action)` answers; `forbid(u, action)` returns a 403 `{error:"forbidden"}` or `null`. Routes call it right after `currentUser()`; the chat tools (`TOOL_ACTION` in `src/lib/chat.ts`), MCP dispatch (`MCP_TOOL_ACTION` in `src/lib/mcp.ts`) and the pages use the same function. A control hidden on a page is display only; the route refuses regardless.
3. `can` fails closed. It answers false without a user or an actor, for an unknown action (an own-property test, so `__proto__` is unknown), for a role that is not one of the three, and when kind and role disagree (a member holding `owner`; an owner, token or founder not holding `owner`). `team.leave` is true only for kind `member`. Otherwise the actor's rank (member 1, admin 2, owner 3) must reach `MIN_ROLE[action]`. Users built by the cron (`allUsers()`) carry no actor, so a `can()` check on a cron path refuses.

| Needs | Actions (`MIN_ROLE`) | Covers |
|---|---|---|
| owner | `billing`, `account.delete`, `account.export` (2026-10-12, rule 1320: the account's own data download), `governance.write`, `mcp.token`, `oauth.approve`, `oauth.grants`, `referral`, `partner`, `partner.bank` (2026-10-01: the partner page and the partner's bank details; `partner.bank` is also impersonation-blocked), `calendar.link`, `profile.account`, `team.invite.admin`, `team.role`, `team.remove.admin`, `team.transfer` (M3, rule 2750: the ownership offer, owner actor only, also impersonation-blocked) | Pay links and activation; the chat's "continue on credits" switch (row 114, since 2026-10-01); the billing page's top-up request buttons (since 2026-10-01: the message carries the account email); account deletion; governance writes; the personal MCP token (status, mint, revoke); OAuth consent and connected apps; linking, choosing and unlinking the one calendar Reem reads (`calendar.link`, Reem B3: `GET`, `PUT` and `DELETE /api/secretary/calendar-link` and `GET /api/secretary/calendar-link/calendars` are owner only, the scope is `sc(u)` and a body field such as `scope` or `email` is a 400; a founder's support session is refused on all of them and on the start of the calendar flow, since `calendar.link` is impersonation-blocked (ruling R10): the declaration is the owner's own statement and the link outlives the session; the `gcal` and `mscal` providers are flagged `secretary`, hidden from `GET /api/connect` and refused by `POST` and `DELETE /api/connect/{provider}` with 404 `unknown_provider`); referral data; the account's phone, language and email preferences; inviting or promoting an admin; any role change; removing an admin |
| admin or owner ("admin+") | `brand.write`, `instructions.write`, `instructions.test`, `brands.manage`, `onboard`, `catalog.import`, `connectors`, `schedule.write`, `publish`, `files.core.delete`, `files.core.write`, `performance.delete`, `performance.import`, `team.invite.member`, `team.remove.member`, `team.invites.view`, `team.approvals.view` (2026-10-12, rule 1480: the team page's per-person approval counts), `team.log.view` (M3, rule 2129: the Members page's Team log section), `usage.view` (2026-10-22, rule 2907: the account's own AI requests in Settings → AI usage and `GET /api/usage`, no dollar figure), `team.invite.cancel`, `todo.close` | Brand file writes (route, profile brand fields, chat `update_brand`); brands add, rename, archive; onboarding; catalog import; connectors (start, callback, key, disconnect, page and ad-account choice, product import); schedule and routine switches; approving and publishing (route, inbox approval, chat `publish_item`); deleting a core memory file, and writing one (route and chat `write_file`: writing one empty is deleting it); importing campaign results from a CSV and deleting them (an import replaces the last); inviting and removing plain members; seeing and cancelling pending invites (an admin cancels member invites only) |
| member (every login of the account) | `chat`, `generate`, `images`, `drafts`, `conversations`, `feedback`, `notifications`, `tools`, `support`, `integrations.request`, `todo.write`, `brands.switch`, `read`, `team.view`, `team.leave`, `password.change`, `sessions.revoke`, `mfa.manage`, `email.change`, `google.manage` (2026-10-16, rules 1930-1953, branch: one's own two-step sign-in, sign-in address and Google link; all three impersonation-blocked), `passkeys.manage` (2026-10-20, rule 2412, branch `claude/wave3c-passkeys`: one's own passkeys, list, add, rename, remove; impersonation-blocked), `profile.self`, `library.write` and `library.export` (2026-10-13, rules 1781-1785: the media library and the cloud drives; `library.export` is also impersonation-blocked) | Chat, generation and images (charged to the account); editing, rejecting, reopening and rescheduling drafts; conversations; thumbs; notifications; free tools; support and integration requests; switching one's own active brand; reading everything in the workspace, the store and ad syncs included; the roster; leaving (members only); one's own password, signing out one's own other devices (row 161), name and interface language |

4. Founder impersonation. `IMPERSONATION_BLOCKED` is `team.access`, `team.cap`, `team.policy` (customer roles, 2026-10-23), `account.delete`, `account.export` (2026-10-12, rule 1320: a founder viewing an account never downloads all of it), `password.change`, `team.invite.member`, `team.invite.admin`, `team.invite.cancel`, `team.role`, `team.remove.member`, `team.remove.admin`, `team.transfer` (M3), `mcp.token`, `oauth.approve`, `library.export` (2026-10-13, rule 1785: a founder viewing an account never writes to the customer's own drive), `feed.manage` and `booking.manage` (2026-10-05: a link or key handed out by an impersonator would outlive the session), since 2026-10-01, `partner.bank` (where a partner's commission is paid) and, since 2026-10-08 (Paymob branch), `billing.pay` (the founder never pays with, saves, removes or renews a customer's card). Since UI/UX phase 3 (2026-10-11, branch), `sessions.revoke` (an impersonator never signs the customer's own devices out). Since 2026-10-16 (account security, rules 1930-1953, branch), `mfa.manage`, `email.change` and `google.manage`: an impersonator never turns the customer's two-step sign-in on or off, never moves the sign-in address and never links a Google account to it. Since 2026-10-20 (customer passkeys, rule 2412, branch `claude/wave3c-passkeys`), `passkeys.manage`: an impersonator never lists, adds, renames or removes the customer's passkeys. Since 2026-10-23 (paid seats, rule 3368, branch `claude/sup-paid-seats`), `billing.seats`: staff never add a paid seat to a customer's bill; `can` also refuses it to every actor but `kind: "owner"` (no admin, no token). Actor kind `founder` is refused all of them whatever its role, so nothing that outlives the two-hour session can be created. Everything else the owner may do, a founder impersonating may do.
5. Member refusals (`resolveSession`, on every request). `currentUser()` returns `null` for a member session when the member record is missing (removed, or left), is `disabled` (seat-paused), holds a role other than `admin` or `member`, or has an empty `memberOf` or one equal to its own address; when the session carries `imp`; when the session predates `member.sessionsAfter` (the member's own password change or reset; set to `acceptedAt` on creation); when the owner record is missing or suspended; when the member is not on the owner's roster (`members:{owner}`, `onRoster`); when the session predates `owner.teamSessionsAfter` (owner reset, founder reset); or when the member was accepted before the owner record was created, so a re-registered owner address inherits nothing. Membership is never cached in the session: a removal, a role change or a seat pause applies at the member's next request. Since multi-workspace M1, a removal or a leave deletes the membership row, the roster and `orgs:` entries and the legacy record together under `lock:team` (and `lock:login` → `lock:person` before the first write). A person with no owned account and no other membership loses its login, its two-step sign-in, its Google link and its device sessions (`deleteMembership`, `flows.md` 91 item 1). Owner sessions keep their checks (`suspended`, `sessionsAfter`), and `createUser` now stamps `sessionsAfter` at creation.
6. Scope. Always `scopeFor(owner email, brandId)`. `brandId` comes from `activebrand:{login}`, the acting login's own key; a member's value must be one of the owner's active brands, else `default`. A member's address never becomes part of a workspace key, and `tests/scope-usage.test.ts` fails when a page or customer route passes an actor, member, owner or login email to a workspace reader.
7. Shared by everyone in the account: the credits and the plan (`charge(u.email, ...)`), the per-agent caps and counters, the chat ceiling (`chat:h:{owner}`, `chat:d:{owner}`), the monthly chat allowance (since 2026-10-01: one counter per account, `usage:{owner}:{YYYY-MM}:chat_msgs`, or `chatm:{owner}:trial` on a trial, with `:chat_over` and `:chat_cred` for the messages continued on credits and what they were billed, counting the owner's, every member's, every brand's and every connected app's messages alike; the owner alone decides what happens when it runs out, `billing`, row 114), the notification bell and its read state (`notifs:{owner}`), model usage, and product analytics (a member's login is tracked on the owner email). Recorded since step 3 of the agent-team upgrade: decision records, governance revisions and activity rows carry `by`, the acting login (section 4 item 10), so which login approved, edited, rejected, published or changed governance is kept there. Not recorded: which login chatted; tasks themselves and chat history carry no actor. Support tickets and integration requests record both the acting login (`user` / `email`) and the account (`account`).
8. Seats. `PLAN_SEATS` (`src/lib/pricing.ts`, since 2026-09-30): trial 3, starter 1, pro 3, business 7, the owner included. Agency has no fixed number: `seatLimit` gives it Business's 7 unless the founder sets `User.seats` for its contract (a deal's seats live there, not in the deal). The retired `enterprise` (25) reads as agency. `User.seats`, set in the founder console, overrides every plan when it is a positive number (`seatLimit` floors it; the console accepts whole numbers 1 to 500). Seats used = 1 + members not disabled + unexpired invites, counted under `lock:team:{owner}` at invite and again at accept. Seats are per account (rule 2118, multi-workspace M1): the count reads this account's memberships only, and a person in two accounts uses a seat in each. `enforceSeats` pauses or resumes the membership in this account only (`disabled` on `mbr:{aid}:{pid}`, mirrored to the home legacy record). Each change writes one team log row (`teamlog:{aid}`, via `system`). `enforceSeats` runs after every plan or seat change in `POST /api/admin/plan`, today the only code that changes either.
9. The founder console follows the actor, not the account: `isFounderActor(u)` is true only for the owner login itself with its own email in `ADMIN_EMAILS` (section 4).
10. The verification gate (2026-10-08, rules 751-756, `flows.md` 53). `resolveSession` returns `null` for an owner login whose record has no `emailVerifiedAt`, unless the session is a founder impersonation or the caller is `currentUserUnverified()` (rule 752). A member login is not gated by its owner's state. The gate sits in the one resolver, so a route or page added later is gated without a line of its own. The doors that do not go through `currentUser()` refuse the same owner on their own: `userFromBearer` (rule 753), `dailyRoster()` and the reminder sweep (rule 755), `loginHolds` for the calendar feed and booking links (rule 756). Release consequence: every account that exists on release day is unverified (no grandfathering), the founder's own cookie access to the console included (`WAITLIST_ADMIN_KEY` access does not depend on a login).

### 0.1a Customer roles: Viewer, Editor, brand scope, approval and per-member cap (2026-10-23, rules 3750-3789, `flows.md` 120)

Files: `src/lib/roles.ts`, `src/lib/team.ts`, `src/lib/membership.ts`, `src/lib/approval-policy.ts`, `src/lib/assign.ts`, `src/lib/credits.ts`, `src/lib/stored.ts`. Release step: expand (rule 679); the contract step is in `flows.md` 120.

1. Four roles (rule 3750): owner, admin, Editor and Viewer, ranked viewer 0, editor 1, admin 2, owner 3. The Editor is stored as `"member"` in this release (rule 3751): a request may name it `"editor"` or `"member"` (`teamRoleFrom`), and a stored `"editor"` reads as `"member"` (`MembershipRow`, `TeamLogRow`, the legacy record in `resolveLegacy`). Where a membership row and the legacy record disagree, the stricter role wins (viewer < member < admin). A build before this one parses neither `"viewer"` nor `"editor"`: such a member reads as signed out there, never as a wider role.
2. A Viewer (rule 3752) reads the workspace (reports, calendar, drafts), comments on a draft (`comment`, rule 3758) and manages their own sign-in (password, sessions, two-step, passkeys, address, Google, calendar feed, profile, bell, support, brand switch). Every write, agent run and credit spend is `member` level in `MIN_ROLE` and so refused in code: the routes (chat, feedback, conversations, files writes `files.write`, generate, schedule run now, image and video renders, integrations request, task edits), the chat tools (`TOOL_ACTION`, a Viewer-specific refusal text) and MCP (`MCP_TOOL_ACTION`). `tests/role-gates.test.ts` holds every route to its declared role and lists the five bare-member writes a Viewer may not reach.
3. Seats (rule 3753): ONE count, `seatCount` in `src/lib/team.ts`: the owner, every membership not paused and every unexpired invite, except that the first three Viewers (`FREE_VIEWERS`, counted from memberships then invites) take no seat; the fourth Viewer takes a paid seat like an Editor. `createInvite` and `setRole` check it under `lock:team`. `teamView` returns `{used, limit, freeViewers, freeViewersLeft}`. An admin invites Editors and Viewers only (rule 3754); the owner invites any role.
4. Brand scope (rules 3756-3757, 3759): a membership may carry `brands`, a list of the account's own active brand ids (absent = every brand; a malformed value reads as no brand at all). Set at invite or later by the owner or an unscoped admin (`team.access`), and an admin's own scope by the owner only. Ids are checked against the account's own brand list; anything else is 400 `invalid`. Enforced in the resolvers next to the brand in the URL: a link to a brand outside the scope is the same 404 as a foreign brand, a stored active brand outside it falls back to the first allowed brand, and `/api/brands` lists and switches to allowed brands only (404 otherwise). A scoped member is refused `brands.manage`, `onboard`, `team.invite.*` and `team.access` whatever the role (`SCOPED_BLOCKED`). No key is built from a request id: the brand id is still only a selector among the account's own active brands.
5. Approval (rules 3760-3762): `approve` is admin level; an Editor or Viewer approves only while their `approves` toggle is on (set with `team.access`). PATCH /api/tasks/{id} ending `approved` needs `approve` and records `approvedBy: {pid, at}` (rule 3761). The owner's policy `approvalRequired` (PATCH /api/team, `team.policy`, owner only): while on, `publishOnce` refuses under the publish lock (409 `approval_required`) unless the copy being sent was approved by a person who may still approve that brand (`mayApprove`: the owner, an admin or a toggled member whose scope holds the brand, membership not paused). The model is never the approval.
6. Assignment (rule 3764): a fresh pending draft gets `assignee: {pid, at}`, one approver of its brand in turn (`approversFor`, counter `assignturn:{scope}`), and one bell entry naming them. Best effort: a failed read leaves it unassigned and never stops the save. An account with no team is left alone.
7. Per-member monthly credit cap (rules 3770-3771): `creditCap` on a membership, set by the owner only (`team.cap`), off by default; a malformed value reads as 0. `debit` checks it under `withUserLock` before the spend, from `User.personSpend` (`{month, by: {pid: credits}}`), and refuses with `person_cap` (shown as no credits, with what is left of the cap). Every metered spender passes the actor (`personOf(u)`) and a refund lowers the counter. Chat messages use the chat allowance, not credits, and are not capped.
8. Team log (rule 3763): a change of brand scope, toggle or cap writes `access_changed`. A build before this one drops such a row on read (an unknown kind fails the row).
9. Scope outside the resolvers (rules 3772-3773): a calendar link stops serving a brand removed from its member's scope (`feedHolds`, both link shapes); the bell (`GET`/`POST /api/notifications`, the app layout, the notifications and chat pages) hides another brand's entries from a narrowed member, and marking one read is 404; `monthUsage` and `teamApprovals` count the allowed brands only.
10. Known limits of this release: chat overflow (`settleChatOverflow`) is not under the per-member cap; and the scope, toggle and cap live only on the `mbr:` row, so a rollback to a build before this one, after members are scoped or capped, widens their access to every brand and removes their cap.

## 1. API routes (128 route files, 191 exported handlers on 2026-10-10; eleven of the files and fourteen of the handlers are agent instructions: ten files and eleven handlers under `/api/instructions`, rows 190-200, and `/api/admin/skills`, rows 201-203)

"Before the body is read" in these rows means before the route reads it. The proxy matches every path (rule 674), so Next has already buffered each non-GET body, at most 6 MB (`proxyClientMaxBodySize`, rule 774), before any route's check runs; a route's own body cap bounds what the route keeps and parses.

Reem added 2 files and 3 handlers (rows 101-103) to main's 74 and 113, so main with teams and Reem counted 76 and 116. Step 3 added 3 files and 3 exports: `governance/rollback` (part H, row 104) and `activity` and `activity/export` (part L, rows 105-106); its own heading said 72 and 109, and 77 and 116 once it was merged with the teams change. Before those merges this heading said 74 and 113, counted after main's MCP `save_draft` and updates and wishlist changes were merged into the teams change; before that, 71 and 109, counted after main's media change was merged into the teams change. On main, after main was merged into the media change, the count was 66 and 102: before the media change the tree had 59 files and 95 handlers while this heading said 57 and 90; the media change added 5 files and 5 handlers (rows 92-96), and main added 2 of each the same day (`onboard/confirm`, row 33a, and `cron/routines`, row 91; main's heading said 60 and 91). The teams change added 5 files and 7 handlers (rows 7a-7g; its own heading said 66 and 104 before main's media change was merged in). The updates and wishlist change added 3 files and 4 exports (rows 97-100; main's heading said 69 and 106); MCP `save_draft` added none (it is a tool inside `mcp/route.ts`, row 53). Rows 56d and 56e (`mcp/grants`) were missing and are added below.

Legend for "Who": A = anonymous, U = signed-in login (the owner or a member), O = a login of the account that owns the addressed resource (U plus scope binding), F = founder admin, C = cron secret, M = MCP bearer token.

Rate limits (since 2026-10-08, rule 680): every 429 in the rows below carries `Retry-After` in whole seconds, whether or not the row says so. A refusal by an `allow()` bucket answers through `retryLater` (`src/lib/rate.ts`) with the window that bucket was given (`allow(key, max, 3600)` waits 3600; with several buckets, the one that refused). Reem's day of new items sends 86400; a media start sends its own hour or day, kie.ai's shared window, or 60 (`BUSY_RETRY_S`) when the refusal is a full Higgsfield queue, a render still open there or a held lock. The body is the route's own and unchanged. `tests/retry-after.test.ts` fails on a route that builds a 429 without the header.

Roles (since 2026-09-29): a row names the `src/lib/roles.ts` action its handler checks with `forbid` or `can`, and that action's minimum role: owner; admin+ (admin or owner); member (every login of the account). A U or O row that names no role accepts every login of the account, members included. `tests/role-gates.test.ts` holds the same map (`ROUTE_ROLES`, `GATED_GETS`) and fails when a POST, PUT, PATCH or DELETE handler is missing from it or does not check what it declares. Since 2026-10-08 the map has a class `unverified-session` for the three `auth/verify/*` routes (rows 1a-1c): the caller is an owner `currentUser()` reads as signed out, so the handler must contain `currentUserUnverified(` and a rate limit (`allow`, or `verifySendRefusal`, which wraps the send buckets), and nothing else may call it (rule 752).

### 1.1 Auth and account

| # | Method | Path | Who | Enforced by |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | POST | `/api/auth/signup` | A | Rate limit 5 per IP per hour (`signup:ip:{ip}`); `validEmail` and `strongPassword`; Cloudflare Turnstile when both keys are set (`captchaRefusal`, `src/lib/turnstile.ts`, before the lookup: 400 `captcha_failed`, 503 `captcha_unavailable`; rule 426); with exactly one key set, a production deploy (staging included) refuses every signup with 503 `captcha_unavailable` and logs the missing name once (rule 978). Then a typed `partnerCode` must be a partner's (400 `bad_partner_code`, nothing created; checked after Turnstile so codes cannot be probed without it, rule 419; 2026-10-01, rules 392 and 419). `getLogin` and `createUser` run inside `withLoginLock(email)` (`lock:login:{email}`, the lock invite acceptance also takes), so an address that is already an owner account or a member login gets the same 409 `exists`. Since 2026-10-08 the new account is created gated (`gatedSignup`, no `emailVerifiedAt`), the session is opened, the first code is mailed (counted in the `verify:gap:` and `verify:hour:` buckets) and the answer carries `verify: true` and `mail: boolean`; a mail that cannot go out never fails the signup (rule 762). |
| 2 | POST | `/api/auth/login` | A | Rate limits 20 per IP and 8 per login per 15 min. `getLogin(email)`; bcrypt against that login's OWN hash (the owner record or the member record), else 401 `invalid`. Owner: 403 `suspended`. Member: 401 `invalid` when the owner record is missing, `memberOf` does not name it, or the member was accepted before the owner record was created; 403 `suspended` when the owner is suspended; 403 `owner_unverified` and no session when the owner has no `emailVerifiedAt` (rule 767); 403 `disabled` when the member is seat-paused. The session is issued for the login email; `track("login")` counts the owner email. An owner without `emailVerifiedAt` still gets the session and the answer is `{ok: true, verify: true}` (`verify: false` once verified): `LoginForm` then goes to `/{lang}/app/verify?next=` with the `safeNext`'d destination, so a `next` outside the app (the OAuth consent page) cannot loop back to login (rule 754). |
| 3 | POST | `/api/auth/logout` | A | No check; `destroySession()` acts on the caller's own cookie `auth/logout/route.ts:5`. |
| 4 | POST | `/api/auth/forgot` | A | rate limit 8 per IP per 15 min first, then Cloudflare Turnstile when both keys are set (rule 427; half-configured in production: 503 `captcha_unavailable`, rule 978), then 3 per email per 15 min (`allow`, `src/lib/rate.ts`); over the limit it still answers `{ok:true, mail}`. `getLogin`: the `pwreset:{hash}` record is `{email, kind}` with `kind` `owner` or `member`; a member whose owner record is gone gets no link, with the same answer as an unknown address. The emailed link is `/{lang}/app/reset#t=<token>`: the token rides in the URL fragment, never a query string (rule 546). Since 2026-10-08 the email also has an HTML part (`src/emails/reset-password.ts`) in the form's language whose button and fallback link carry the same fragment, so Resend click tracking must stay off for the sending domain (a dashboard setting, not checked in code). |
| 5 | POST | `/api/auth/reset` | A holding a valid reset token | Rate limit 10 per IP per 15 min; token lookup; single use under `pwresetuse:{hash}` (SET NX, 1 h) before the record is deleted, so two requests racing on one link cannot both pass. The login must still be of the kind the record names (a record without `kind` is an owner's), else 400 `bad_token`; a suspended owner gets 403. A member reset writes the member record only (`withMemberLock`); a seat-paused member gets the new password but 403 `disabled` and no session, and so does a member of an owner who has not verified (403 `owner_unverified`, rule 767). An owner reset saves the hash, revokes the sessions and stamps `emailVerifiedAt` when unset, all inside one `withUserLock` (rule 769: the single-use link was mailed to that address); a member reset never stamps the owner. Rule 2992 (2026-10-22): an owner reset stamps `teamSessionsAfter` and revokes MCP tokens and grants, linked calendars, and the feed and booking links of the owner and roster in EVERY account the person owns (`ownedBy`), not only the one `getLogin` picked; a member reset also revokes the MCP tokens, grants and calendars of any account the person owns. |
| 5a | POST | `/api/auth/email-revert` | A holding a valid revert token | Account-id R2 (rule 2744, flows 88.2), answers only while `OPAQUE_IDS` is on. Rate limit 10 per IP per 15 min (`rate:emailrevert:ip:{ip}`) first; the token (mailed to the old address after a proved move, 7 days; the reset page posts it only from a confirm button, never on load) is read, then used up with GETDEL only under the locks once every check passed (put back on a failure after that), and one of a move made after the person's last revert is refused; a bad, used or expired token is 400 `bad_token`, a held lock 409 `busy` with the token kept. The person moves back in place under the same locks as the move; every session ends, the password becomes unknown, every passkey, two-step enrolled after the move or within 7 days before it, the Google link and the owned accounts' MCP tokens, feed, booking and calendar links go; the answer is a one-hour reset token for the old address. A partner's address or a taken one is 409. |
| 1a | POST | `/api/auth/verify/send` | U, owner who has not verified (`currentUserUnverified`); 401 without a session or for a member of an unverified owner (rule 767), 409 `already_verified` for a member of a verified owner or a verified owner | Mails a fresh 6-digit code to the SESSION's address (never one from the request; no body is read). 503 `mail_unavailable` when `mailReady()` is false (a test deploy skips this refusal, rule 766); then `allow` buckets in order, the refusing one names the wait with `Retry-After`: `verify:gap:{email}` 1 per 60 s, `verify:hour:{email}` 5 per hour, `verify:day:{email}` 10 per day (rule 768, `Retry-After: 86400`), `verify:ip:{ip}` 20 per hour (429 `slow_down`). 502 `send_failed` when the provider refuses the mail (the code is dropped). The code is never in a response except `devCode` when `APP_ENV=test` off a production deploy. Rules 757, 759, 760, 766, 768. |
| 1b | POST | `/api/auth/verify/check` | U, owner who has not verified (`currentUserUnverified`); 401 without a session or for a member of an unverified owner; a member of a verified owner or a verified owner answers `{ok:true}` | In order: the 401 and the `{ok:true}` shortcut for a member or a verified owner, then `verifycheck:ip:{ip}` 60 per hour (429 `slow_down`). Reads `{code}`; Arabic-Indic and Persian digits are normalised, and input that is not 6 digits is 400 `wrong_code` with no attempt used. `checkVerifyCode(session email, code)`: the attempt is taken with `INCRBY verify:tries:{email}` before the comparison (`sameSecret` on the hash), 5 wrong answers delete the code, 400 `wrong_code` with `attemptsLeft`, `expired` or `locked`. A correct code runs `markVerified` under the account lock. Rules 757, 758, 761. |
| 1c | POST | `/api/auth/verify/email` | U, owner of a gated signup who has not verified (`currentUserUnverified`, then `actor.kind === "owner"` and `gatedSignup`); 401 without a session or for a member of an unverified owner, 403 `not_allowed` otherwise (a member session, a founder impersonating, an older account, a verified owner) | Moves the session's own account to a new address, the only value taken from the body. `verifymove:ip:{ip}` 20 per hour and `verifymove:acct:{session email}` 10 per day (every attempt, taken or not), 3 moves per account per UTC day (`emailMoves`); 400 `bad_email` and `same_email`, 409 `exists` for any owner or member login at the target, 429 `slow_down` with `Retry-After`. `moveUnverifiedAccount` under both login locks, both person locks (old and new pid, the smaller first; a timeout is 409 `busy`, rule 2414) and the old account's user lock, the passkeys moved to the new pid (rule 2424), then `wipeAccount(old)`, a new session for the new address and a fresh code through the send refusals; it answers 200 `{ok, email, mail?}` even when that send is refused. Rules 763-765. |
| 6 | PATCH | `/api/account` | U, member (`password.change`, refused to a founder impersonating), plus the acting login's current password | `forbid(u, "password.change")`; the current password is checked against the ACTING login's own hash, never the account's for a member. A member rewrites only its member record under `withMemberLock` and stamps its own `sessionsAfter`; the owner's hash, sessions, MCP token and grants are untouched. The owner path is unchanged: user lock, `revokeSessions`, `revokeAllMcp`, now of every account the person owns (`ownedBy`, rule 2992), and so for a member who owns workspaces. Wrong current passwords count in `rate:pwchange:{login}` (10 per hour, `allow` before the compare, `giveBack` after a right one, 429 `slow_down`, rule 2991). The device is signed in again as the same login. |
| 7 | DELETE | `/api/account` | U, owner (`account.delete`, refused to members and to a founder impersonating), plus the owner's password | `forbid(u, "account.delete")`, then the owner's own hash behind `rate:acctdelete:{login}` (10 wrong passwords per hour, 429 `slow_down`, rule 2991). Wipes the caller's own account only, its member logins and invites included (`wipeAccount` calls `wipeMemberships`). Conversations are deleted first, each under its lock `convlock:{sc}:{id}` built from the account's own index (rule 905): a conversation whose lock is not taken in time stops the wipe (500, nothing deleted yet beyond the links, a retry runs it again). |

### 1.1a Team (2026-09-29)

Route files under `team/`; `team/respond.ts` is a shared helper (the HTTP status of each `src/lib/team.ts` failure), not a route. Signed out, every route but `accept` answers 401 `{error:"unauthorized"}`.

| # | Method | Path | Who | Enforced by |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 7a | GET | `/api/team` | U, member (`team.view`) | `forbid(u, "team.view")`; the body is `teamView(u)`: the acting person, seats used and limit, the roster with the owner first (never a hash), and pending invites only when `can(u, "team.invites.view")` (admin+), else `[]`. Since 2026-10-12 (rule 1480) also `approvals` when `can(u, "team.approvals.view")` (admin+): each person's approvals, rejections, edits and approval rate over 30 days from the account's own `decisions:{scope}` lists (`teamApprovals(u.email)`, every brand of the owner); absent for a plain member. |
| 7b | POST | `/api/team/invite` | U, admin+ (`team.invite.member`); inviting an admin needs owner (`team.invite.admin`); refused to a founder impersonating | `forbid(u, "team.invite.member")`; rate limits `team:invite:acct:{owner}` 20 per day (5 on a trial), `team:invite:ip:{ip}` 30 per hour and `team:invite:to:{sha256(address)}` 5 per day across all accounts, counted on every attempt; then `createInvite`: 400 `invalid_role` or `invalid_email`; 403 when the role needs the owner or the acting member no longer holds its role; one 409 `unavailable` for the owner's or the inviter's own address, an existing owner or member login anywhere, and a pending invite in this team; 409 `{error:"seats", used, limit, included, extra, unitSar, canBuy}`. Paid seats (2026-10-23, rules 3366-3369, branch `claude/sup-paid-seats`, flows.md section 121): over the limit the invite is refused unless the body carries `addSeat: true` (only the boolean) AND `billing.seats` passes (the owner's own session, never an admin, a founder impersonating or a token) AND the plan sells seats (starter, pro, business; not a trial, not Agency) AND billing is not locked AND exactly one seat is missing; then `extraSeats` rises by one under `lock:team:{aid}` then `lock:user` and the answer carries `seatAdded: true`. 409 `busy` when the user lock is not had in time. The existence and seat checks run under `lock:team:{owner}`. With mail set up the link goes only to the invited address, from a fixed template naming the inviting login's address (no display or brand name); a failed send withdraws the invite and answers 502 `{error:"send_failed"}` with no link. Only with mail not set up is `link` returned to the inviter. The bell notice names neither the invitee nor the role. Yearly seats (2026-10-24, rules 4040-4050, flows.md section 121 item 5): on an active yearly subscription the 409 also carries `charge` and the seat is charged on the saved card only when the body also carries `seatHalalas` equal to that total; answers 402 `seat_declined`, 502 `seat_failed`, 409 `seat_busy` / `seat_pending` / `seat_changed`, `refunded: true` on an answer whose paid seat was refunded; without a saved-card charge `canBuy` is false with `blocked` (`no_card`, `no_saved_charge`). |
| 7c | DELETE | `/api/team/invite` | U, admin+ (`team.invite.cancel`), member invites only for an admin; refused to a founder impersonating | `cancelInvite`: the id must be 64 hex characters naming an invite of this account (else 404 `not_found`); an admin invite needs `team.invite.admin`; the acting member must still hold its role; the delete runs under `lock:team:{owner}` after reading the invite again (404 when an accept used it first). |
| 7d | POST | `/api/team/accept` | A holding an invite token, in the JSON body, never a URL | `{token}` alone: a preview, rate limit `team:peek:ip:{ip}` 30 per 15 min, consumes nothing; 400 `invalid` for an unknown, expired or orphaned invite, a suspended owner, or an inviter who is no longer an enabled admin of the team. With `name` and `password`: rate limit `team:accept:ip:{ip}` 10 per 15 min, then `acceptInvite` (single use under `inviteuse:{hash}`; the invite, its index entry, the owner, the inviter and the seat limit read again under `lock:team:{owner}`, the address under `lock:login:{email}`, `flows.md` 26); on success the device's session is destroyed and one is issued for the new member. Never creates an owner account. With `MULTI_ORG` on (M2, rule 2116): `{token, join: true}` is the signed-in person's accept, checked before the preview: `currentPerson()` (401 signed out or impersonating), rate limit `team:accept:ip:{ip}`, then `joinInviteAsPerson` under `inviteuse` → `lock:team` → `lock:person`: the person must be verified, not suspended, with the invite's address, not already in the account, and a seat free; the session and `lastorg:` move to the account and the answer is its link (handle only). The password way answers 409 `signin_required` for an address that already signs in; an address that already holds a membership here gets 409 `already_member`. |
| 7e | PATCH | `/api/team/member` | U: `role` owner (`team.role`); `brands`, `approves` owner or an unscoped admin (`team.access`); `creditCap` owner (`team.cap`); each refused to a founder impersonating | One `forbid` per field present (none present: 400 `invalid`). `setRole`: `admin`, `editor` (stored `member`) or `viewer` (400 `invalid_role`), the member must belong to this account (404), the seat count checked (400 `seats_full`), written under `lock:team:{owner}` and `lock:member:{email}`. `setAccess` (rules 3756, 3760, 3770): brand ids of the account's own active brands only (400 `invalid`), `approves` boolean, `creditCap` a whole number 0-10,000,000 or null (400 `invalid`); an admin's own access only by the owner; re-checked under the lock; team log `access_changed`. |
| 7f | DELETE | `/api/team/member` | U, admin+ (`team.remove.member`); removing an admin needs owner (`team.remove.admin`); refused to a founder impersonating | `removeMember`: the member must belong to this account (404); the acting member must still hold its role; deletes the login, its `activebrand:`, its roster entry and the invites it sent, under `lock:team:{owner}` and then `lock:member:{email}` (the lock every member-record writer holds), so a write in flight cannot restore the login. |
| 7g | POST | `/api/team/leave` | U, a member login (`team.leave` is false for the owner, a token and a founder) | `forbid(u, "team.leave")`; `leaveTeam` deletes the login as a removal does, then `destroySession()`. |
| 7h | GET | `/api/account/export` | U, owner (`account.export`, refused to members and to a founder impersonating) | `currentUser` first (401); `forbid(u, "account.export")` (403); `allow("accountexport:h:{email}", 3, 3600)` and `allow("accountexport:d:{email}", 10, 86400)` (429 `slow_down`, `Retry-After`); then `buildExport(u)` (`src/lib/privacy-export.ts`): every key from the session owner's email and its own brand scopes, nothing from the request; no password hash, session stamp, token, `enc` blob or card token in the file (rules 1320-1324; `GET /api/account/export` in `flows.md` 65). |
| 7i | GET | `/api/workspaces` | U, a signed-in person, not an impersonation (`currentPerson()`; 401 otherwise); 404 on a build with `MULTI_ORG` off, the rollback floor (on since the enable release, rule 3810; M2) | `listWorkspaces(person, acct)`: the person's held accounts (`heldAccounts`, every one re-checked on its forward records), each as `{handle, name, role, current}`, never an account id or an address. |
| 7j | POST | `/api/workspaces` | U, a verified person, not an impersonation; 404 on a build with `MULTI_ORG` off, the rollback floor (on since the enable release, rule 3810; M2, rule 2123) | Body `{name (1-80), lang}` (400 `invalid_name`); 403 `email_unverified`; rate limit `wscreate:{pid}` 3 per day (429 with `Retry-After`); under `lock:login` → `lock:person` the person owns fewer than `ownedCap ?? 3` accounts (409 `workspace_cap`); the new `a_` account names the person in `ownerPid`, the trial only when `trialUsedAt` is unset and the person owns nothing; the session `acct` and `lastorg:` move to it; the answer is its handle and link. |
| 7k | POST | `/api/workspaces/switch` | U, a signed-in person, not an impersonation; 404 on a build with `MULTI_ORG` off, the rollback floor (on since the enable release, rule 3810; M2, rule 2112) | Body `{handle}`: `switchWorkspace` matches it among the person's held accounts only (a foreign, stale, made-up or malformed handle is the same 404 `not_found`, nothing written), then `SET XX` of the session's `acct` (TTL kept) and `lastorg:{pid}`; never the brand choice. |
| 7l | GET | `/api/workspaces/invites` | U, a signed-in person, not an impersonation (`currentPerson()`; 401 otherwise); 404 on a build with `MULTI_ORG` off, the rollback floor (on since the enable release, rule 3810; M3, rule 2753) | `invitesWaiting(person)` reads `invitesfor:{email}` of the person's own verified address only; answers workspace name, inviter address, role and expiry, never an invite id, a token or an account id; dead entries are dropped at read. |
| 7m | GET | `/api/team/log` | O or admin (`team.log.view`); an impersonation may view; 404 on a build with `MULTI_ORG` off, the rollback floor (on since the enable release, rule 3810; M3, rule 2129 read half) | `teamLogView(aidOf(u))`: the session's own account's `teamlog:` rows with addresses only, never a person or account id. |
| 7n | POST, DELETE | `/api/team/transfer` | O only, the owner's own session (`team.transfer`, actor kind `owner`; never an admin, member, token or impersonation); 404 on a build with `MULTI_ORG` off, the rollback floor (on since the enable release, rule 3810; M3, rules 2128, 2750) | POST body `{email, password, code?}` (the mail goes in the receiving person's own `lang`): rate `rate:xfer:offer:{pid}` 5 a day; mail must be set up (409 `mail_unavailable`); `freshProof` checks the password against the PERSON's hash (wrong-password bucket `rate:mfapw:{login}`) and, with two-step on, a fresh authenticator code (a recovery code is refused); `offerTransfer` only to an enabled admin on the account's roster whose `person:` is verified, never for a legacy-shape account or person or a partner account; stores only the token's SHA-256 in `xfer:{aid}` (7 days) and mails the token in the link's fragment; the link's handle is made before the offer is written (503 `not_configured` without CONNECT_SECRET, nothing written), and a failed send or any throw while building the mail withdraws it (502). DELETE withdraws the account's own offer. |
| 7o | POST | `/api/workspaces/transfer` | U, a signed-in person, not an impersonation (`currentPerson()`; 401 otherwise); 404 on a build with `MULTI_ORG` off, the rollback floor (on since the enable release, rule 3810; M3, rules 2128, 2751, 2752) | Body `{handle, token}`: rate `rate:xfer:accept:{pid}` 10 an hour; the handle is matched only among the accounts the person holds; the token's hash is compared with `sameSecret`; under `lock:team` → `lock:login` → `lock:person` → `lock:user` the offer is re-read and must still name this person, the same owner and a still-enabled admin (else it is voided); then single use: ownership, contact, card and renewals, MCP, the old owner an admin; the device switches to the workspace. |
| 7p | DELETE | `/api/workspaces/person` | U, a signed-in person, not an impersonation (`currentPerson()`; 401 otherwise); 404 on a build with `MULTI_ORG` off, the rollback floor (on since the enable release, rule 3810; M3, rules 2127, 2754) | Body `{password, code?}`: rate `rate:persondel:{pid}` 5 an hour; a `p_` person from its own `person:` row only (409 `delete_unavailable` otherwise); `freshProof` (the person's password, and with two-step on a code; a recovery code is accepted); 409 `owns_workspace` while it owns any account. Then `deletePerson`: every membership, paused ones included, under its own team lock, then the person's keys under `lock:login` → `lock:person`; this device is signed out. Names no workspace and reads no id from the request. |
| 7q | GET | `/api/usage` | O or admin (`usage.view`); an impersonation may view; members 403, signed out 401 (rule 2907) | `handler` with rate `usageview` (300 an hour) and `?month` checked by `isMonth` (400 `bad_input`). `ownerUsage(u, month)` reads only `acct(u)`'s month: requests and share per person, brand, agent, model and day. No dollar figure and no other account; the month is the only input. The page `/{lang}/app/settings/usage` makes the same check (`can(u, "usage.view")`, else 404) and renders the same body. |
| 7r | PATCH | `/api/team` | O only (`team.policy`, refused to a founder impersonating, rule 3762) | Body `{approvalRequired: boolean}` (400 `invalid`); written on `acct(u)` under `withUserLock` (409 on a lock timeout); `publishOnce` enforces it (section 0.1a item 5). |

### 1.2 Workspace data (all scope-bound through `sc(u)` unless noted)

| # | Method | Path | Who | Enforced by |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 8 | GET | `/api/brand` | O | `currentUser` `brand/route.ts:9-10`; `getBrand(sc(u))` `:11`. |
| 9a | POST | `/api/brand/proposals/{id}` | O, admin+ (`brand.write`) | `currentUser`; `forbid(u, "brand.write")`; `{action: "apply" \| "dismiss"}` else 400; `decideProposal(sc(u), id, action)` (`src/lib/brand-proposals.ts`): the proposal is read from `brandprop:{sc(u)}:{id}` only (another account's id is 404). Each answer takes `brandprop-claim:{scope}:{id}` with `setNx` (60 s) and releases it afterwards; an answer that arrives while another holds it is 409 `busy`. `dismiss` deletes the proposal and records `dismissed` in `brandprop-done:{scope}:{id}` (7 days). `apply` runs under `brandlock:{scope}`: any field other than `banned` applies only while it still equals `from` (since 2026-10-05 every editable field can be proposed, rule 568), a banned-word removal or addition applies to the list as it is now; each is 409 `stale` otherwise (an addition whose words are all there already), and an addition that would pass the 400 characters is 409 `too_long` and stays open (nothing written, the proposal deleted, `stale` recorded). A missing brand file is 409 `no_brand` and the proposal stays open. A decided or expired proposal is 404. Added 2026-09-29. Since step 3a was merged with it (2026-09-29), an applied proposal is one `brand_changed` activity row under `sc(u)` with `by` from the session (`byOf(u)`), the proposing agent and the field's name (never its value); a dismissal writes none. |

Since 2026-09-29 `timeZone` is stored only when it is one of the six zones in `src/lib/zones.ts` (`isZone`, `:43`); anything else is dropped, and every reader falls back through `zoneOf`. ### 1.3 Agents, content, tasks

| # | Method | Path | Who | Enforced by |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 29 | GET | `/api/chat` | O; `conv` from query is a key suffix under `sc(u)` | `currentUser` `chat/route.ts:13-14`; `history(sc(u), conv)` `:16`, with the account's `chatStatus(u, model)` beside it (2026-10-01); since 2026-10-08 `model` is the conversation's routed model (`getConversation(sc(u), conv)`, then `chatRouteFor`), so the hold shown is that model's. |
| 30 | POST | `/api/chat` | O | `currentUser` `chat/route.ts:28-29`. Before anything is saved: 400 `empty`; an agent the plan does not include (`entitlements(u).agents`, rule 782) is 403 `agent_not_included`, ahead of every gate and count below (every plan includes all eight agents today, so nothing triggers it yet); an ended trial (`trialOver(u)`, since 2026-10-01, rule 330) is 402 `trial_over` with `limit:"trial"` and a message linking the Subscription page, checked first so it uses up neither a ceiling nor a message of the trial's allowance (`chat()` refuses an ended trial too, for any caller, writing nothing); the per-account burst limit `chatRefusal(u)` (`chatCaps(u)`: 30 an hour and 150 a day on a paid plan, 10 an hour and 30 a day on a trial, rule 331; 429 `slow_down` with `retry` "hour" or "day" and a message naming the account's own ceiling); since 2026-10-10, read in the same round and answered before it, the agent-scope ceiling of refused messages (`agentScopePreflight(u)`, rule 795: only when the input gate is in enforce, 20 enforced input refusals an hour and 60 a day per brand scope `sc(u)`, counted in `rate:agent-scope:h|d:{scope}`; 429 `slow_down` with `retry` and `Retry-After`, no classifier call; in enforce an out-of-scope message is answered by the refusal inside `chat()`, with no model call, and the message is given back to the allowance, rule 794); since 2026-10-01 the account's monthly chat allowance `reserveChatTurn` (`src/lib/chat-allowance.ts`; 402 `chat_allowance` while chat is paused, 402 `chat_credits` when the owner's "continue on credits" is on but the balance is below `chatOverflowHold()` (5 credits), both with `chat` and `credits`, nothing saved, sent or charged), counted on the ACCOUNT's counter whoever is acting, and settled after the turn on every path (a failed or uncosted turn is given back and not billed; an over-allowance one is billed after the reply its metered cost at the floor rate, at most 5 credits and at most what the balance still holds, by `settleChatOverflow` under the user lock; nothing is taken before the turn). The turn's model calls are bounded in code by the $0.35 per-message ceiling and the history guard (`src/lib/chat-budget.ts`: `trimHistory`, then `fitFirstCall`) and by the request's deadline (rule 336); a call ended by `max_tokens` is never acted on, so its possibly cut-off tool calls do not run. The reply carries `chat` and `charged`, never the turn's model cost (`usd`). Tool side effects run under `user.scope` `src/lib/chat.ts:568`. The chat tools `update_brand`, `publish_item` and `import_catalog` are refused inside `runTool` unless `can(user, TOOL_ACTION[name])` (`brand.write`, `publish`, `catalog.import`, all admin+), and `write_file` to a core file unless `can(user, "files.core.write")`, with a sentence for the model to relay and nothing written; a user with no actor is refused too. Reem's five tools (`agenda_get`, `agenda_next` since 2026-09-30, `todo_create`, `todo_update`, `todo_propose_done`, offered to every agent) are free and cannot complete, delete, restore or reopen an item, change an item's inbox link, write a private note or touch a contact's phone number: `changeItem` and `mutateItem` refuse those unless the actor is the owner on the web (`owner_only`; a note from chat is stored shared). Their `scope` and `email` arguments are ignored (`sc(user)`); they are member-level (`todo.write` is what the route needs too), and the audit line records the chatting login (`login`). The agents' handoff notes (`handoff_note`, since 2026-09-30, Reem section item 18) are a different tool: offered to every agent, refused over MCP, the sender is the turn's agent (section 3.2). Since 2026-09-30 `propose_handoff` (Majed's handoffs, section "Handoffs") is offered only in Majed's turns and `runTool` refuses it to any other agent, a turn with no agent, a token or a turn over MCP (`ctx.via`), and a second proposal in one reply; it is free, needs no action beyond chat (a member may ask Majed for a plan) and writes only `handoff:{sc(u)}:{id}` and its index, with the agent, cost, status and `requestedBy` set in code; the proposal is marked shown (`shownAt`, `markShown`) only after the reply carrying its card is saved in the thread, so a turn that fails after the tool never leaves a proposal Majed's auto-run could start without the owner seeing it (`flows.md` 35). Majed's `generate_content` and `draft_items` for a type he does not own are refused in code (`roleRefusalText("majed", type)`, rule 798), in every mode of the founder's role layer, with nothing charged; for the other agents a type that is not their own follows that layer's mode (`off` runs it, `shadow` runs it and logs `wrong_agent`, `enforce` refuses it with a `switch:` card, nothing created or charged), in code and not in the prompt; and since 2026-10-10 (rule 799) `runTool` answers `Unknown tool.` first, with no other check or write, to a turn with an agent for a tool that agent was not offered (`toolsFor(agent)`). ### 1.4 Connectors and publishing

| # | Method | Path | Who | Enforced by |
|---|---|---|---|---|

| 44 | POST | `/api/connect/{provider}` | O; per action (`ACTION_ROLE`): `select_page`, `select_org`, `post_as`, `select_ad_account` and `select_property` admin+ (`connectors`), `import_products` admin+ (`catalog.import`), `sync_sales`, `sync_ads`, `sync_site` and `sync_social` member (`read`), `sync_x` member (`generate`: it spends 1 credit of the account); any other action 400 `bad_action` before the connection lookup | `currentUser`; `forbid(u, ACTION_ROLE[action])`; `getFreshConnection(sc(u), provider)`. Actions: `import_products` (Shopify, WooCommerce, Odoo, PrestaShop, Salla, Zid), `sync_sales` (Salla, Shopify, WooCommerce, Zid), `select_page` (Meta; the Page id must be one Meta returned for this connection; on a Composio Meta record the new Page is read at once with `readSocialSoon(sc(u))` only when `allow("socialsync:{scope}", 6, 3600)` grants it, the `sync_social` bucket, and the answer is `{ ok: true, account, read }`, `read` false past the hour's six; a direct Meta record answers `{ ok: true, account }`, rule 739). `select_org` (LinkedIn through Composio; the URN must be one listed at connect time in `meta.liOrgs`, 400 `unknown_org` otherwise; resets `liPostAs` to `me`; since 2026-10-09 the new page is read at once only when `allow("socialsync:{scope}", 6, 3600)` grants it, the `sync_social` bucket, and the answer is `{ ok: true, account, read }`, `read` false past the hour's six, rules 671, 735). `post_as` (LinkedIn through Composio; `value` `me` or `org`, 400 `bad_value` otherwise; `org` needs a chosen page, 400 `no_page`; one `connector_changed` activity row per change). `select_property` (Google through Composio only, rule 734; a direct Google record is 400 `bad_action`, and on another provider's path the action is 400 `bad_action` only when that provider has a connection: without one, 404 `not_connected` comes first; `kind` `ga4` or `gsc`, 400 `bad_value` otherwise; `id` must be one listed at connect time, a property in `meta.gaProperties` or a site in `meta.gscSites`, 400 `unknown_property` otherwise; writes `gaProperty` and `gaName` (`clean(…, 80)`) or `gscSite` and the rebuilt `account` under `sc(u)`, always; reads the site again at once with `readSiteSoon(sc(u))` only when `allow("sitesync:{scope}", 6, 3600)` grants it, the `sync_site` bucket; answers `{ ok: true, account, read }`, `read` false past the hour's six). For every ad provider (`AD_PROVIDERS`: `meta_ads`, `snapchat`, `google_ads`, `tiktok_ads`, `linkedin_ads`): `sync_ads` (read only, results saved under `sc(u)`; since 2026-10-09 six an hour per workspace, every ad platform together, `allow("adrefresh:{scope}", 6, 3600)`, the seventh 429 `slow_down` with `Retry-After: 3600` before any call to the platform, rule 735) and `select_ad_account` (only an id the platform listed). `sync_site` (Google only; the record must be a Composio one holding GA4 or Search Console, `holdsSite`, else 404 `not_connected`) and `sync_social` (Meta, LinkedIn, YouTube, TikTok; the path's record must hold a part the readers read, `holdsSocial`, else 404 `not_connected`; on any other path either is 400 `bad_action`), rules 735 and 736: free (no credit held or taken), six an hour per workspace each (`allow("sitesync:{scope}")`, `allow("socialsync:{scope}")`, scope from `sc(u)`, checked after the 404s so a refusal spends none), the seventh 429 `slow_down` with `Retry-After: 3600`; a forced read of the workspace's held parts under `sc(u)` beside `markOwnerSync(sc(u))` (`ownersync:{scope}`, Dana's gate), answering `{ ok: true, read }`, or for `sync_social` 200 `{ ok: true, read: true, reconnect }` when the snapshot was saved and a part's token was refused (every refused part's record marked); a refused token (the adapter's 401 only, never a 403 quota or permission answer, which is 502 with its message and marks nothing) is converted inside the readers with each refused part's provider, never by the route's `refusedToken`, which never sees the read's error (flows section 50 step 9). `sync_x` (X only; on any other path 400 `bad_action`; rules 744, 745, flows section 50 step 17): six clicks an hour per workspace (`allow("xsync:{scope}", 6, 3600)`, rule 735), the seventh 429 `slow_down` with `Retry-After: 3600` before any charge or call to X; spends 1 credit of the account, charged to `u.email` (the account record, also for a member) in code before X is asked, by `refreshX` (`src/lib/x-stats.ts`) with `chargeOnce` under Dana's monthly cap and the claim `xread:{scope}:{UTC day}` (scope from `sc(u)`), so at most one paid read per workspace per UTC day and two clicks at once debit one credit; a read already dated today answers `{ ok: true, x, charged: false }` with no charge and no call to X; a Composio-routed X record 409 `x_direct_only` before any charge; 402 `trial_over`, 402 `agent_cap` with `spent` and `cap`, 402 `no_credits` with `need` and `credits`, none of them calling X; a read answers `{ ok: true, x, charged }`, `x` without its account id (`xForPage`, rule 746). Every failure after the charge refunds once, in the click that took it, unless a read dated today exists by then (a refund that fails is logged `x_refund_failed` and the click still answers X's failure, so X's 401 still asks for a reconnect); a click that read on that hold never refunds and, once its read is saved, charges the same claim again (a no-op while the hold stands), so one credit stands for the day's read. X's 402 and 403 answer 502 `x_unavailable` and X's 429 answers 429 `x_unavailable` with X's own `Retry-After` (900 s when X gives none), none of them marking the record; only X's 401 reaches the route's `refusedToken` and asks the owner to reconnect (502). Since 2026-10-19 (rule 2243, branch only) `sync_sales` is limited to six an hour per workspace in `rate:storesync:{sc(u)}` and `import_products` to six an hour in `rate:catalogimport:{sc(u)}`, every store together, taken after the role and connection checks and before the store is read; over either, 429 `slow_down` with `Retry-After`. |
| 45 | GET | `/api/connect/{provider}/start` | O, admin+ (`connectors`) | `currentUser`; `forbid(u, "connectors")`; the state records the scope and `actor`, the login that started the flow. For a provider routed through Composio it also records the toolkit and the pending Composio account id, and the callback stores that account only if Composio lists it under this workspace's own opaque id (rule 648). Since 2026-10-19 (rule 2242, branch only) twenty starts an hour per workspace in `rate:connectstart:{sc(u)}`, taken after the role and provider checks; over it a redirect to the Integrations page with `error=slow_down` and no state. |
| 46 | GET | `/api/connect/{provider}/callback` | O who started the flow: a valid, unused `state` issued for this provider, the matching `thakeen_oauth` cookie, a signed-in account that owns the state's scope, the same login that started it, and admin+ (`connectors`) at that moment | `currentUser` and `sameSecret(cookie, state)` in `connect/[provider]/callback/route.ts`, before any code exchange; the state's `actor` must equal the acting login (a state from before `actor` was recorded completes only for the owner), else `error=session_mismatch`; then `can(u, "connectors")`, else `error=forbidden`; Shopify also verifies the query `hmac`. Target scope comes from the state record. A store connected in place of another store of the same platform drops the old store's sales snapshot under that same scope after the save (`dropSnapshotIfStoreChanged`, rule 2207, 2026-10-18, branch only). Failed checks are limited per workspace before the outside call: 10 an hour and 30 a day in `rate:keycheck:{hour|day}:{sc(u)}`, 429 `slow_down` with `Retry-After`; a passing check gives its hits back (rule 2240, 2026-10-19, branch only). |
| 47 | POST | `/api/connect/{provider}/key` | O, admin+ (`connectors`) | `currentUser`; `forbid(u, "connectors")`; saved under `sc(u)`. A WooCommerce key for another site drops the old site's sales snapshot under `sc(u)` after the save (`dropSnapshotIfStoreChanged`, rule 2207, 2026-10-18, branch only). Failed checks are limited per workspace before the outside call: 10 an hour and 30 a day in `rate:keycheck:{hour|day}:{sc(u)}`, 429 `slow_down` with `Retry-After`; a passing check gives its hits back (rule 2240, 2026-10-19, branch only). |
| 48 | POST | `/api/publish/{id}` | O; `id` resolved with `getTask(sc(u), id)`; connection read under `sc(u)`; a `publish` decision recorded under `sc(u)` when the item was pending; admin+ (`publish`) | `currentUser` `publish/[id]/route.ts:18-19`; `forbid(u, "publish")` `:21`; scope lookup `:24-25`; rejected item 409 `:29`; `byOf(u)` read with the connections `:31`; brand gate with `publish: true` 409 `:34-35` (no placeholder of any kind goes out; the gate gets the body's `provider`, and only for `whatsapp`, a template for Meta's review, numeric `{{n}}` variables pass, rule 659); `targetsFor` 400 `wrong_target` `:37`; the send, the result write and the approval go through `publishOnce(sc(u), id, provider, {again, live, check})` (`live` only from the body's `live === true`; `live` with a provider other than `wordpress` is 400 `live_unsupported`, checked before the target; rules 618-622) (`src/lib/publish-once.ts`, which runs the rejected and brand checks again on the copy it sends, then calls `publishTask` and `updateTask` under `sc(u)`); `record` `:54-58`: `recordDecisions(sc(u), ...)` with `by` `:56`, only when the stored item was pending, and one `published` activity row under `sc(u)` with `by` `:57`, only for a post that went out: a publish (`:75`) or an `earlier_live` answer (`:69-71`, at the time of the earlier attempt, which ended `publish_unknown` and wrote neither); a refused, busy, duplicate or unknown attempt returns without it. Answers since 2026-09-29: 409 `publish_busy` while another publish of the same item and platform runs, 409 `already_published` (with `url`, `at`) when the item already went there, 409 `publish_unknown` (with `at`) when this or an earlier publish ended without an answer that proves nothing went out, 409 `duplicate_post` when the platform refused the same text again (X's duplicate, Facebook's #506: nothing went out, the connection is untouched), 409 `earlier_live` (with `at`) when that refusal answers a `again` after an unknown attempt (the earlier attempt went live and is now recorded on the item, with `decidedAt` at that attempt's time when the item was pending, and in the decision and activity records), 409 with the check's error when the item changed under the lock; `{again: true}` is the owner's confirmation to publish anyway. Since 2026-10-05 (rules 563-564) the body must carry `version` (`taskVersion`): missing is 400 `version_required`, different from the task the route read is 409 `changed` with the task, and `publishOnce`'s check compares it again under the publish lock (409 `changed`). Since 2026-10-12 (rule 1410, `flows.md` 54 item 6), after the target and connection checks and before `publishOnce`, `publishScopeCheck` checks the item's title and text with the `output` layer: `enforce` answers 409 `agent_scope_held` with the refusal in `message` (nothing sent, the item unchanged) and, when the classifier fails, 503 `agent_scope_unavailable` (fails closed); `shadow` logs and publishes; `off`, or a text the founder released (`agentScopeRelease.hash` equals `itemHash(t)`, rule 1411), makes no classifier call. |
