| 50 | POST | `/api/integrations` | U, member | `currentUser`. The request records `email` = the acting login and `account` = the owner's email. Since 2026-10-12 (rule 1353): `id` must be in `INTEGRATIONS` (the vote is keyed by it and the row names the catalogue's name) or start with `other:` (free text cut to 80 characters, counted under `other`), else 400 `missing`; then `allow("intreq:{login}", INTEGRATION_REQUESTS_PER_HOUR, 3600)` (30 an hour), else 429 `slow_down` with `Retry-After`. |

### 1.5 MCP and the OAuth authorization server

| # | Method | Path | Who | Enforced by |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 51 | OPTIONS | `/api/mcp` | A | CORS preflight `mcp/route.ts:49`. |
| 52 | GET | `/api/mcp` | A | Static descriptor `mcp/route.ts:52`. |
| 53 | POST | `/api/mcp` | A for `initialize`, `ping`, `notifications/initialized`, `tools/list` (`mcp/route.ts:63-65`); M for `tools/call` | `userFromBearer` `mcp/route.ts:67-68`: owner accounts only, actor kind `token` with role `owner`. Dispatch maps every tool to an action (`MCP_TOOL_ACTION` in `src/lib/mcp.ts`: `ask_noor` `chat`, `generate_content` `generate`, `list_inbox`, `read_file`, `list_files`, `get_brand_rules` and `check_brand` `read`; `save_draft` `drafts`; Reem's `agenda_get` and, since 2026-09-30, `agenda_next` `read`, and `todo_create`, `todo_update`, `todo_propose_done` `todo.write`; the chat tool `handoff_note` (agents' handoff notes, not Majed's handoffs, which have no MCP tool either) is deliberately absent), refuses a tool not in the map (-32602) and any tool `can()` refuses (a tool error, nothing run) `:73-75`. `ask_noor` (since 2026-10-01) refuses an empty message, then an agent the plan does not include (`entitlements(user).agents`, rule 782; a tool error, "This agent is not included in this account's plan. Nothing was sent.", nothing counted, and no plan triggers it yet), then an ended trial (`trialOver`, rule 330, with the Subscription page's absolute link), then checks the burst limit (`chatRefusal`, the same buckets and the same `chatCaps(u)` ceilings as the web chat, its tool error naming the account's own ceiling, rule 331), read in the same round the agent-scope ceiling of refused messages (`agentScopePreflight`, rule 795, the same per-brand-scope counters as the web chat, only in enforce; answered first as a tool error with the slow text, no classifier call), and the account's monthly chat allowance on the same counter as the web chat (`reserveChatTurn`); in enforce an out-of-scope message gets the refusal as plain text (not `isError`), with no model call and nothing counted (rule 794), all before any model call and before `chat()` saves anything; a refusal is a tool error in plain words (`chatRefusedText`, `refusalText`), and the turn is settled like a web turn. All data access uses `user.scope ?? user.email` (`sc(u)`), the owner's active brand. `get_brand_rules` and `check_brand` read only the caller's own brand and spend nothing. `save_draft` writes one `pending` task with `source:"external"` to `sc(u)` only (arguments naming a scope, email or id are ignored), is never auto-approved, runs the brand gate in code, spends no credits, and is rate limited per account by `draftAllowed` (30 an hour, 150 a day); since 2026-10-10 (rule 802) the draft's title and body then pass the agent-scope output check (the only model call, metered to Thakeen's global meter, never to the account): in `enforce` a draft outside the team's work is a tool error with the refusal and nothing is saved (it still counts toward the hourly and the daily draft allowance, which ran first); `shadow` (the default) saves it and logs the refusal; a classifier error saves it. `generate_content` items pass the same output check (rule 801, row 32): withheld items are named in the answer, which says their credits were refunded only when the refund landed, and a batch with nothing left is a tool error with the refusal. `generate_content` (since 2026-10-10, rule 796) refuses an unknown type first ("Unknown type."), then reads the agent-scope preflight (modes and the per-brand-scope ceiling of rule 795) and calls `generateBatch` with the notes' gate (`notesGate`), which runs after the batch's own free refusals (paused, no brand, ended trial, missing cart data: each its own tool error, with no classifier call) and before the charge: with the layer `off` or the notes empty nothing is asked, counted or refused; otherwise in enforce only, a spent ceiling or more than 60 classifier asks in the hour or 200 in the day for the brand scope (`rate:agent-scope-gen:h|d:{scope}`) is the slow text as a tool error with no classifier call (a request with no notes is never held by it), and text outside what the team works on is a tool error with the refusal text, counted toward the ceiling; `shadow` only logs (and over the asks bound skips the classifier), awaited before the answer; a classifier error lets it through; so nothing is charged or saved on a refusal; it then charges against the owning agent's monthly cap in the active brand and returns a tool error at the cap, charging nothing. Reem's `agenda_get`, `todo_create`, `todo_update` and `todo_propose_done` (2026-09-29) are free, run in the bearer's active brand with `source:"mcp"`, share the per-account allowances of rows 102-103, and can set a repeat (not stop it) and cannot complete, skip, delete, restore or reopen an item, change its inbox link or read or write a contact's phone number (arguments are not checked against the schema here; `toolFields` and `mutateItem` decide in code). Its turn ends by the request's deadline (rule 336). |
| 54 | GET | `/api/mcp/token` | U, owner (`mcp.token`, refused to a founder impersonating) | `forbid(u, "mcp.token")` after `currentUser`. Returns only `{ok, exists}` (rule 555): the token is stored as a hash and is shown once, by `POST`, never again. The slot is keyed by the acting login, which past the gate is always the owner. |
| 55 | POST | `/api/mcp/token` | U, owner (`mcp.token`, refused to a founder impersonating) | `forbid(u, "mcp.token")` after `currentUser`. |
| 56 | DELETE | `/api/mcp/token` | U, owner (`mcp.token`, refused to a founder impersonating) | `forbid(u, "mcp.token")` after `currentUser`. |

| 56b | POST | `/api/performance` | O, admin+ (`performance.import`): an import replaces the last snapshot and `performance-learnings.md`, so it needs what deleting needs | `currentUser`; `forbid(u, "performance.import")`; `allow("perf:{email}", 20, 3600)`; body capped at 2,000,000 characters (413); parsed as data; saved under `sc(u)`: `savePerf` and the upload's own totals in `adstats:{scope}:csv` (rule 731). No model call, no credits. |
| 56c | DELETE | `/api/performance` | O, admin+ (`performance.delete`) | `currentUser`; `forbid(u, "performance.delete")`; `clearPerf(sc(u))` deletes `perfimport:{scope}` and `performance-learnings.md`, and `adstats:{scope}:csv` goes with them; the ad accounts' `adstats` rows stay. |
| 56d | GET | `/api/mcp/grants` | U, owner (`oauth.grants`), account level | `currentUser` `mcp/grants/route.ts:11-12`; `forbid(u, "oauth.grants")` `:13`; `listGrants(u.email)` `:15` lists the account's OAuth grants without tokens. |
| 56e | DELETE | `/api/mcp/grants` | U, owner (`oauth.grants`); grant `id` from the query resolved under `u.email`, the account | `currentUser` `mcp/grants/route.ts:18-19`; `forbid(u, "oauth.grants")` `:20`; `?all=1` revokes every grant of the account `:23`; `?id=` revokes one: the `id` must match `^[a-f0-9]{16}$` `:25` and another account's id is 404 `:27`. |
| 57 | POST | `/api/oauth/register` | A | `allow("oauth:register:ip:{ip}", OAUTH_REGISTER_PER_IP, OAUTH_REGISTER_WINDOW)` (30 per hour, `src/lib/rate.ts`) first, else 429 `{error:"slow_down"}` with CORS and `Retry-After` `oauth/register/route.ts:6`; a refused registration counts. Then `registerClient` validates redirect URIs `src/lib/oauth.ts:56-60`. |

The code is bound to `u.email`, the account. Since 2026-10-12 an access token lives 1 hour (rule 1347), a code exchange replaces this account's earlier grant of the same client (rule 1348), and every code or token renews the client's 90-day life (rule 1349). |

### 1.7 Founder console

| # | Method | Path | Who | Enforced by |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 75 | POST | `/api/admin/login` | A presenting the admin key (break-glass since rule 1645) | The console write check first (`consoleWriteRefusal`, rule 1647: a foreign `Origin`, `Sec-Fetch-Site: cross-site` or a non-JSON body is 403/415); rate limit 5 per IP per 15 min; `sameSecret` on the key. Since 2026-10-13 (rule 1645, branch `claude/staff-identity`): once `admin:2fa` is enrolled the body also needs `code` (the console's 6-digit code or its recovery code, `checkCode`); a missing or wrong code is the same 401 `{ok:false}` as a wrong key. Opens a key session (`createAdminSession`); a 6-digit code also opens a step-up tied to it. Writes one `key.login` row (`actor: "key"`, `via: "key"`, `extra.factor`). Since 2026-10-23 (rule 3705, branch `claude/m0-staff-door-653d30`): surrounding spaces of the key are trimmed before `sameSecret`, on the server as well as in the form; the form is reached only by its direct address, no screen links to it. |
| 76 | DELETE | `/api/admin/login` | A | None; ends the caller's own key session: deletes its server record and the `nashr_admin` cookie (`destroyAdminSession`). |
| 225 | POST | `/api/admin/staff/login` | public (staff sign-in, step 1) | 2026-10-13, rules 1631, 1644 (branch `claude/staff-identity`). `consoleWriteRefusal` first; 5 per IP per 15 min (`stafflogin:ip:{ip}`, a right password hands its hit back); `{email, password, code?}`. Every refusal (unknown address, wrong password, no password yet, no authenticator yet (the password alone never enrols; only a setup or reset code does), disabled, locked) is the same 401 `invalid`, and one bcrypt compare always runs (a fixed dummy hash for an unknown address). 10 failures for one address in 15 minutes lock it for 15 minutes (`stafffail:{sha256(email)}`, outside `admin:log`; one `staff.locked` row per lock, written in `after()`). The lock blocks the password alone: while locked, the right password with a right 6-digit `code` from the person's authenticator (5 code tries per address and per IP per 15 minutes, step burned) opens the session at once (`next: "done"`, `staff.signin` row with `locked: true`); the lock stays. A right password opens only the 5-minute pre-auth step (`__Host-thakeen_staff_pre`, `staffpre:{sha256(id)}`) and answers `next: "code"` or `"enrol"`. No mail is ever sent (rule 1643). Since 2026-10-23 (rule 3702): when the address is locked (or this failure locks it) the 401 body adds `locked: true`, so the form shows the code field; an unknown address counts and locks the same way, so the flag says nothing about which addresses exist. |
| 226 | DELETE | `/api/admin/staff/login` | staff session (its own) | `consoleWriteRefusal` first. Deletes the staff session record and both staff cookies; a `staff.signout` row when a session was ended. |
| 227 | POST | `/api/admin/staff/login/code` | pre-auth cookie at stage `code` | `consoleWriteRefusal`; 30 per IP per 15 min (`staffstep:ip:{ip}`); the pre-auth step must be live and issued after the record's `sessionsAfter`. A 6-digit code from the person's OWN authenticator (5 tries per IP and 5 per address per 15 min, a right code hands them back, each 30-second step burned per person) opens the staff session and a step-up tied to it (`staff.signin`). A recovery code (hashed, single use, rule 1642) is spent and moves the step to `enrol`: no session opens until a new authenticator is confirmed (`staff.recovery`). |
| 228 | POST | `/api/admin/staff/login/enrol` | pre-auth cookie at stage `enrol` | `consoleWriteRefusal`; 30 per IP per 15 min. `start`: a new secret and its `otpauth://` URI (`Cache-Control: no-store`), kept sealed 15 minutes in `staffenrol:{sha256(email)}` tied to this pre-auth step; 503 `no_secret_key` without `CONNECT_SECRET`. `confirm`: a code from it (the same tries and burned steps) stores it sealed with its `kid` and 8 new recovery codes (answered once), ends earlier sessions when an authenticator is replaced, and opens the session (`staff.enrol`). No skip (rule 1632). |
| 229 | POST | `/api/admin/staff/setup` | public, with a setup or reset code | `consoleWriteRefusal`; 5 per IP per 15 min (`staffsetup:ip:{ip}`). `{email, code, password}`: the code is typed into the form, never in a URL; it works once, within 30 minutes, for the address it was issued for (`staffsetup:{sha256(code)}`); anything else is 401 `invalid`. The password policy (12 characters to 72 UTF-8 bytes, not on the common list, not the customer password of the same address, rule 1641) is checked before the code is spent. A reset code also removes the authenticator and recovery codes; every session of the person ends. Then the pre-auth step moves to `enrol` (`staff.password`). Since 2026-10-23 (rules 3701, 3706): also redeems an invite code (24 hours, from the link fragment, typed by the page, never a query), and with no `code` in the body takes the code from the setup hand-off cookie the founder bootstrap set, then clears it; still only for the address the code was issued for. |

Only `{action: "seen", until?}`: writes `until` (the newest lead the console showed; clamped to now; the current time when omitted) to `admin:leads:seen` unless the stored time is already later, so the leads shown stop counting as unread and a lead that arrived after the load does not; another action is 400 `bad_action`, an `until` that is not a date string of at most 40 characters is 400 `bad_until`. |

Since 2026-10-01 (rules 446-447, 454-455): `action` "add" or "void" (else 400 `bad_action`); "add" is checked by `parseTopup` (`src/lib/topups.ts`: the pack is one of `TOPUP_PACKS`, the amount paid 0..100,000 SAR, a real payment date at most a year back and a day ahead, a reference of at most 80 characters: 400 `bad_pack`, `bad_paid`, `bad_date`, `bad_ref`) before any read; "void" takes an `id` of 16 hex characters (400 `bad_id`). 404 `not_found` for an unknown account. The change runs under the ledger lock (`addTopup` / `voidTopup` in `credits.ts`): a trial is refused (409 `topup_needs_paid_plan`), so is a 51st live pack (409 `too_many_topups`), an unknown pack id is 404 `topup_not_found`. Since 2026-10-02 (rules 454-455, 459): the payment date is not after today in Riyadh; "add" takes the console's `paymentId` (`pay_` and hex, else 400 `bad_id`; 400 `bad_id` too when a plan payment holds it, 409 `payment_voided` when its payment was voided) and records the pack's payment under it through `addPaidTopup` → `recordPayment` (the customer's `withUserLock`, the commission under `lock:partner:{email}`), so a retry adds one pack and one payment; "void" voids that payment and its commission (`voidPaidTopup`) before removing the pack. Since 2026-10-02 (rules 460-463, founder decision): `action` "refund" `{id, credits, on, ref, refundId?}` refunds a live pack's unused credits: `parseTopupRefund` checks the credits shown, the transfer day and a required reference (400 `bad_credits`, `bad_date`, `bad_ref`) and the ids are checked (400 `bad_id`: a 16-hex pack id, an `rf_` request id) before any read; `refundPaidTopup` then runs under the customer's `withUserLock` (the commission under `lock:partner:{email}`): exactly the credits left leave the pack and the balance, the refund is recorded on the pack's payment and the commission on it is cut by commission × refunded / paid, once; 409 `topup_expired`, `topup_voided`, `topup_refunded`, `topup_not_paid`, `nothing_left`, `left_changed`; 404 `topup_not_found`. Since 2026-10-02 (rule 465) "refund" `{id, finish: true}` ("Finish refund") skips `parseTopupRefund` and only completes a refund already on the pack whose payment was not marked, from the pack's own refund record (409 `not_refunded` otherwise); a pack whose payment was never recorded has it recorded with the refund from the pack itself (409 `payment_missing` off a paid plan). The amount is computed in code from the pack (credits left × price per credit paid), never taken from the body. Each change is logged in `admin:log` with the balance before and after, the pack without its payment reference, the payment id and the commission (for a refund, the credits and amount refunded and the commission before and after, never the transfer's reference). No Redis key is built from the request but the account's own `user:{email}`, its lock and its `payments:{email}`; the partner's ledger key comes from the account's stored attribution (`referredBy`), never from the body. |
| 180 | POST | `/api/admin/agent-scope/release` | F; target `email`, `brandId`, `itemId` from body | `adminActor` first (401 before the body is read). Since 2026-10-12 (rule 1411, `flows.md` 54 item 6): `email` and `itemId` strings and `itemId` a plain id (`^[A-Za-z0-9_-]{1,64}$`), else 400 `bad_request`; `brandId` (default `default`) a plain id and one of `listBrands(email)`, else 400 `bad_brand`, so no key is built from an unchecked value; unknown account 404 `no_user`; an item not in `scopeFor(email, brandId)` 404 `no_item`. Under the task lock it stores `agentScopeRelease: {hash: itemHash(t), by, at}` on the item; nothing is published here. One `admin:log` row `{actor, email, what: "agent_scope_release", brandId, itemId, at}`. Since the 2026-10-12 build batch the second factor gate (`secondFactorRefusal`, item 10a) runs right after `adminActor`. Its `admin:log` row is written through `adminAudit` (rule 1172, section `quality`). |
| 181 | GET | `/api/admin/agent-scope` | F | `isAdmin` first, else 401 (rule 1413, 2026-10-12). `?day=` must match `YYYY-MM-DD`, else today (UTC); it only names a log key `agent-scope:log:{day}`. One round reads the modes, `agent-scope:eval:last`, the day's newest 500 log lines (owner emails and the first 200 characters of each text: founder only) and this month's `usage:_all:{month}:kind:agent_scope` and `…:usd_micro`. |
| 182 | POST | `/api/admin/agent-scope/modes` | F | `adminActor` first, else 401 (rule 1412). The only writer of `agent-scope:modes`. Any subset of `prompt` (on/off), `input`, `role`, `output` (off/shadow/enforce); none sent or a value outside its set is 400 `bad_mode`. Since rule 1500 a `reason` of 3 to 200 characters (`reasonRefused`, rule 1185), checked after the modes and before any read: missing or shorter is 400 `reason_short`, longer is 400 `reason_long`, nothing written. `input` or `output` to `enforce` needs `agent-scope:eval:last` with `passed: true` on `agentScopeModel()`, else 409 `eval_not_passed`; `role` needs no eval. One `admin:log` row `{actor, what: "agent_scope_modes", before, after, reason, at}`. Since the 2026-10-12 build batch the second factor gate (`secondFactorRefusal`, item 10a) runs right after `adminActor`. Its `admin:log` row is written through `adminAudit` (rule 1172, section `quality`). |
| 183 | POST | `/api/admin/agent-scope/eval` | F | `adminActor` first, else 401 (rule 1412); `maxDuration = 300`. 503 `model_paused` unless `modelState()` is on (no call); 409 `already_running` while `agent-scope:eval:lock` (300 s, an owned lock freed only by its own run) is held. Runs the labelled set (about 200 classifier calls, ten at a time, global meter only: the founder's money, never an owner's credits), stores the score in `agent-scope:eval:last`, one `admin:log` row `agent_scope_eval`. Nothing else calls it: no cron, no test. Since the 2026-10-12 build batch the second factor gate (`secondFactorRefusal`, item 10a) runs right after `adminActor`. Its `admin:log` row is written through `adminAudit` (rule 1172, section `quality`). |
| 204 | GET | `/api/admin/2fa` | F | `adminActor` first (401). Answers whether the console's second factor is enrolled (and when) and whether this browser holds a live step-up (rule 1377). Never answers the secret. |
| 205 | POST | `/api/admin/2fa` | F | `adminActor` first (401), then `consoleWriteRefusal` for every action (rule 1647, 2026-10-13; the `x-admin-key` header actor skips its Origin and JSON parts). `start`: a new authenticator secret, kept encrypted 15 minutes in `admin:2fa:pending` (tied to the actor), enrols nothing; replacing an enrolled one passes `secondFactorRefusal` first (rule 1375); 503 `no_secret_key` when production has no `CONNECT_SECRET`. `confirm`: a code from that secret (rate limited) writes `admin:2fa`, answers the recovery code once, opens a step-up, logs `2fa.enrol`. `verify`: a 6-digit code opens a step-up; the recovery code removes the enrolment and logs `2fa.recovery` (rule 1379). Codes: 5 wrong per IP per 15 minutes, 30 wrong per hour for the whole console (429 `slow_down` with `Retry-After`), a right code hands its hit back, every accepted step is burned (rule 1378). Rules 1370-1384. |
| 210 | GET | `/api/admin/integrations` | F | `adminActor(req)` first, else 401 (rule 1541, 2026-10-13, branch only). Read-only: no second factor (reads never need one), no audit row, nothing written, `cache-control: no-store`. Answers each outside service (`integrationsView`, `src/lib/integration-status.ts`) as on, partial or off from the PRESENCE of its environment variable names only, the names with set or missing, and the code gates (`CLAUDE_PAUSED`, and the presence of `PAYMOB_LIVE`, `MEDIA_MODELS_ON`, `LLM_PROVIDER`, `COMPOSIO_API_KEY` + `COMPOSIO_PROVIDERS`, and since 2026-10-18 a Composio provider whose two toolkits have only one `COMPOSIO_AUTH_*` name set, rule 2165). Each row also carries `callbacks`, the fixed callback paths its platform app must list (rule 2165), and `webhooks`, the webhook paths (Shopify's `/api/shopify/compliance`, rule 2172). Never a value, a length or a prefix (rule 1542). Beside the rows, after `adminActor`, `shopifyCompliance` (rule 2173): `complianceSummary()` reads `shopify:redact` and the 10 newest of `shopify:compliance` (topic, store, time, workspace count; no customer field is ever kept), null when that read fails. And `connections` (rules 2177-2178): `connectionHealth()` reads the fixed `connections:health` row the stores cron writes, as `{at, workspaces, rows}` with the registry's platform name and three counts per row, busiest first; never a workspace, account or store; null before the first run or when the read fails. Since rule 2202 it also carries `keepAlive`, that run's token keep-alive counts (`{due, refreshed, lost, failed}`), null when none is recorded, and since rule 2205 `uninstallHooks`, that run's Shopify `app/uninstalled` subscription counts (`{due, subscribed, failed}`), null the same way. |
| 257 | GET, POST | `/api/admin/passkeys` | F (key session, `x-admin-key`, founder customer session) or staff; POST only a console owner (`passkeys.switch`: staff role `owner`, the key and the founder) | 2026-10-20, customer passkeys rule 2426 (console half), branch `claude/wave3c-passkeys`, release expand; spec flow E. `adminActor(req)` first, else 401. GET answers `{ok, on, may}` (`passkeysOn()`, `cache-control: no-store`; `may` = `staffMay(consoleRole(req), "passkeys.switch")`, so the Integrations row disables its toggle with an owner-only line for any other role; POST checks again), no second factor, no audit row. POST `{on: boolean, reason}`: `staffMay(consoleRole(req), "passkeys.switch")` (403 `forbidden`, so a support staff session is refused before anything else) → `secondFactorRefusal(req, who)` (the console write check, then 403 `second_factor` without a live step-up or a right code once a second factor is enrolled; its code check is rate limited per IP) → `reasonRefused` (400 `reason_short` / `reason_long`) → `on` a boolean (400 `bad_request`) → `setPasskeysOn(on)`: off writes `passkeys:off` = `"1"` (SET NX), on deletes it (GETDEL), and the write answers the state before → one `admin:log` row `{section: "system", action: "passkeys.off" | "passkeys.on", target: "passkeys", what, via, before: {on}, after: {on}, reason}`; an audit write that throws puts the switch back and answers 500 `audit_failed` (a failed undo is logged with `log.error`). Fail-open by design: only an explicit `"1"` is off. No key is built from the body and no stored passkey is read, changed or deleted; with the switch off both sign-in routes (rows 255, 256) and both register steps of row 252 answer 404 `unavailable`, while list, rename and remove keep working. |

An apply also needs `secondFactorRefusal` and `reasonRefused` (400 `reason_*`), the conversion to allow it (S1 `multi-org-index` is dry only: 400 `dry_run_only`) and a clean dry run of the same conversion in the last 15 minutes (`conversion:clean:{name}`, taken with GETDEL; 409 `dry_run_first`). Every run holds `lock:conversion` (409 `busy`). Writes one `admin:log` row, section `data`, action `account_ids_dry_run`, `account_ids_apply` or `multi_org_index_dry_run`, the counts in `what` and `after`, the reason on an apply. Answers counts only (`countsOnly`: no address, id or key), `cache-control: no-store`; a throw is 500 `failed` with a log line that names no record. `maxDuration` 300 s |

| 236 | POST | `/api/admin/founding` | F; target email from body, normalized, account must exist | `adminActor` (401), `secondFactorRefusal` (403), action one of `grant_seat`/`end_seat`/`grant_lock`/`end_lock` (400), a reason (400) (rule 1710). Under the account's `withUserLock`; a hand seat is capped at 20 (409 `seats_full`), a duplicate grant or an end with nothing live is 409; a lock's plan must be self-serve and its first day real, not in the future, at most 400 days back (400). One `adminAudit` row (section billing, before and after). Works while Paymob is off. |

### 1.8 Cron

Since 2026-10-08 (rule 755) the jobs that act for an owner skip an owner who has not verified the address: `dailyRoster()` and `allUsers()` leave them out (`daily` keeps only Reem's purge for their idle scopes, and its renewal, top-up settlement and hold refunds in `dailyFor` do not run for them; `evening`, `weekly`, `routines` and `stores` skip them), and the reminder sweep of `cron/secretary` keeps their reminders waiting. `cron/media` still finishes jobs already started and paid, and `cron/golden` is unaffected. Their authentication is unchanged.

| # | Method | Path | Who | Enforced by |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 88 | GET | `/api/cron/daily` | C | `sameSecret(bearer, CRON_SECRET)` `cron/daily/route.ts:139`. Serves every active brand scope (`dailyFor`), then, in the same resumable batch, only Reem's purge for the idle scopes (`purgeIdle`: archived brands and every brand of a suspended account, which get no other scheduled work, charge or email). Since 2026-09-30 (rule 246), once per account on its default brand's entry, `sweepOnboardHolds` (`src/lib/onboard-confirm.ts`) refunds an added brand's held onboarding charge (`User.onboardPaid`) that no run charged or reused for a day, or only drops the hold when that brand exists; archived brands are covered there, and a failure is reported and never stops the rest of the day (`:32-46`); since 2026-10-01 (rule 337), on the same entry, `sweepWeeklyHolds` (`src/lib/schedule.ts`) refunds a Sunday-plan charge held on the account (`User.paidOnce`) whose week ended with no plan in its brand, or only drops the hold when the plan exists (`:47-60`). Since 2026-10-08 (Paymob branch, rules 712-714), first on the default brand's entry and only while Paymob is configured, `renewPaymob` charges a due online subscription on its saved card (Moto integration, at most one attempt per account per Riyadh day), marks it past due, or locks it after 7 days of grace; a locked subscription gets no credit renewal (`renewalDue`). Since 2026-10-19 (rules 2273-2275), once per account on its default brand's entry before the renewal, `endHandPlanIfDue` (`src/lib/hand-plan-end.ts`) moves a paid plan set by hand whose paid-through day passed to an ended trial, under the ledger lock, reading only the stored record; no request value names the account. |
| 212 | GET | `/api/cron/daily` (margin step) | C | Inside row 88's `sameSecret(bearer, CRON_SECRET)` check, on the first hop only (no `chain`), after the reminder sweep (rule 1534, 2026-10-13, branch only). `runMarginAlert` reads every account's stored record (never a request value), writes only `margin:alerted:{day}:{hash}`, `margin:day:{day}`, `margin:last` and one bell item per `ADMIN_EMAILS` founder; it changes no customer account, plan, credit or model (rule 1537). The answer adds `margin` (counts only). |
| 240 | POST | `/api/admin/fair-use` | F; target `email` from body | `adminActor` first (401), then `secondFactorRefusal`, then a reason of 3-200 characters (`reasonRefused`) (rule 1730, 2026-10-13, branch only). `email` normalized (400 `bad_email`); `action` exactly `lift` or `clear` (400 `bad_action`); under the account's `withUserLock` it sets `User.fairUseLift = {month: monthKey(), at, by}` or deletes it (404 `not_found` for an unknown account). One `admin:log` row (`section` users, `action` fair_use_lift or fair_use_clear) with the lift before and after and the reason. Answers `{ok, fairUse: {email, month, lifted, paused, costSar, limitSar}}`, `no-store`. No key is built from the body but the account's own `user:{email}`, its lock and its two usage counters. |

It reads only `usage:{email}:{month}:usd_micro` and `media_usd_micro` of the caller's own account, and writes only the founder marker `fairuse:alerted:{month}:{hash}` and the founders' bell items. Since 2026-09-30 it also runs Majed's auto-run (`src/lib/handoff-auto.ts`, `flows.md` 35) for brands whose owner switched it on: at most one handoff item per brand scope a day (cron id `{scope}:auto_handoff`), read only from that scope's own index and records, started only when `autoBlock` allows it under the handoff's lock with the settings read again (the owner's own proposal from the app's chat, whose card reached the thread (`shownAt`) and whose conversation still exists, still proposed, at most 7 days old, a cap on the item's agent, a type that keeps the margin floor on a cold cache, at most 3 items and 12 credits), inside the weekly cap and an attempt count, charged to the item's agent under that cap by `systemBy("auto_handoff")`, every task pending. Every item, a routine or an auto-run, needs 180 s of the invocation left before its hold and charge (`retry:late`, nothing charged; a hop that ran something hands late items to a fresh invocation). Runs `sweepReminders` (scopes come from `sec:duescopes`, never from the request; nothing new after 40 s) and, when CRON_SECRET is set, starts row 113 with that bearer for the zones due. Answers counts only. Writes `sec:tick:last` only for a QStash call (2026-09-30) |

Runs `eveningWrap` for every active workspace of the roster (filtered by the brand's zone when `zones` is given) through `runCronBatch` (2026-09-30) |

### 1.9 Media jobs and provider callbacks

| # | Method | Path | Who | Enforced by |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 93 | GET | `/api/media/{jobId}` | O; `jobId` resolved with `getJob(sc(u), jobId)`; any role (`member`) | `currentUser` `media/[jobId]/route.ts:18-19`; `getJob(sc(u), jobId)` `:22-23` reads nothing unless the id matches `^[a-f0-9]{32}$` (`JOB_ID`), and another account's id is 404 with no provider call; `allow("mpoll:u:{email}", 120, 60)` `:24` (429 `slow_down`); `advance` `:27` bounds provider calls to one status call per job per 4 s. The task is read under `sc(u)` `:33`. `maxDuration = 120`. |
| 94 | POST | `/api/media/hook/{provider}` | A (public webhook) | Only `kie` and `higgsfield`, and only when configured, else 404 `media/hook/[provider]/route.ts:51`; per-IP `allow("mhook:{provider}:{ip}", 240, 60)` `:53` (429), before the body is read; body at most 64 KB on the declared length and a running total `:14,56-57` (413, after Next has buffered up to 6 MB of it for the proxy, rules 693 and 774); JSON `:58-61` (400); only the provider task id is read (`kieTaskIdFrom` / `hfRequestIdFrom`) and it must match `PROVIDER_TASK_ID` `:63-64` (400); kie with `KIE_WEBHOOK_HMAC_KEY` set: `verifyKieSignature` (timestamp ±300 s, HMAC compared with `sameSecret`) `:66-69` (401); `jobForHook` `:70` takes the scope from the stored `mjobref` pointer and requires the job's provider and task id to match; `advance` in `after()` without `force` `:72`, so hints share the job's 4 s throttle and job lock and the status is re-fetched with our key. 200 whether or not a job matched. `maxDuration = 120`. |
| 213 | GET, HEAD | `/m/{name}` (2026-10-13, rules 1754-1758) | A by route, private by content: a signed-in member of the workspace whose index holds the file (`mfile:{sc(u)}:{name}`), or the holder of a 24-hour signed link for that one name (`?exp=&sig=`, minted only at hand-off to Meta, kie, Higgsfield, Composio or WordPress) | `allow("mfile:{ip}", 1200, hour)` first in each handler (429); the name must match `MEDIA_NAME` (`[a-f0-9]{32}.(jpg\|mp4)`) before any disk or store read; a request with `sig` or `exp` is judged by `shareValid` only (`sameSecret`), a session never rescues a bad signature; otherwise `currentUser` and the index entry in the caller's own scope. Every refusal (bad name, other workspace, missing file, bad or expired signature) is the same bare 404 with `no-store`. `src/app/m/[name]/route.ts`. Open past the staging lock (rule 1760). |
| 214 | GET | `/api/drives` (2026-10-13, rules 1781, 1786, branch only) | O; any role (`member`) | `currentUser` first (401). Answers the drives that can be used now (`driveReady`: Composio on for the provider and its auth config set) with a `connected` flag read from `conn:{sc(u)}:{provider}`, this workspace's library (`listLibrary(sc(u))`: index entries with no task) and whether the store is set. `src/app/api/drives/route.ts`. |
| 215 | POST, DELETE | `/api/drives` (2026-10-13, rules 1778-1785, branch only) | O; `library.write` (member) for `search`, `import` and DELETE, `library.export` (member, impersonation-blocked) for `export` | `currentUser` (401), then `forbid` (403), then the drive must be one of `google_drive`, `dropbox`, `onedrive` and ready, else 404 `unknown_drive`, then `allow("drive:{action}:{sc(u)}")` per hour: search 60, import 30, export 30, delete 120 (429 + Retry-After). The account used is the one stored for `sc(u)`; the query, file id, name and URL are tool arguments only, never keys. Import: id format and extension checked before the call, `s3url` fetched through `safeFetch` with `httpsOnly` and a cap, bytes sniffed. Export: only a URL whose index entry is in `sc(u)` (`mediaEntryOf`), staged through a 24-hour signed link. DELETE `?name=`: only a library file (no task) of `sc(u)`. Composio's text never reaches the caller. |
| 216 | POST | `/api/media/upload` (2026-10-13, rule 1783, branch only) | O; `library.write` (member) | `currentUser` (401), `forbid` (403), `allow("mupload:{sc(u)}", 30, hour)` (429), `Content-Length` over 5 MB + 64 KB refused before the body is read (413), the `file` field at most 5 MB, judged by magic bytes, re-encoded as one JPEG under `sc(u)` (`keepInLibrary`). Images only: the proxy buffers at most 6 MB of a body (rule 774). |

Related non-API surface: `/oauth/authorize` (`src/app/oauth/authorize/page.tsx`) requires a session and redirects anonymous visitors to `/ar/app/login?next=...`; it shows the consent form only when `can(u, "oauth.approve")` (the owner, not a founder impersonating) and otherwise says only the owner can connect apps, naming the acting login; `/.well-known/oauth-*` are public rewrites (`next.config.ts:16-23`).

### 1.10 Staging site and release checks (2026-10-08)

| # | Method | Path | Who | Enforced by |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 152 | POST | `/api/staging-access` | A (the staging site only; public by design, because nobody holds the cookie yet) | `isStaging()` first (`APP_ENV=staging`), else a bare 404 `staging-access/route.ts:28`. Per-IP `allow("stagingaccess:{ip}", 5, 900)` `:29`, counted before the body is read: the sixth try in 15 minutes is 429 `{ ok: false, error: "slow_down" }` with `Retry-After: 900` (`retryLater`, rule 680), even with the right code. The code is read only from the form body, `req.formData()` `:31`, never the URL; `sameSecret(code, STAGING_ACCESS_KEY)` `:38`. No key set, a missing `code` field and an unreadable body are all a wrong code. Wrong: 303 to `{APP_ORIGIN}/{lang}/staging-access?e=1`, no cookie. Right: 303 to `{APP_ORIGIN}/{lang}` with cookie `thakeen_staging` = `accessToken(key)` (never the code), options from `sessionCookie(30 days)` `:46-47`: `HttpOnly`, `SameSite=Lax`, `Path=/`, and `Secure` whenever `APP_ORIGIN` is https, whatever Host and `x-forwarded-proto` the proxy forwarded (otherwise the helper's rule: Secure except plain http to loopback). `lang` is `en` when the form says so, else `ar`. Redirects use `appOrigin()`, never `req.url`. The bucket key is `clientIp(req)`: off Vercel it is the `x-forwarded-for` entry `TRUSTED_PROXY_HOPS` from the right (a whole number 1 to 3; unset or anything else is 1, the last hop; a shorter list gives its first entry; rules 773 and 979). It is per client only if that entry is the client's address, which is unverified on Hostinger: if a second proxy appends after the CDN, the default last hop is the CDN edge and callers behind one edge share a bucket until the founder sets `TRUSTED_PROXY_HOPS=2` (`GET /api/admin/client-ip`, rule 908, shows what arrives). Only when no address arrives at all does every caller share the `unknown` bucket; flows.md section 51, item 7 is the staging check. Nothing in the route logs the code. Every other method (GET, HEAD, PUT, PATCH, DELETE, OPTIONS) is the same bare 404, on staging and off it, with no `Allow` header `:51-61`, so live shows nothing of the route; without them Next would answer 405, and OPTIONS 204 with `Allow: OPTIONS, POST`. A method outside Next's list is 400 from Next itself, as on every route. Rules 674-675, `tests/staging.test.ts`, `tests/role-gates.test.ts`. |
| 153 | GET | `/api/health` | A (public by design: the release probe and the smoke script read it with no session; on staging it sits behind the lock like every other path, so they send the cookie) | No session and no tenant data: the only store access is the rate counter `rate:health:{ip}`, which the limiter increments on every call. Per-IP `allow("health:{ip}", 60, 60)` `health/route.ts:22`: the 61st call in a minute is 429 `{ ok: false, error: "slow_down" }` with `Retry-After: 60` (`retryLater`, rule 680) `:25`. The bucket key is `clientIp(req)`: off Vercel it is the `x-forwarded-for` entry `TRUSTED_PROXY_HOPS` from the right (a whole number 1 to 3; unset or anything else is 1, the last hop; a shorter list gives its first entry; rules 773 and 979). It is per client only if that entry is the client's address, which is unverified on Hostinger: if a second proxy appends after the CDN, the default last hop is the CDN edge and callers behind one edge share a bucket until the founder sets `TRUSTED_PROXY_HOPS=2` (`GET /api/admin/client-ip`, rule 908, shows what arrives). Only when no address arrives at all does every caller (the release probe at about 50 a minute, the smoke script, a monitor, a visitor) share the `unknown` bucket of 60 a minute and the 429 reaches whoever calls next. flows.md section 51, item 7 is the staging check, and the probe and the smoke script are not relied on before it passes. The probe starts its requests at least 1.2 s apart (`PROBE_INTERVAL_MS`, at most 51 in any 60 seconds, so a smoke run or a manual check still fits) and counts a 429 as `throttled`, not as a failure, without waiting or stopping; the smoke script's health check waits for `Retry-After` on a 429 (a minute when the header is missing, never more than 65 s), asks once more, and reports a 429 that survives as a shared or full bucket, not an outage (`scripts/probe.mjs`, `scripts/smoke.mjs`). A limiter that throws (its Redis call fails) counts the caller as allowed, so the route still answers while the store is down: it exists to say the process is up. A slow limiter does too: the answer waits for it at most 1 s (`LIMITER_WAIT_MS` `:17`, raced `:21-24`), because the Upstash client retries for about 4.3 s and the probe gives a sample 10 s; the count still lands when the store answers. The body is exactly `{ ok: true, commit, built }` (`BUILD_COMMIT` and `BUILD_TIME`, `unknown` when the build set neither) with `Cache-Control: no-store` `:26-29`: no environment name, no store status, no dependency versions. Two opt-in query forms extend it for the uptime monitor and leave that body exactly as it is (rules 1011-1013, `tests/monitoring-ci-parity.test.ts`). `?store=1` is public and uses the same limiter: it reads one key (`health:ping`, one `GET`, raced against `STORE_WAIT_MS` = 2 s) and adds `store: { ok, ms }`; the answer is 503 with `ok: false` when the read fails or hangs, and it names no key, host or error. `?crons=1` needs `Authorization: Bearer ${CRON_SECRET}` compared with `sameSecret` after the limiter has counted the call: 401 `{ ok: false }` without it and when `CRON_SECRET` is unset; with it, the answer adds `store` and `crons: { stale, waiting, jobs }` from the cron heartbeats (`src/lib/cron-heartbeat.ts`, rule 1014) and is 503 when the store is down or a job is late (a job with no heartbeat yet is `waiting`, not late). Declared `public-read` in `tests/role-gates.test.ts`. Rule 676, `tests/health.test.ts`. |
| 237 | GET | `/api/founding/seats` | A (public by design: the founding offer's seats left) | Per-IP `allow("foundingseats:ip:{ip}", 60, 60)` (429) (rule 1711, 2026-10-13, branch only). Answers `{ok, open:false}` while Paymob is off, before the sale or at zero seats, else `{ok, open:true, left, total}`. No session, no account data, never an email; `Cache-Control: public, max-age=30`. |
| 259 | GET | `/api/ops/summary` (2026-10-20, rules 2380-2382, branch only) | The ops agent holding `OPS_AGENT_TOKEN` (`Authorization: Bearer`) | Per-IP `allow("ops:ip:{ip}", OPS_CALLS_PER_IP_PER_MINUTE, 60)` (429) first, then `opsAuthorized` (`sameSecret`; 401, also while the variable is unset; a token in the query string is never read). No session, no tenant data: counts only (`opsSummary`, src/lib/ops.ts), no email, name or workspace. `no-store` |
| 260 | POST | `/api/ops/telegram` (2026-10-20, rules 2383-2385, branch only) | The ops agent holding `OPS_AGENT_TOKEN` | The same per-IP limit and token check, then `parseRelay` (400), then `allow("ops:relay", OPS_RELAY_PER_HOUR, 3600)` and, for a proposal, `allow("ops:proposals", OPS_PROPOSALS_PER_DAY, 86400)` (429). Sends only to `TELEGRAM_CHAT_ID`, as plain text; a chat, parse mode or buttons in the body are ignored. The only proposal action is `github_issue`; it is stored, not run |
| 261 | POST | `/api/ops/telegram/callback` (2026-10-20, rules 2386-2389, branch only) | Telegram holding `TELEGRAM_WEBHOOK_SECRET` (`X-Telegram-Bot-Api-Secret-Token`) | The same per-IP limit, then `telegramAuthorized` (`sameSecret`; 401, also while unset). `handlePress` acts only when both the presser and the chat are `TELEGRAM_CHAT_ID`, on callback data `ok:` or `no:` plus 24 hex, and on a stored action in `OPS_ACTIONS`; the proposal is taken with GETDEL, so one press runs it once. The only action opens one issue on the constant `OPS_GITHUB_REPO` with `OPS_GITHUB_TOKEN`. Always 200 `{ ok: true }` after the secret check |
| 262 | POST, DELETE | `/api/push` (2026-10-20, rules 2562-2563, branch `claude/agent-status-push-0d7320`) | U, owner or member acting in their own session | `currentUser` first (401); push off answers 503 `push_off`; `pushLogin(u.actor)` is null for an MCP token and for a founder's view of the account (403); `allow(accountKey.pushBucket(login))` = `rate:push:{login}`, 60 an hour shared with row 260 (429); body `{endpoint}` must pass `pushEndpoint` (https, a browser push service host, no credentials or port, at most 1024 characters; 400 `bad_endpoint`). POST stores `{endpoint, login, at}` in `pushsubs:{sc(u)}` (at most 10 devices, the oldest dropped); DELETE removes that endpoint's field from the caller's own workspace only. No scope, login or brand is read from the body |
| 263 | POST | `/api/push/seen` (2026-10-20, rule 2564, branch `claude/agent-status-push-0d7320`) | U, owner or member acting in their own session | `currentUser` first (401); push off answers 204 and writes nothing; `pushLogin` (403 for a token or a founder's view); `allow(accountKey.seenBucket(login))` = `rate:tabbeat:{login}`, 120 an hour (429). Sets `tabseen:{login}` for 5 minutes. No body is read |
| 264 | GET | `/api/push/latest` (2026-10-20, rule 2567, branch `claude/agent-status-push-0d7320`) | U, owner or member, called by the device's service worker with its own cookie | `currentUser` first (401: the worker then unsubscribes the device); `pushLogin` (403); the same `rate:push:{login}` bucket as row 258 (429). Answers `{ok, title, body, href}` with `cache-control: no-store`: the app's short name, one agent's name and state in the actor's language from `pushlast:{login}`, or a generic line. Never a draft, a brand name, a count or an address |

Related non-API surface: `/ar/staging-access` and `/en/staging-access` (`src/app/[lang]/staging-access/page.tsx`) are the form that posts to row 152. The page is a 404 unless `isStaging()` and the language is `ar` or `en`, is marked `noindex, nofollow`, and sets no cookie. Both paths and the route are open in the proxy's lock; every other staging path is a 404 without the cookie (section 0, the staging visitor row).

## 2. App pages under `src/app/[lang]/app`

Since 2026-10-20 (rules 885, 891, branch `claude/brand-parts-bc-29277a`) every page that reads or writes the brand's data through `sc(u)` sits under `b/[brand]/` (`/{lang}/app/b/{brandId}/…`); account pages (billing, members, brands, account, security, danger, refer, partner, mcp, notifications feed, help) keep their path. The old brand paths answer 307 from `[...legacy]/page.tsx`. The rail's brand button is the `BrandSwitcher` (rule 893, `src/components/app/BrandSwitcher.tsx`, not a page): it lists `GET /api/brands` active brands and offers «أضف براند» / "Add a brand" to owners and admins under the cap, display only; the checks are `brands.switch`, `brands.manage` and the cap in the route. Paths in the table below are relative to `src/app/[lang]/app`.

Two layers exist. Layer 1, the layout (`layout.tsx:24-41`: the `PUBLIC` list at `:24`, the verify redirect at `:35-40`, the login redirect at `:41`): reads `x-pathname` set by the proxy (`src/proxy.ts:48`, for `/{lang}/app` paths only); if there is no user, the path is non-empty and it does not end with `/login`, `/signup`, `/forgot`, `/reset`, `/admin`, `/join` or (since 2026-10-08) `/verify`, redirect to `/{lang}/app/login`; except that when `currentUser()` is null because the owner has not verified the address (rule 751), the layout asks `currentUserUnverified()` and redirects an owner to `/{lang}/app/verify`, with the requested page in a `safeNext`'d `?next=` (rule 754); a member of an unverified owner resolves in neither and goes to login (rule 767). Layer 2, the page's own check, listed below. The proxy itself enforces no login: on `/api` it refuses only a state-changing request another site started (section 0 and item 23 of the known gaps), and on staging it adds the access-cookie lock (section 0).

Since 2026-09-29 pages also render by role: they compute flags with `can()` and hide or disable the controls a lower role may not use, with a one-line note (`ReadOnlyNote`). This is display only: each control's route checks the same action.

The sidebar shows the acting person's name, email and role, a Members link for everyone, Referrals only with `referral` and MCP only with `mcp.token`, the console link when `isFounderActor(user)` (`layout.tsx`), and a link to the public `/{lang}/updates` page for every signed-in login whatever its role (`Sidebar.tsx`); all display only. The trial banner and the limit dialog offer paying and raising caps only to the owner and tell other roles to ask the owner.

Public page `/{lang}/updates` (outside the app shell, 2026-09-29): anyone can read it. It reads `currentUser()` only to show the viewer's own votes and the vote and suggestion controls; anonymous visitors get the counts and sign-in links. Votes are read with `listWishes(u?.email)`, the account email, which for a member is the owner's. The page reads wishlist data through `listWishes` only and never reads `wish:suggestions`.

### 2.1 Founder console pages under `src/app/[lang]/admin` (2026-10-09, rules 870-872)

Its own route tree beside `/{lang}/app`, so the customer layout (sidebar, trial banner, install hint, limit modal) never wraps it. The proxy sets no `x-pathname` here and the customer layout's login redirect does not run.

| `login/page.tsx` (2026-10-13, rules 1631, 1652) | public: email and password, then the code, a recovery code or the enrolment (QR code; recovery codes shown once). A browser with a staff session goes straight to `next` (a console path only) or the overview | none (the routes of rows 225-228 decide) | none |
| `login/setup/page.tsx` (rules 1639, 1640) | public: email, the setup or reset code typed into the form (the page reads no search parameter), a new password, then the enrolment | none (row 229) | none |
| `staff/page.tsx` (rules 1638-1640, 1650) | a staff owner (list, add, turn off and on, reset, role, close the customer path) or a key session (list and reset only); others with console access see a notice | `requireAdmin()`, then the role; the API checks again (rows 231-234) | the staff records only |
| `account/page.tsx` | a staff session: own password, new recovery codes, sign out; others see a notice | `requireAdmin()`, then `gate.staff` | the caller's own `staff:{email}` |

All four new pages are `noindex` (`staffScreenMeta`) and every `/{lang}/admin` page and `/api/admin/staff*` route gets `x-robots-tag: noindex, nofollow` and `Cache-Control: no-store` from `src/proxy.ts` (rule 1653).

## 3. Cross-tenant isolation

### 3.1 How a scope is derived
0. The scope comes from the ACTIVE account (multi-workspace M1, rule 2135): the account the session record names (`acct`), re-checked on every request (`roleIn` and `membershipHolds` on records read in that request), never one named by the request. A membership removed or paused, or an account suspended, ends the session's access to that account at its next request, and a stale `orgs:` entry, a forged `mbr:` row or a row without its roster entry grants nothing (rule 2110, `tests/multi-org-isolation.test.ts`). From M2 (behind `MULTI_ORG`, 2026-10-21) the account is the one the request's handle names (`/{lang}/app/w/{handle}/…` for pages, `x-thakeen-ws` for API calls), and only when the session's person holds it now: `resolveSession` matches the handle among `orgs:{pid}` and the legacy home and re-checks the match with every forward check, and any other handle is a 404 (`tests/multi-org-isolation-handles.test.ts`). The handle is an HMAC of the account id, never a key part and never stored. Without a handle the session's `acct` applies, as in M1; the workspace handle in the URL and the `x-thakeen-ws` header must name the same account (decision 7 of the spec; 409 `workspace_missing`); the brand is the person's choice in that account, `activebrand:{pid}:{aid}` (in the person's home account the legacy `activebrand:{login}` first, written alike), and only while it is one of that account's active brands, for owners too. Items 1 and 3 describe a session written before R1, which resolves as before.
1. `currentUser()` resolves the session's login (section 0.1): an owner login loads its own record; a member login loads the owner's. In both cases `activeBrandId(login)` reads `activebrand:{login}` (default `"default"`), a member's value must be one of the owner's active brands, and `scopeFor(ownerEmail, brandId)` returns the owner email for the default brand or `` `${ownerEmail}#${brandId}` `` otherwise (`src/lib/auth.ts`, `src/lib/brands.ts`). A member's own address is never a scope.
2. `sc(u) = u.scope ?? acct(u)` since 2026-10-18 (account-id R1, flows 88; `acct(u) = u.id ?? u.email`, and in R1 the id is the first address, so the scope is unchanged); before it `u.scope ?? u.email` (`src/lib/auth.ts:101`). `scope` and `brandId` are runtime only and stripped by `saveUser` (`auth.ts:149`).
3. The active brand can only be set to an id found in the account's own `brands:{owner}` list (`brands/route.ts`), or to a brand just created by `addBrand`. Both move only the acting person's choice in this account (`setActiveBrandIn`: `activebrand:{pid}:{aid}`, plus the legacy `activebrand:{login}` when the account is their home; multi-workspace M1, rule 2119). Archiving the brand the caller has open resets the caller's choice to `default`, and an admin archiving the brand the owner has open resets the owner's choice in this account too. A session from R1 on re-validates every stored id, the owner's included, except when the store fails to answer the brand list: that request then uses the stored choice unchecked, still inside this account's scope; a session written before R1 still does not re-validate the owner's own stored id, and a member's is re-validated on every request.
4. Brand ids are 8 base36 characters from `Math.random()` (`brands.ts:69`).
5. Every brand-scoped key embeds the scope string (see `architecture.md` section 3). Isolation between tenants therefore depends on (a) the session to email mapping and (b) scope strings of different accounts never being equal.
6. Account-level (not brand-level) records: user, credits, sessions, notifications, MCP tokens, referral data, usage, analytics, and since 2026-10-01 the chat allowance's counters (`usage:{email}:{YYYY-MM}:chat_msgs`, `:chat_over` and `:chat_cred`, `chatm:{email}:trial`) and the owner's "continue on credits" choice (`User.chatOverflow`): one allowance for every brand and seat of the account. The per-agent monthly counters live on the account's user record but are keyed by brand scope (`agentSpend.by[scope]`), and the caps they are checked against are per brand (`gov:{scope}.agentCaps`), so one brand's spend and caps never apply to another. Chat thumbs are per brand scope since 2026-09-21 (`fblog:{scope}`), and so is the feedback built from them, the decision records and the judge's notes for an agent's prompts (`feedbackFor`, 2026-09-29). Media: a job record is per scope (`mjob:{sc(u)}:{id}`) but charges and refunds the account (`job.email`); the trial video marker (`mtrialvid:{email}`) and the render limits (`rate:media:*:{email}`) are per account; the render spend counters (`render:_all:*`) are global and carry no email. Since 2026-09-29 the account also holds its team: `members:{owner}`, `invites:{owner}`, the `member:{email}` records and each member's `activebrand:{member}`; all of them are keyed by the owner email or the member's own login, never used as a scope.
7. Cron and admin code construct scopes with the same `scopeFor` (`dailyRoster()` and `allUsers()` in `schedule.ts:237-258`, `admin/users/[email]/route.ts:37`, `account.ts:37`). The routines cron keys every read and write by the `scope` `allUsers()` set (`routinelast:{scope}:{id}`, `routinelock:{scope}:{id}`, `routinecap:{scope}`, `routinehold:{scope}:{id}`, `routinetry:{scope}:{id}:{period}`, `schedule:{scope}`, `gov:{scope}`, the tasks).
8. MCP callers resolve the active brand the same way a browser session does (`userFromBearer`, `src/lib/mcp.ts`), so a bearer token works in the brand the owner has active. Fixed 2026-09-20; it used to always mean the default brand. Tokens resolve to owner accounts only: a token naming a member login is refused.
9. Members (2026-09-29). A member session resolves to the owner's `User`, so `sc(u)`, `charge(u.email)`, plan checks and every scoped getter work unchanged and never see the member's address; only `u.actor` differs. The routes that act on a member or an invite take the email or invite id from the request but act only on a record whose `memberOf` or `owner` is the caller's account (3.2).

10. Account id (2026-10-18, rules 1980-1986). Account keys are built from `acct(u)` only, scopes are parsed only by `accountOfScope`/`brandOfScope`, and an account is read by id (`getAccount`) from the session, a stored pointer or `users:all`, never from the request. A sign-in address finds its person through `login:{email}` (`getLogin`), which accepts an account only while its `email` is still that address.

11. Brand selector (2026-10-20, rule 886, branch only). The `x-thakeen-brand` request header is the one request value that picks a scope, and only as a SELECTOR: `resolveSession` accepts it when it is "default" or an active brand of the session account's own `brands:{acct}` list, and builds `u.scope` from that row (`selectedBrand` in `src/lib/auth.ts`). A malformed, archived, unknown or foreign id, or an unreadable list, is a 404 from `currentUser()`/`currentUserUnverified()`; the account never changes, and the stored choice is not written. Callers that wrap `currentUser()` in a `try` must `unstable_rethrow(e)` first in the `catch` (none on 2026-10-20). Tests: `tests/brand-url-session.test.ts`. Where the header comes from (rule 887, `src/proxy.ts`): on a page request the proxy deletes whatever the browser sent and sets it only from the path's `/{lang}/app/b/{id}` segment (`brandFromPath`; a malformed segment sets none); on an `/api/` request a header passes untouched, and on GET or HEAD with none a `brand` query value is copied into it (R-C13: links and downloads cannot send headers), a value no header can hold becoming `?`, which is no brand id; the value is checked by `resolveSession` exactly as above, and a mutation never takes it from the query. Tests: `tests/brand-url-proxy.test.ts`.
12. Brand guard (2026-10-20, rule 888, branch only). A POST, PUT, PATCH or DELETE with a session cookie to a route in `BRAND_API` (`src/lib/brand-guard.ts`: every route file that changes state and calls `sc(` or uses `handler({ auth: "user" })`, plus `PUT /api/profile`, whose brand fields and `emails` are the brand's) that carries no `x-thakeen-brand` header is noticed by the proxy after the cross-site check. `BRAND_GUARD_MODE` is `"log"` in this release (R-C4, expand): the request goes on to its route on the session's own brand and one `brand_missing` warning with the path only is logged, so a tab opened before the deploy keeps saving; the contract release sets `"enforce"`, which answers 409 `{"ok":false,"error":"brand_missing"}` with `Cache-Control: no-store`. Reads, requests without a session cookie (MCP bearer, crons, webhooks) and account routes pass. `ACCOUNT_SC_API` lists the routes the scan takes that are the account's (`GET /api/account/export`, `POST /api/wishlist/suggest` and `/vote`), each with its reason; tests/brand-url-proxy.test.ts holds both lists to the routes on disk. Tests: `tests/brand-url-proxy.test.ts`.

### 3.2 Routes that accept an id, email or other locator from the request

| Route | Locator | Binding to the caller |
|---|---|---|
| Every workspace route with `MULTI_ORG` on, `POST /api/workspaces/switch`, `POST /api/workspaces/transfer` | workspace handle (`x-thakeen-ws`, the `w/{handle}` path segment, the switch or transfer body) | A selector only: matched among the accounts the session's person holds now (`matchHandle` over the person's own records, then `resolveIds`); a miss is the same 404 for every case and writes nothing. No route reads an account id from the request. |
| `PATCH /api/tasks/{id}`, `POST /api/tasks/{id}/image`, `POST /api/tasks/{id}/video`, `GET /api/tasks/{id}/pptx`, `POST /api/publish/{id}` | task id (path) | Key is `task:{sc(u)}:{id}`; a foreign id yields 404. No format check on `id`. The media job a render creates stores `scope = sc(u)` and `email = u.email` at creation, and the engine only ever writes the task under that stored scope, through `updateTask`, whose lock `tasklock:{scope}:{id}` is built from the same server-side scope and task id. |
| `POST /api/tasks/{id}/image` and `/video` body `model` | catalogue model id | `isMediaModelId` is a list membership test (not `in`), so `constructor` and other prototype keys are 400; the model must be of the route's kind. |
| `GET /api/media/{jobId}` | media job id (path) | `getJob(sc(u), jobId)`: the id must match `^[a-f0-9]{32}$` before a key is built, and the key is `mjob:{sc(u)}:{jobId}`, so another account's job id is 404 and causes no provider call. |
| `POST /api/media/hook/{provider}` | provider task id (kie `taskId` / `data.taskId` / `data.task_id`; Higgsfield `request_id`) | Format-checked against `PROVIDER_TASK_ID[provider]` (kie `^[A-Za-z0-9_-]{6,80}$`, Higgsfield a UUID) and used only to read the server-written pointer `mjobref:{provider}:{id}`; the workspace is the pointer's stored `scope`, never anything from the request, and the loaded job must carry the same provider and task id (`jobForHook`). Same shape as `oauthstate:{state}` below. Nothing else in the body is read, including result URLs. |
| Chat tools `generate_image`, `publish_item` (`src/lib/chat.ts:815,708`) | `task_id` chosen by the model | `getTask(user.scope, id)`; same binding. `generate_image` can only render the 1-credit default model: the tool has no `model` argument. |
| `PUT /api/models` | `id` (body) for `target: "conversation"` or `"brand"`; `model` (catalog id) | Conversation: the id must match `^[A-Za-z0-9_-]{1,64}$` before a key is built, and the key is `conv:{sc(u)}:{id}`, so another account's id is 404 with nothing written. Brand: no key is built from the id at all; the brand is `brand:{sc(u)}` and an `id`, when sent, must equal `u.brandId ?? "default"` (else 404). `model` is a lookup in the Map-backed catalog (`modelById`), so `constructor` and `__proto__` are 400 `unknown_model`. The China confirmation is written to `user:{u.email}`, the session's account. The `chat` a conversation pick answers is read from the session's account (`chatStatus(u, …)`), never from anything in the body. |
| `GET/POST /api/chat` | `conv` (query or body) | Key `chat:{sc(u)}:{conv}`; not checked against `convs:{sc(u)}`; no format check (the chat page applies `^[a-f0-9]{6,16}$` at `chat/page.tsx:51`, the API does not). A handoff proposed in a thread with no `conv:` record (a `conv` the client made up) is never started by Majed's auto-run: it treats the thread as deleted (`liveConversations`). |
| `PATCH/DELETE /api/conversations` | conversation id | Format-checked (`^[A-Za-z0-9_-]{1,64}$`) before a key is built: `conv:{sc(u)}:{id}`, `chat:{sc(u)}:{id}` and the lock `convlock:{sc(u)}:{id}`. |
| `GET/PUT/DELETE /api/files` | file name | `safeName` lowercases the name and replaces every character that is not a Unicode letter or digit, `.`, `_` or `-` (`[\p{L}\p{N}._-]`) with `-`, max 60 chars, then `file:{sc(u)}:{name}`. |
| `POST /api/brands` | brand id | Must be in `listBrands(u.email)`, the account's list; a switch writes the acting login's `activebrand:{login}` only; the page's `x-thakeen-brand` is never written (rule 892). |
| `/api/connect/{provider}*`, `POST /api/publish/{id}` body `provider` | provider id | `conn:{sc(u)}:{provider}`; start and key routes also require a registry match; `DELETE` and `POST /api/connect/{provider}` do not validate the provider id against the registry. |
| `GET /api/connect/{provider}/callback` | `state` | Scope is read from `oauthstate:{state}`; the signed-in account must own it, the acting login must be the state's `actor`, and the path `provider` must equal the stored provider. |
| `POST /api/oauth/approve` | `client_id`, `redirect_uri` | Redirect URI must be registered for that client; the code is bound to the session email. |
| `POST /api/oauth/token` | `code`, `refresh_token`, `client_id` | Code and refresh token are bound to the issuing `client_id`; email comes from the stored record. |
| `POST /api/auth/reset` | reset token | Email and login kind come from the stored `pwreset:` record; the login must still be of that kind. |
| `POST /api/auth/email-revert` | revert token | Account-id R2 (rule 2744): the person and addresses come from the stored `emailrevert:` record, never from the body; refused while `OPAQUE_IDS` is off. |
| `POST/DELETE /api/team/invite`, `PATCH/DELETE /api/team/member` | invitee email, invite id (SHA-256 of the token), member email | Invite: the email may be any valid address (it is the invitee), stored under the caller's account. Cancel: the invite must name the caller's account as `owner`, else 404. Role change and removal: the member's `memberOf` must be the caller's account, else 404. |
| `POST /api/team/accept` | invite token | The invite record names the owner, the role and the invitee's email; nothing comes from the session or the body except the name, password and language. |
| `POST /api/auth/signup` | `ref` code | Referrer resolved from `ref:{CODE}`; self-referral blocked by equality. |
| `POST /api/auth/verify/send`, `POST /api/auth/verify/check` | none (the address is the session's) | The code key is `verify:code:{email}` and the counter `verify:tries:{email}`, both built from the session's normalized email; the body carries only the typed code. |
| `POST /api/auth/verify/email` | `email` in body (the target address) | The only locator taken from the request, and never as a source: the account moved is the session's own (`currentUserUnverified`), only an unverified gated signup (`gatedSignup`), only to an address `getLogin` finds free under both login locks. A taken address is 409 `exists`, and every attempt counts in `verifymove:acct:{session email}`, so it is no address oracle. |
| `POST /api/support`, `/api/affiliates`, `/api/waitlist` | `email` in body | Not bound; free text. Support additionally records the acting login as `user` and the account (the owner's email) as `account`. |
| `GET /p/{code}`, `POST /api/partner/code`, signup `partnerCode` (2026-10-01) | partner code (path or body) | Public by design (partners share it). Trimmed, uppercased and checked against `^[A-Z0-9]{3,16}$` before `partnercode:{CODE}` is read (on signup, only after Turnstile when it is on, rule 419); the record found must name the same code. Attribution is written only to the caller's own account (`u.email`, or the account just created), under `withUserLock`. |
| `GET/POST /api/admin/partners`, `GET/POST /api/admin/payments/{email}` (2026-10-01) | partner or customer email, application id | Founder only (`isAdmin` / `adminActor`). Emails are normalized and the account or partner must exist; an application id must match `^app_[a-f0-9]{8,32}$`, a payment id `^pay_[a-f0-9]{8,32}$`, a payout id `^po_[a-f0-9]{8,32}$`. `PUT /api/partner` takes no locator: it writes `partner:{u.email}` only. |
| `GET/POST /api/admin/users/{email}`, `POST /api/admin/plan`, `POST /api/admin/topups` (2026-10-01), `POST /api/admin/brand-cap` (2026-10-12) | target email (and, to void or refund, a pack id) | Founder only; no binding by design. `admin/topups` normalizes the body's email once and reads, locks and saves only that account's record and its `payments:{email}` (rule 454); a pack id is looked up only among that account's own packs, after a 16-hex-character check, and a payment id only after the `pay_` check `recordPayment` applies. The decoded path value is normalized once (`admin/users/[email]/route.ts:66`) and that one key is used for the lookup, the lock keys and `wipeAccount` (`:125`); `POST /api/admin/plan` normalizes the body's email the same way (`admin/plan/route.ts:73`). |
| `POST /api/mcp` | bearer token | Email from `mcptoken:{sha256(token)}`; an owner account only (a member login's email is refused). |
| `PATCH /api/secretary/{id}`, chat and MCP `todo_update` / `todo_propose_done` | item id (path or tool argument) | Must match `^s[a-f0-9]{16}$` before a key is built; the key is `sec:item:{sc(u)}:{id}`, so another account's or another brand's id is 404 (tools: "No item with that id") and the item is untouched, whatever `scope` or `email` the body or the tool arguments name (tests rules 190 and 203). |
| `POST /api/secretary`, `PATCH /api/secretary/{id}`, chat and MCP `todo_create` | `taskId` / `task_id` (a link to an inbox item) | Must match `^[a-f0-9]{16}$` and exist as `task:{sc(u)}:{taskId}` (400 `unknown_task`); the automatic close reads the task under the same scope. From chat or MCP a link is accepted only by `todo_create` and only to a task still `pending` (400 `link_not_pending`), so the close always follows an approval the owner made after the link. `todo_update` never reads `task_id` (`toolFields`), and `mutateItem` refuses a link change from any actor but the owner on the web (`owner_only`): the MCP route passes arguments without checking them against the tool schema. |
| `POST /api/secretary/walk`, chat and MCP `agenda_next` | a card key or `skip_id` | Must match `todo:s[a-f0-9]{16}` / `task:[a-f0-9]{16}` (or the bare id, for the tool) before it is stored; it only ever lands in `sec:walk:{sc(u)}`, so naming another account's id skips nothing of theirs (2026-09-30) |
| Chat and MCP `free_slots` (Reem B3, 2026-10-10, rule 919) | `minutes`, `day` | Read only: `minutes` must be a whole number 15-240 and `day` a real `YYYY-MM-DD` within today and the next 6 local days, else a refusal sentence; every key read (`sec:cal`, `sec:away`, the brand file, `sec:cal:link`, `sec:cal:events`) is built from `sc(u)`, never from the input. It writes nothing, spends no `sec:write` allowance and is `read` in `MCP_TOOL_ACTION`, so a member's MCP or chat call may use it. In chat only Reem's turn is offered it, and `runTool` refuses it for any other agent before any read (founder, 2026-10-10). Meeting titles in `agenda_get` are quoted with `JSON.stringify` under a header that marks them as data (rule 918). |
| `PUT /api/secretary/series/{seriesId}/keep`, `GET /api/secretary/series/{seriesId}/export` and the page `/app/todo?series=` (2026-10-03) | `seriesId` (path, or the `series` query) | Must match `^r[a-f0-9]{16}$` before a key is built and only ever becomes `sec:series:{sc(u)}:{seriesId}` (one `ZCARD` first): another brand's or account's id is 404 on both routes and an empty "not found" view on the page, and nothing of theirs is written or read; the page's series view reads that one set and then the brand's zone and keep flag, and no item record on a miss (rule 509). The export's file name is built from the validated id, never from a title. `SADD` to `sec:serieskeep:{sc(u)}` writes only the id that passed the form and existence checks (rules 506, 507). |
| `PUT` / `DELETE /api/secretary/away` (2026-10-03) | a contact's `label` | Only ever becomes a field (`partyKey(label)`) of `sec:away:{sc(u)}`: a `scope` or `email` in the body is not read, so naming another brand's or account's contact writes or ends nothing of theirs (DELETE answers 404). A label whose key is the owner's (an `OWNER_ALIASES` word) or an agent id's is `not_contact`, so no absence ever pauses the owner's or an agent's items (rules 481 and 483). |
| `POST /api/secretary/away/proposals/{id}` (2026-10-03) | `id` (path) | Format-checked (16 hex characters) before any key is built, and only ever read as `secawayprop:{sc(u)}:{id}`: another brand's or account's id is 404 and its proposal stays as it was. What is saved is the stored proposal's label and day, never a body value, and `by` is the session's login (rule 485). |
| `PUT /api/secretary/calendar` (2026-10-03) | none: the body is the settings object, and every field is checked by name | The key is `sec:cal:{sc(u)}` from the session alone: a `scope` or `email` in the body is refused with 400 `bad_request` (not an allowed field), so no body can write another brand's or account's Working days, and `updatedBy` is the acting login, never a body value. GET reads the same single key (rule 484). The optional `workHours` window (2026-10-10, rule 922) is checked like the rest: one `{from, to}` on the quarter hour with from before to and no other key, else 400 `work_hours_invalid`. |
| `GET /api/secretary/feed/{token}` (2026-10-05) | `token` (path) | The token is the credential and the only locator. It is shape-checked before any key is built, hashed, and read only as `sec:feed:{sha256(token)}`; the record's `login` and `scope` are then checked (`feedHolds`) and the items are read with the record's `scope`, so a token reaches exactly the one brand it was made for and no request value builds a key. `POST` and `DELETE /api/secretary/feed` take no locator: the login and scope are the session's (`sc(u)`), and `lang` is only `ar` or `en`. |
| `POST /api/secretary/booking/{token}` (2026-10-05) | `token` (path), the raw body | The token locates the link and is shape-checked before any key is built, hashed, and read only as `sec:book:{sha256(token)}`; the record's `login`, `scope` and `provider` are then checked (`loginHolds`) and the signature is verified before the body is parsed. The item is written in the record's `scope` with the record's `provider`; the payload gives only the external booking id (cleaned, at most 200 characters, a part of the claim key `sec:book:ext:{scope}:{provider}:{id}`), the attendee's name, the event-type name and the instants, so no request value picks a scope, a login or a provider. |
| `GET`, `POST`, `DELETE /api/secretary/booking` (2026-10-05) | `provider`, `lang`, `token` (body of POST), `provider` (query of DELETE) | `provider` is checked against `BOOK_PROVIDERS` and `lang` against `ar` and `en` before use; the login and the scope are `loginOf(u)` and `sc(u)` from the session, never read from the request; the link address uses `appOrigin()`. The Calendly `token` is a secret the owner pastes: it goes only into the `Authorization` header of two fixed `https://api.calendly.com` calls through `safeFetch`, and is never stored, logged, put in a URL or returned. |
| `GET`, `DELETE /api/secretary/meeting/{id}` and `POST .../approve` (2026-10-05) | `id` (path), `kept` (body of POST: `ref`, `title`, `agent`, `dueAt`) | `id` is checked with `SEC_ID` before any key is built and must be a live booking item of `sc(u)`; the scope and the account are the session's, never the request's. `kept` is data: each `ref` is looked up in the stored set (a `Map`, so `__proto__` and the like are just unknown refs) and only stored proposals are created; `title` is cleaned with `cleanLine` and refused above 120 characters, `agent` must be exactly `owner` or an `AGENT_IDS` value, `dueAt` a real local day within 366 days. The booking item's title, which comes from the booker, is cleaned and cut before it goes into a description. What a model later proposes passes `cleanProposals` (never throws, drops what fails) and is stored as a proposal only: approval is this click by a signed-in login. |
| `POST /api/secretary/meeting/{id}/propose` (2026-10-05) | `id` (path), `kind` and `text` (body) | `id` is checked with `SEC_ID` and must be a live booking item of `sc(u)` before the body is read. `kind` must be exactly `notes` or `transcript`, `text` a string of at most 4,000 or 40,000 characters, refused before any charge. The text is untrusted: it goes to the model only as `<meeting_text>` + `asData(text)`, its instructions are told to be ignored, the model's answer is a tool call checked by `cleanProposals`, and the text is never stored or logged. The scope, the account and the credit holder are the session's, never the request's. |
| `POST /api/instructions/refs/search`, `POST /api/instructions/refs/import` (2026-10-10, rules 955-956) | `q` (body), `kind`, `id`, `title` (body) | `id` must match `[A-Za-z0-9_-]{10,100}` and `q` is at most 100 characters before anything is called; both go to Google only as tool arguments and no Redis key is built from them. The Google login and the files are those of `sc(u)`; an account id, `scope` or `email` in the body is never read. |
| `POST /api/instructions/restore`, `GET /api/instructions/history?compare=` (2026-10-10) | `revisionId` (body), `compare` (query) | Matched against the ids in `listHistory(sc(u))` (the list read from `skillshist:{sc(u)}`); a value that is not in it is 404 and no key is built from it. The workspace is `sc(u)`; `scope`, `email` and `brandId` in a body are never read (rule 949). |
| Chat `propose_meeting_tasks` (Reem's tool, 2026-10-05, rule 607) | `item_id`, `tasks[].title`, `tasks[].agent`, `tasks[].due` written by the model | `item_id` is checked with `SEC_ID` and must be a live `booking` item of `sc(u)` (`liveBooking`); every other case, including another brand's id, a non-booking or deleted item and a malformed or non-string id, gets one refusal text, so an id says nothing, and a scope or email in the input is never read. `tasks` passes `cleanProposals` (an unknown agent dropped, titles cut at 120, at most 10, a due day outside today to 366 days ahead left out, any `ref` or `itemId` of the model ignored); nothing valid is an error that stores nothing. Gate: `TOOL_ACTION` maps it to `todo.write`, so a chat with no acting login is refused (a member passes: `todo.write` is `member`); it is offered in Reem's turn only (`toolsFor`), refused in `runTool` for another agent, a turn with no agent, `via: "mcp"`, a token `by` and a token actor, and is not an MCP tool. It stores only the pending set (`savePending`, replacing the item's set) and creates no item; its answer says `stored` only after the save returned. No model call, no credit; `sec:write:{email}` limits it. |
| Chat `handoff_note` (agents' handoff notes, not Majed's `propose_handoff` below) | `to` | `"owner"` or an allow-listed agent id other than the sender (`isAgentId`); the sender is the chat turn's agent, never an argument; the note and any item go to `sc(u)` only; refused over MCP (2026-09-30) |
| `GET /api/activity` | `agent`, `kind`, `before` (query) | Checked against `AGENT_IDS`, `ACTIVITY_KINDS` and an ISO time before the read; they only filter `activity:{sc(u)}`, never build a key. |
| `GET /api/handoffs/{id}`, `POST /api/handoffs/{id}/run`, `POST /api/handoffs/{id}/decline` | handoff id (path), `item` position (body) | `isHandoffId` (`^[a-f0-9]{16}$`) before any key is built; the key is `handoff:{sc(u)}:{id}` (and the lock `handofflock:{sc(u)}:{id}`), so another account's or brand's id is 404 and stays untouched; `item` is only a position inside that record; nothing else in the body is read. |
| Chat `propose_handoff` | `items[].type`, `items[].brief`, `why` written by the model | `parseProposal` reads only these: the type must be in `HANDOFF_TYPES` (another agent's type; never Majed's, never Reem's, never a render), the brief is cleaned and cut to 600 characters; a cost, agent, status, scope, id or `requestedBy` in the input is ignored and set in code under the chat turn's own scope. |
| `POST /api/governance/rollback` | revision id (body) | `isRevisionId` (`^[a-f0-9]{12}$`) before any read; looked up only in `govhist:{sc(u)}`, so another account's or brand's revision id is 404 and nothing changes. |
| `PUT /api/governance`, `POST /api/governance/rollback` | `confirmAutoHandoff` (body) | Only the literal `true` counts (`"true"`, `1` do not); it is read by the route, passed to `saveGovAs` as the owner's confirmation for switching Majed's auto-run on, and never stored (`mergeGov` drops unknown keys). The switch itself, `majedAutoHandoff`, changes only with a real boolean. |
| Any brand route, header `x-thakeen-brand` (and on GET/HEAD `/api/` the query value `brand`, R-C13) | brand id | A selector only: `resolveSession` takes it when it is "default" or an active brand of the session account's own `brands:{acct}` list, else 404 (rule 886). The proxy sets it from the page path on pages and drops a browser-sent one there (rule 887); the guard logs a brand-route mutation that carries none (rule 888; 409 `brand_missing` once enforced). |
