No route accepts an email or scope from a non-founder request and uses it to read or write another tenant's workspace data.

## 4. Admin model

1. `adminEmails()` (`src/lib/auth.ts:327-329`): `ADMIN_EMAILS` split on commas, normalized. Fails closed since 2026-10-01: unset or empty means no founder email at all (rule 425); there is no address in the source.
   Since the staff identity (2026-10-13, rules 1630-1654, branch `claude/staff-identity`, phase 1 = expand): `ADMIN_EMAILS` keeps two roles. It names the alert recipients for good (golden set, margin alerts), and, until the contract release (rule 1651, not built), the founder customer accounts whose own session still opens the console (rule 1649). It no longer decides who the console's staff are: those are the `staff:{email}` records (section 0).
2. `isFounder(email)`: membership test. Used by `isFounderActor(u)` (sidebar link, `adminActor`, `isAdminCookie`) and as the recipient list for golden-set alerts (`golden.ts`, `adminEmails()`). `isFounderActor(u)` requires `u.actor.kind === "owner"` and `u.actor.email === u.email` besides the list: a member of the founder's own account (whose `u.email` is the founder's) and a founder impersonating never pass (2026-09-29).
3. `adminActor(req)` (`src/lib/auth.ts`) and `isAdmin(req)` (= `adminActor(req) !== null`), used by every `/api/admin/*` data and action route plus the admin branches of `/api/support`, `/api/affiliates`, `/api/integrations`: `"key"` when `WAITLIST_ADMIN_KEY` is set and the `x-admin-key` header equals it (`sameSecret`, throttled 30 per IP per 15 min), or when `adminKeySession()` finds a live key session for the `nashr_admin` id (the cookie holds a random id, never the key; item 5); that check also renews the key session, at most once a minute (rules 422, 435); otherwise the founder's email when `isFounderActor(currentUser())`, otherwise `null`. The key is never read from the URL. Since 2026-10-13 (rules 1630, 1649, 1650) the order is: the `x-admin-key` header, then a staff session (`readStaffSession`, `via: "staff"`), then a key session, then, only while `staff:config.customerPathOff` is unset, the founder's own customer session (`via: "founder-session"`). Who and how are kept per request in a `WeakMap` (`actorInfo(req)`), so the audit row's `via` and the role check never come from the request body. No bearer token, MCP token, OAuth grant or agent tool is ever a console actor (rule 1653).
4. `isAdminCookie()`, used by the `/{lang}/admin` layout and pages (`requireAdmin()`, rule 870; `/{lang}/app/admin` only redirects there since 2026-10-09): a live key session (`adminKeySession()`) or `isFounderActor(currentUser())`. Since 2026-10-13 (`consoleIdentity()`): a staff session first, then the key session, then the founder customer session while that path is open. A visitor with none of them is redirected to the staff sign-in page (rule 1652) instead of the 404: the sign-in page is public anyway, as the key form at `?login=1` is, so the 404 no longer hid anything, and a `SameSite=Strict` staff cookie never arrives on a link opened from mail or chat.
5. `nashr_admin` cookie: set by `POST /api/admin/login` after a rate-limited `sameSecret` comparison; the value is a random 32-byte id, never the key; the cookie carries the 12-hour absolute cap; httpOnly; lax; Secure by transport (`sessionCookie`). The record `adminsession:{sha256(id)}` holds a fingerprint of the key it was opened with: logout deletes it, rotating `WAITLIST_ADMIN_KEY` invalidates every one, unsetting the key disables the path (rules 420-424, 2026-10-01). The session ends after 15 minutes without an admin request (`ADMIN_SESSION_IDLE`) and never outlives 12 hours from the key entry (`ADMIN_SESSION_MAX`): every admin request (API call or console page load) renews it, at most once a minute, with one `SET XX EX` whose TTL never runs past the cap; both limits are also checked in code against the record's `at` and `seen`. Requests made through a customer impersonation that the key session opened renew it the same way (`keyImpLive`: the founder working in the customer view is not idle), and a key impersonation ends with it (rules 422, 429, 435; founder decision 2026-10-01: 15 minutes of inactivity). A cookie that still holds the raw key opens nothing.
6. Founder accounts are ordinary users: they sign up through the public signup route. Corrected 2026-10-13: signup HAS email verification since rule 751 (2026-10-08): an owner who has not proved the address with an emailed code resolves to `null` in `currentUser()` (section 0, "Owner who has not verified the address"), so registering an address listed in `ADMIN_EMAILS` does not open the console until that mailbox's code is entered. The founder-account path is expand only (rule 1649) and closable at runtime (rule 1650); staff logins (section 0) are not customer accounts at all and are created only by a staff owner or, for the first one, from a key session (rules 1639, 1640).
7. A suspended founder loses the session path (`currentUser` returns `null`) but the key path still works. Since 2026-10-13 a suspended or deleted customer account has no effect on a staff login of the same address (rule 1630): the two never share a password, a session, a cookie or a reset path.
8. Impersonation (`admin/users/[email]/route.ts`): requires `adminActor`. Refused with 409 `impersonation_active`, before any log row or cookie, when the caller's `thakeen_session` is already an impersonation session (its record has `imp`), so a second view can never save the first customer's session as the founder's way back (rule 1350). Otherwise saves the caller's current `thakeen_session` value in `thakeen_return` (2 h), sets `thakeen_impersonate` to the target email (2 h), then `createSession(target, minutes, {imp: who})` with the 15, 60 or 120 minutes the founder chose (120 when the request names none, rule 1177): the session record carries `exp`, its end, and `currentUser` refuses it from that moment even before the TTL drops it; the record carries the founder (or `"key"`) and resolves to actor kind `founder`, role `owner`. With the key, the record also carries `kp` (the key's fingerprint) and, from a key session, `as` (that key session's record hash), from `keyImpBinding`; `currentUser` refuses an `imp: "key"` session once the key is rotated or removed, once that key session is logged out or has ended (15 minutes without a request in the console or the customer view, 12 hours at most), or when `kp` is missing (rules 429, 435). The log entry carries `expiresInMin` (the chosen minutes) and, for one opened from a key session, `idleMin: 15, endsWithKeySession: true`. While impersonating, the founder console is closed through the session path (`isFounderActor` is false) and the `IMPERSONATION_BLOCKED` actions are refused; everything else acts as the owner. The app shows the founder `ImpersonationBanner` (rule 1177) only when the resolved session's actor is a founder, never from the `thakeen_impersonate` cookie alone, with the time left until `actor.until`.
9. Return (`admin/impersonate/route.ts`): no admin check; deletes the impersonation session record, restores `thakeen_return` into `thakeen_session` when that token is still a live session, clears the helper cookies, and logs `impersonate:stop` only when the burned record carried `imp`, with `actor` = that `imp` and `email` = the record's own email, never the `thakeen_impersonate` cookie value. It is rate limited to 30 per IP per 15 min (rules 540-542).
10. Audit: `adminAudit` (`src/lib/admin-audit.ts`, rules 1172-1174) is the only writer of `admin:log`; it drops secret-named keys at any depth, masks the value of every secret-named environment variable and secret-shaped text (rule 1189), never lets an `extra` field override who, when or what (rule 1184), cuts the reason to one line of 200 characters and keeps 5,000 rows. Since rule 1185 the founder write routes refuse a change without a reason (400 `reason_short` or `reason_long`, 3 to 200 characters, `reasonRefused`) for every admin caller, the console key included: every action of `POST /api/admin/users/{email}`, `POST /api/admin/plan` and `POST /api/admin/topups`, `void` on `POST /api/admin/payments/{email}` and `void_payout` on `POST /api/admin/partners`. The payment `record` and Paymob `refund`, and the partner `approve`, `reject`, `payout` and `confirm_bank` keep it optional until their forms ask one (deferred). Entries written by `POST /api/admin/users/{email}` and `POST /api/admin/plan` carry `actor` = the founder's email or `"key"` (`adminActor`), the target email, action, optional before/after (plan, credits, seats, the Agency deal as `credits@priceSar` or `none` since 2026-09-30, the account's chat messages, `chatAllowanceOf`, since 2026-10-01, the team members paused or restored, the founder's video grant as `all@{until}` or `plan` since 2026-10-08) and reason. `POST /api/admin/golden` and `POST /api/admin/wishlist` log `adminActor` too since rule 1186 (the literal `"founder"` before). The impersonation row is written before the session is created (rule 1187), and the redirect is `/ar/app` or `/en/app` only, checked again in the console (rule 1188). Since 2026-09-29 decisions on items (`decisions:{scope}`), governance revisions (`govhist:{scope}`) and the activity log (`activity:{scope}`) also carry `by` from `byOf(u)` (`src/lib/actor.ts`), worked out from the session's actor (section 0.1), never from a request body: the owner as `{kind: "owner", email}`, a member as `{kind: "member", email: <the member's own login>, role}`, an MCP bearer as `{kind: "token", email: <account>}`, scheduled work as `{kind: "system", job}`, and a founder impersonating as `{kind: "founder", email: <account>, imp: <the founder's email or "key">}`, from the session record's `imp`, not as the owner. `imp` names the founder console's own login (or the key), so it is kept in storage and shown only to the founder console (row 80): what the account's own logins receive (GET /api/activity, the Activity page, the Governance page, and the `entry` of PUT /api/governance and POST /api/governance/rollback) goes through `publicBy` (`src/lib/actor.ts`), which drops it, so such a row reads "Thakeen support"; the CSV export writes only `by.kind` and `by.email`. No cookie marks an action as support's: `byOf` reads only the session's actor, and the impersonation cookies (`thakeen_impersonate`, `thakeen_return`, `nashr_admin`) are never looked at. A user with no actor (a cron, routine or script user, never a request) is recorded as `{kind: "system", job: "cron"}`, not as the owner. The task records themselves (their `published` entries included) and the brand file carry no actor. `setLang` is logged since 2026-10-11. Since 2026-10-13 (rule 1638) every row carries `via` (`staff`, `key`, `header` or `founder-session`, from `actorInfo(req)`) and a staff actor is the staff email; the staff events are written with section `system`: `staff.bootstrap`, `staff.create`, `staff.disable`, `staff.enable`, `staff.role`, `staff.reset`, `staff.signin`, `staff.locked` (actor `system`), `staff.enrol`, `staff.recovery`, `staff.password`, `staff.signout`, `key.login` and `console.customerPathOff`. No row holds a password, a secret, a setup code or a recovery code.
10a. Second factor (2026-10-12, rules 1370-1384, 360 study C-004, leftovers L055): `secondFactorRefusal(req, who)` (`src/lib/admin-2fa.ts`) runs right after `adminActor` in every founder WRITE under `/api/admin` (POST `plan`, `topups`, `payments/{email}`, `partners`, `users/{email}` (impersonation and every account action), `golden`, `wishlist`, PUT and POST `skills`; since the 2026-10-12 build batch also POST `brand-cap` and `agent-scope/release`, `agent-scope/modes` and `agent-scope/eval`). Until the console has enrolled an authenticator (`admin:2fa`) it lets everything through, so the rollout cannot lock the founder out; after enrolment a write needs a live step-up (`nashr_admin_2fa` cookie, a random id; record `admin2fa:step:{sha256(id)}` tied to the actor and to a hash of the `nashr_admin` or `thakeen_session` cookie it was opened in; 30 idle minutes, 12 hours at most) or a 6-digit code in `x-admin-otp` (for the key header; a recovery code there is refused), else 403 `second_factor`. Reads (every GET, `export` included) stay key-only by choice (rule 1382); `POST /api/admin/leads` `seen` was in that list until the 2026-10-13 staff security review, which put it behind `secondFactorRefusal` (row 115). Exempt by design: `admin/login`, `admin/impersonate` (stop) and `admin/2fa` itself. Break glass when both the phone and the recovery code are lost: delete `admin:2fa` in the Upstash console. Since 2026-10-13 (rules 1636, 1637, branch `claude/staff-identity`): for a staff actor the step-up is the staff member's own (`who` = `staff:{email}`, tied to a hash of the staff cookie), opened at sign-in and by a code from the person's own authenticator (in `x-admin-otp` or through `POST /api/admin/2fa` `verify`; a recovery code is never taken there for staff), and the console's `admin:2fa` enrolment does not apply to staff. The same gate refuses any staff role other than `owner` with 403 `read_only` (unknown roles fail closed). Staff management, the customer-path switch and own credential changes need a FRESH code in the request as well (`freshCodeRefusal`). `POST /api/admin/staff/me` skips the role check (it changes only the caller's own login) and runs `consoleWriteRefusal` and the fresh code instead. Every console write also refuses a foreign `Origin`, `Sec-Fetch-Site: cross-site` and a non-JSON body (`consoleWriteRefusal`, rule 1647).
11. `WAITLIST_ADMIN_KEY` has two further roles: it is one of the ways through `isAdmin`, which gates `GET /api/waitlist` (row 74), and outside production it is the fallback encryption secret for connector credentials when `CONNECT_SECRET` is unset (`src/lib/connectors/store.ts:23`); in production an unset `CONNECT_SECRET` throws (`:22`).

## 5. Unclear or missing rules

Each item states what the code does and why the intended rule cannot be read from it. None of these is guessed as intended or unintended.

1. **Email characters versus scope strings.** Signup accepts any address matching `^[^\s@]+@[^\s@]+\.[^\s@]+$` (`auth/signup/route.ts:13`), which allows `#` and `:`. Extra-brand scopes are `email#brandId` (`brands.ts:61`) and keys are `prefix:{scope}[:{id}]`. An account registered as `victim@x.com#<brandId>` has a default scope equal to the victim's brand scope, and an account registered as `victim@x.com:<convId>` has a default thread key equal to the victim's `chat:{scope}:{convId}`. Both require knowing a random id. No rule forbids these characters and nothing states that scope strings must be unique across accounts. Fixed 2026-09-18: `validEmail` (`auth.ts:119`) refuses `:` and `#`, at signup and for invites (`team.ts:203`), so no address can equal another account's brand scope or thread key.
2. **No email verification at signup.** Accounts become active immediately. Combined with `ADMIN_EMAILS` (no hard-coded default since 2026-10-01, rule 425), founder status is granted to whoever first registers a listed address. No rule says founder addresses must be pre-registered.
3. **Rate limits on the public routes.** Fixed for OAuth on 2026-09-29: `POST /api/oauth/register` allows 30 registrations per IP per hour and `POST /api/oauth/token` 60 requests per IP per 15 minutes (`OAUTH_*` in `src/lib/rate.ts`), counted on every attempt and answered 429 `{error:"slow_down"}` in OAuth's shape with the CORS headers and `Retry-After` (the window, rule 680). Hosted MCP clients (claude.ai, ChatGPT) call both from shared egress addresses for many accounts, so the ceilings are sized for that; if one of them is ever throttled, raise the constant rather than drop the limit. The token endpoint's secret check now uses `sameSecret` (was `!==`). Registered clients still never expire. Login and signup are throttled per IP and per email; support, affiliates and waitlist were given a per-IP limit on 2026-09-20; chat is bounded per account (`chatCaps(u)` in `src/lib/rate.ts`: 30 an hour and 150 a day on a paid plan, 10 an hour and 30 a day on a trial, since 2026-10-01) because a turn can run up to five model calls; since 2026-10-01 that is a burst limit in front of the plan's monthly chat allowance (`src/lib/chat-allowance.ts`, the section "Chat allowance" at the end), and each turn's chat model calls are capped at $0.35 in code (`src/lib/chat-budget.ts`). `clientIp` prefers the platform headers and falls back to the last `x-forwarded-for` hop (`rate.ts`).
4. **Password policy differs by route.** Reset requires 8 chars plus a letter and a digit (`auth/reset/route.ts:13`); signup and change-password require only 8 chars server-side (`auth/signup/route.ts:10`, `account/route.ts:12`); the signup form enforces the stricter rule client-side only. Update: signup, change and reset, and invite acceptance, all use `strongPassword` (`auth.ts:117`) server-side now. The line references above are to `4959f7e`.
5. **Sessions end when the password changes.** Fixed 2026-09-18. A session record is `{email, at}`; the user record carries `sessionsAfter`, set on password change, password reset and founder `resetPassword`; `currentUser` refuses any session issued before it, including the older bare-email records (treated as issued at 0). The device that changed the password gets a new session. Password writes now happen inside the user lock on the fresh record. Suspension is still enforced on read. Since 2026-09-29 a session record may also carry `imp` (impersonation), `createUser` stamps `sessionsAfter` at creation so a re-registered address never revives an old session, each member has its own `sessionsAfter` (its own password change or reset), and the owner record's `teamSessionsAfter` (owner reset, founder reset) signs every member out without touching the owner's sessions.
6. **Suspension.** Fixed: `userFromBearer` (`mcp.ts:47`) refuses a suspended account, so its MCP personal and OAuth tokens stop working, and `allUsers()` (`dailyRoster`, `schedule.ts:246`) skips suspended accounts, so no cron (weekly plan, routines, daily emails) charges or emails them; the daily cron only purges their brand scopes' expired to-dos (`purgeIdle`). The `User.suspended` comment says "login and all API access blocked" (`auth.ts:51`).
7. **MCP token revocation.** Fixed 2026-09-18. Every OAuth token pair belongs to a grant (`mcpgrant:{email}:{id}`, index `mcpgrants:{email}`). `GET /api/mcp/grants` lists connected apps without tokens, `DELETE ?id=` or `?all=1` revokes; the MCP page shows the list. Refresh rotation deletes the previous access token. Password reset, founder reset and `wipeAccount` revoke the personal token and every grant. Since 2026-10-05 the grant is the authority: tokens are stored hashed, an access or refresh token is accepted only while its grant lists it, rotation takes the refresh record with GETDEL and rewrites the grant with SET XX, so a concurrent refresh, or a rotation racing a revoke, leaves no untracked token. Refresh tokens without a grant are refused (that client reconnects). A legacy OAuth access token (plain email value, before hashing) is not grant-checked; revocation still deletes it in its raw and hashed forms and it expires within 30 days (rules 548-555).
8. **MCP scope.** Partly fixed 2026-09-18: the approve route only writes scopes this server defines (`SCOPES`, today `noor`), and MCP `generate_content` applies the same trial-expiry check as `/api/generate`. On 2026-09-20 a token started resolving the active brand like a session, and `ask_noor` was put under the same per-account chat ceiling as the web route. Still open: there is one OAuth scope, so every token grants every MCP tool including `ask_noor` (which reaches `write_file`, `update_brand` and credit-spending tools; `publish_item` still needs an approved item).
9. **`POST /api/admin/impersonate` has no admin check.** It acts only on the caller's own cookies, so it cannot mint access. Since 2026-10-05 it writes the founder audit log only for a real impersonation session (record has `imp`), names that record's founder and account rather than any cookie value, and is rate limited per IP (rules 540-542), so a visitor can no longer flood `admin:log` and push the real rows out of its cap.
10. **Impersonation lifetime and audit.** The log says `expiresInMin:120` and the banner says "2 hours max", but the impersonated session is a normal 30-day session and is not deleted on return (`admin/users/[email]/route.ts:87-90`, `admin/impersonate/route.ts:11-14`). Actions performed while impersonating are attributed to the target user; `admin:log` never records which founder acted. Update 2026-09-29: the impersonation session now lasts 2 hours, is burned on return, carries the founder in `imp`, is refused the `IMPERSONATION_BLOCKED` actions, and `admin:log` records which founder acted (section 4). The line references above are to `4959f7e`. Update 2026-10-11 (rule 1177): the founder picks 15, 60 or 120 minutes and the banner counts down to that end instead of saying "2 hours max".
11. **Admin key handling.** `isAdmin` accepts the key in the URL query (`auth.ts:118`), where it can end up in logs and browser history, and every comparison now goes through `sameSecret` (`isAdminCookie` used `===` until 2026-09-20). The `nashr_admin` cookie stored the key itself until 2026-10-01; it is now a server-side key session (rules 420-424). Rate limiting applies to `/api/admin/login` only, not to guesses sent as `?key=` or `x-admin-key` to other admin routes. The same key doubles as the connector encryption fallback (`connectors/store.ts:23`), with a fixed development string as the last fallback. Update 2026-09-29: `adminActor` reads the key from the `x-admin-key` header (throttled) or the cookie only, never the query string; `auth.ts:118` above is the line at `4959f7e`. The connector fallback applies outside production only: production throws when `CONNECT_SECRET` is unset (`connectors/store.ts:22`, 2026-09-18).
12. **`GET /api/waitlist` uses its own check.** Fixed 2026-09-18: it compared a query-string key of its own instead of calling `isAdmin`; it now calls `isAdmin(req)` (`waitlist/route.ts:60`), so the `x-admin-key` header, the admin cookie and a founder signed in as themselves reach it, and the query string does not. The query string only chooses the CSV format.
13. **Publishing and approval.** Changed since the baseline: the chat tool `publish_item` (`chat.ts:708`) publishes only items the owner already approved (`:718`) and only to a target `targetsFor` lists (`:715`), and both it and `POST /api/publish/{id}` apply the brand gate (`chat.ts:726`, `publish/[id]/route.ts:34-35`). The HTTP route still publishes a pending item (the owner's click is the approval) but refuses a rejected one (`:29`) and a target `targetsFor` does not list (`:37`); afterwards both set the item `approved` through `updateTask` inside `publishOnce` (`src/lib/publish-once.ts`), which records `decidedAt` for a pending item. Whether the model publishes at all is still decided by the model on the owner's request; the approval it needs is checked in code. Since 2026-09-29 approving and publishing need admin or owner (`publish`) on the route, the inbox approval and the chat tool; a plain member can draft, edit and reject only. Security gates (2026-09-29): `publish_item` also needs an approval a person made (`decidedAt`, or an earlier publish by the owner; an item only auto-approval passed is published by the owner from the inbox, and an owner who rewrites an approved item gets `decidedAt` on the approve route), refuses every call that came over MCP (`ask_noor` runs `chat(..., { via: "mcp" })`), and both it and the route publish through `publishOnce` (one publish per item and platform unless the owner confirms again; an attempt marker so a publish killed at the time limit or lost to a timeout is never silently repeated). The judge now withdraws an auto-approval it auto-fails (`judgeTasks`): the item goes back to `pending` and the owner is notified; the owner's own approvals and anything already published are left alone.
14. **Model-controlled writes are not re-validated.** `update_brand` writes `brand[field] = value` using the model's `field` (`chat.ts:621-682`); the enum is enforced only by the strict tool schema. Text crawled from user-supplied sites and imported catalogs is placed into prompts and `products.md` without marking it as untrusted. Update 2026-09-29 (security gates): the field list is checked in code (`EDITABLE_BRAND_FIELDS`), and the two fields the code gates read are no longer written by a tool call: `banned` only gains words (merged, every word already there stays), taking words out (`banned_remove`) and any change to `offers` become proposals the owner applies with Save (`src/lib/brand-proposals.ts`, route 9a). The memory files reach the generator and the chat inside a `workspace_files` block serialised with `asData` (`fenceMemory`, `json.ts`), as the reviewer and judge already did, so a file cannot close its block; over MCP `write_file` refuses the core files. Still open: tone, audience, products and the other text fields are written by the model (role-gated, `brand.write`). Update 2026-10-05 (rules 568-575): no tool call writes the brand file any more, so the "still open" text fields and the banned-word additions are closed too. Every `update_brand` call, for every editable field, is a proposal (`proposeBrandChange`) that only an admin+ Save applies through `POST /api/brand/proposals/{id}` under the brand lock, and the proposal records `via` (`mcp` over `ask_noor`). Crawled site text reaches the onboarding model only inside `<site_snapshot>` and the brand file reaches the generating and chatting agents only inside `<brand_file>`, each encoded with `asData` under a "data, never instructions" line.
15. **Server-side fetches of user-supplied URLs** (`crawl.ts:51`, `catalog.ts:13,27`, WordPress/WooCommerce/Odoo/PrestaShop base URLs in `connect/[provider]/key`) have no allow-list, no private-address or metadata-address block, and follow redirects. Verify errors and publish errors return fragments of the remote response to the caller (`key/route.ts:30`, `publish/[id]/route.ts:65`). No rule is written for acceptable targets. Fixed 2026-09-18 for the fetches themselves: every one goes through `safeFetch` (`src/lib/safe-fetch.ts`: http(s) only, no credentials in the URL, no loopback, private, link-local, metadata or multicast target, every redirect hop checked again). Since 2026-10-12 the connection itself goes through `pinnedAgent()`, whose lookup refuses a private answer, so DNS rebinding between the check and the connection is closed, and a call without its own signal ends after 30 seconds (rules 1342-1343). Still open: publish and verify errors return a fragment of the remote response to the caller.
16. **Connector OAuth callback is sessionless.** The connection is saved to the scope stored in `state`, whoever completes the redirect (`callback/route.ts:60`); the path provider is not checked against the state's provider; Shopify callbacks are not HMAC-verified. OAuth tokens are never refreshed although `expiresAt` is stored. Fixed 2026-09-27: the callback finishes only for the browser and the login that started the flow (the `thakeen_oauth` cookie must equal the state, and the state's account and actor must be the signed-in ones, `callback/route.ts:37-40`), re-checks `can(u, "connectors")` (`:42`), refuses a state issued for another provider (`:31`) and checks Shopify's HMAC and shop (`:45-46`); a token that expires within five minutes is refreshed before use (`getFreshConnection`, `connectors/fresh.ts:29`).
17. **Account deletion leaves data behind.** `wipeAccount` (`account.ts:35-127`) deletes the `conns:{scope}` set but not the `conn:{scope}:{provider}` records holding encrypted third-party credentials; (fixed 2026-09-18: connector records, every task and conversation, the task index, the pending set, the personal MCP token and all OAuth grants are deleted); it still leaves `session:*` (harmless: the user record is gone),  `notifs:`, `notified:`, `notifseen:`, `usage:`, `uev:`/`ulast:`/`ufirst0:`, `fb:`/`fblog:` (fixed 2026-09-21: `fblog:{scope}` for every brand and the legacy `fb:` counters are now deleted — the log holds the free-text reason the owner typed; `decisions:{scope}`, added 2026-09-29, is deleted for every brand for the same reason; the `usage:` counters of the last 36 months are deleted from the list `usageMonthKeys` builds from `KINDS` and `AGENT_IDS`, so the per-agent counters added 2026-09-29 go too), `ref:`, `referrals:`, `referredBy:`, `shared:`, OAuth tokens, membership in `users:all` and `active:*` sets, tickets, and for non-default brands the `profile:{scope}` key. A `deleted-accounts` entry keeps the email. No retention rule is documented in code. Media (2026-09-29, spec A19): generated images and clips on Vercel Blob, and images still hot-linked at fal.ai, survive the wipe (public addresses that cannot be guessed, no tenant identifier in the path); the render records expire by TTL within 14 days (`mjob:` and `mjobref:` 7 days, `mtrialvid:` 14 days, the flight locks and per-account render limits within a day), and the global `render:_all:*` counters and `mjobs:recent` carry no email. A job of a wiped account still running is failed without a refund or a notification (`accountSince`), so `notifs:{email}` is not recreated. The privacy policy and the help centre state all of this; deleting the Blob files and job records with the account is a follow-up. Since 2026-09-29 `wipeAccount` also deletes the team (`wipeMemberships`): every member login and its `activebrand:`, every invite, `members:{owner}` and `invites:{owner}`, so a member login never survives to join a new account registered at the owner's address. Since 2026-09-30 it also deletes the brand proposals an agent made (`brandprop:`, `brandprop-done:`, `brandprop-claim:` through the `brandprops:{scope}` index, and `brandlock:{scope}`), which hold the brand's old and proposed offers or banned words for 7 days; the publish markers `pubtry:` (a time, 3 days) and `publock:` (a lock, at most 330 s) are left to their TTL. Since 2026-10-01 it deletes the chat allowance's counters too: the monthly `usage:{email}:{YYYY-MM}:chat_msgs`, `:chat_over` and `:chat_cred` of the last 36 months through `usageMonthKeys`, and the trial's `chatm:{email}:trial` (`chatTrialKey`); the `User` fields `chatAllowance`, `chatOverflow` and `chatCarryMicro` go with `user:{email}` (rule 314).
18. **Whole-record user saves outside the credit lock.** Fixed 2026-09-20. `PUT /api/profile`, `PATCH /api/account` and `POST /api/auth/reset` called `saveUser` with the record read at request start, so a concurrent `charge` or `refund` could be overwritten. They now write the owner record inside `withUserLock` on the record re-read there (`profile/route.ts:145`, `account/route.ts:57`, `reset/route.ts:64`), which also keeps the per-agent counters (`agentSpend`, 2026-09-29) a charge wrote, and a member's fields go to its own record under `withMemberLock`. The once-only flags are single atomic steps as well: `POST /api/referral/share` claims a platform with `SADD` (`share/route.ts:26`) and `once()` with SET NX (`notify.ts:74`), both since 2026-09-20, and `pushNotif` fingerprints with one SET NX claim since 2026-09-29 (`db.setNx`, Reem section item 8).
19. **Brand-scope inconsistencies inside one account.** Pages fixed 2026-09-18: files, governance, tasks, generate, onboarding, and `listConnections` in chat and reports now read `sc(u)`; `tests/scope-usage.test.ts` fails when a page or customer API route passes the bare account email to a workspace reader. Still open: email preferences are written to `profile:{sc(u)}` and cron reads the scope first and the account second; the founder overview counts credits from the default brand only.
20. **Credits and plan limits not tied to a payment event.** Plans and credits change only through the founder console. Free tools cost signed-in users no credits, capped at 50 calls per account per 24 hours since 2026-09-20 (`SIGNED_IN_PER_DAY`, `tools/[tool]/route.ts:13,25-26`); catalog import is uncharged and repeatable, and the first brand's onboarding is uncharged, once per workspace and at most three runs a day (`flows.md` 6); an added brand's onboarding costs 9 credits since 2026-09-30. Since 2026-10-01 chat is included up to the plan's monthly messages and past them costs credits only when the owner turned "continue on credits" on; the founder's chat override (`User.chatAllowance`) is, like an Agency deal, typed in the console and tied to no payment. (Trial expiry has been checked on every image and video path since 2026-09-29, through `renderImage` / `renderVideo` in `src/lib/media/start.ts`.) Since 2026-10-12 active brands are capped per plan (rules 1390-1391; creation is also rate-limited), and a downgrade still leaves every brand in place (it only stops new adds and restores), and each one keeps its own Sunday plan and routines. An Agency price is a deal the founder types in the console: nothing ties it to what the customer actually pays. Whether these are intended is not stated.
21. **Layout guard depends on a header.** The layout redirect runs only when `x-pathname` is non-empty (`layout.tsx:28`) and public paths are matched with `endsWith`. Every current page has its own guard except `reset` (public by design), so this is defence in depth only; the rule for future pages is unwritten.
22. **`next` redirect parameter.** Fixed 2026-10-05 (rules 543-544): login and signup take `next` only through `safeNext` (`src/lib/safe-next.ts`), which refuses control characters (browsers drop tab, CR and LF, so `/\t/evil.com` became `//evil.com`), any backslash, anything not starting with a single `/`, and anything that does not resolve to the same origin. Markdown links use the same rule (`safeHref`, rule 545). Since 2026-10-08 (rule 694) `safeNext` answers the normalised path, query and fragment, not the raw string, and refuses a value whose dot segments resolve to `//host` (`/.//evil.example`, `/a/..//evil.example`, `/%2e%2e//evil.example`); `%2F` is not decoded, so an encoded `//` stays a path on this site; `safeHref` checks the href after decoding its HTML entities, so the link a browser follows is the one checked. No server-side rule exists for post-login destinations (the redirect is client-side).
23. **No CSRF token; a cross-site check instead (2026-10-08, rules 690-693).** `sameSite=lax` alone left three holes: routes call `req.json()` without checking `Content-Type`, so a cross-site `<form enctype="text/plain">` could deliver JSON; login and logout need no cookie to be abused; and a sibling under the same registrable domain, such as `staging.thakeen.com`, is same site, so Lax sends the cookie on its posts. `src/proxy.ts` now runs `crossSiteRefusal` (`src/lib/csrf.ts`) before every `/api` handler. Only POST, PUT, PATCH and DELETE are checked. `Sec-Fetch-Site`, when present, decides alone: `same-origin` or `none` passes, `same-site`, `cross-site` and any other value are refused, and pages cannot set any `Sec-` header. `Origin` is not compared as well (the order of Go 1.25's `CrossOriginProtection`): a browser never pairs `same-origin` with another site's `Origin`, a client that forges the pair holds no victim's cookie, and the comparison would refuse every login on a server that cannot see its public name (Hostinger without APP_ORIGIN behind a proxy that rewrites `Host`). Without `Sec-Fetch-Site` (an older browser), `Origin`, when present, must be `appOrigin()` or the request's own origin (the first `X-Forwarded-Host` value or `Host`, with `https` always accepted on that host, plain `http` only when the server sees no TLS), and `null` is refused. A refusal is 403 `{"error":"cross_site"}`, `no-store`. A request with neither header passes: only browsers send them, and the callers that send neither (crons with the CRON_SECRET bearer, QStash, webhooks, MCP and OAuth clients, curl) hold no browser cookie, so they can do only what their own credentials allow. The two form posts of our own pages, `POST /api/oauth/approve` (the consent form on `/oauth/authorize`) and `POST /api/admin/impersonate`, are same-origin and pass. Exempt paths (`CROSS_SITE_EXEMPT`; the proxy still runs on them, because the staging lock must see every path, so Next buffers their bodies up to 6 MB, rule 774, before the routes' 64 KB caps refuse a larger one): `/api/mcp` (exact; bearer only, CORS `*`, browser-based MCP clients post from their own origin), `/api/oauth/token` and `/api/oauth/register` (exact; public or client-authenticated, CORS `*`), `/api/media/hook/` (provider callbacks, only a hint re-checked with the provider), `/api/secretary/booking/` (Cal.com and Calendly webhooks proven by signature), `/api/billing/paymob/` (the Paymob webhook, proven by Paymob's HMAC) and `/api/cron/` (the cron bearer and QStash). The cookie routes beside them (`/api/mcp/token`, `/api/mcp/grants`, `/api/oauth/approve`, `/api/secretary/booking`) stay checked. `tests/csrf.test.ts` walks the import graph of every exempt route that changes state and fails if any reaches the session or a cookie, and checks that every route granting a CORS preflight is exempt or changes nothing, so a cross-site page cannot set `X-Forwarded-Host` on a checked route. MCP and OAuth endpoints still send `Access-Control-Allow-Origin: *` (bearer or public by design).
24. **Anonymous counters without expiry.** Fixed 2026-09-18 for the free tools: the limiter is `allow("tool:{ip}")` with a 24 h window and the platform IP. The in-memory store honours TTL since 2026-09-18. Since 2026-09-29 every `allow()` window is set with EXPIRE NX on every hit (`rate.ts:13`, `db.expireNx`): before, only the first hit set the TTL with a SET, which reset hits landing in between, and when that one call failed the counter kept no expiry and refused every caller for good (for `rate:kie:create`, every tenant). A failed expiry call is logged and repaired by the next hit; a store that refuses the NX form falls back to the first hit's SET (`:18`).
25. **Email normalisation on founder actions.** Fixed 2026-09-18: `POST /api/admin/users/{email}` used the raw decoded path value for `withUserLock` and `wipeAccount` while the lookup was normalized, so a mixed-case path could wipe different keys while the log still recorded `delete`. It now normalizes once (`admin/users/[email]/route.ts:66`) and uses that key for the lookup, the locks and `wipeAccount` (`:125`).
26. **Pages that still offer what the route refuses (teams, 2026-09-29).** Fixed 2026-09-29: the inbox and chat cards offer a plain member no Approve or Publish (an edit is saved without approving) and say who approves; the WhatsApp card is the owner's only; the files page hides catalog import and core-file edit and delete from members; reports hide the CSV import and removal; the start page tells a member who sets the brand up; every refused call on these screens says the role does not allow it, and none is shown as a success. The profile page hides the password and delete forms from a founder viewing the account.
27. **Core files can be overwritten by any member.** Fixed 2026-09-29: writing a core file needs admin+ (`files.core.write`) in `PUT /api/files` and in the chat's `write_file`, as deleting one does, and replacing imported performance data needs admin+ (`performance.import`) as deleting it does. A member still writes any other file, and the reason a member types when editing or rejecting is appended to `preferences.md` by the tasks route. That is the member's own text inside a fixed sentence ("Owner rejected a {type} item ({channel}). Reason: …"), so since 2026-10-05 it is capped in code (`cleanNote`, since 2026-10-20 in `src/lib/task-notes.ts` and shared with the line notes of `POST /api/tasks/{id}/revise`, rule 2491): a string only, one line with control and direction characters removed, at most 300 characters; the append runs under the `prefsfile:{scope}` lock (rules 565-567).
28. **Signup does not see a pending invite.** No invite-by-email index exists, so an address with only a pending invite can sign up as an owner; the later accept then answers 409 `unavailable` under `lock:login:{email}`.
29. **An owner reset does not remove invited logins.** An owner reset signs every member out (`teamSessionsAfter`), but a member login invited by someone who held the old password stays, and can sign back in with its own password. Nothing prompts the owner to review the Members page after a reset.
30. **No attribution to the acting login.** Tasks, decision records, publishes, brand edits and chat history carry no actor; the account's notification bell and its read state are shared by everyone in it; the chat ceiling is per account. The design lists per-actor attribution as later work. Update (step 3 of the agent-team upgrade, merged after): decision records, governance revisions and the activity log carry `by`, the acting login (a member's own address and role, a founder's `imp`, a token or a system job; section 4 item 10), so which login approved, rejected, edited, published, generated or changed governance is kept there. The task and brand records themselves and chat history still carry none.
31. **Cron paths carry no actor.** `allUsers()` returns users without `actor`, so any `can()` check added to a cron-driven path refuses (fail closed). Routines and the weekly plan go through `generateBatch` (`via` "routine" / "weekly"), which has no role check: a cron or routine run records `systemBy(...)` (`systemBy("routine")`, `systemBy("cron")`; `byOf` gives a user with no actor `{kind: "system", job: "cron"}`), while the weekly plan's "Run now" (POST /api/schedule `{run: "weekly"}`) records the login that pressed it. `generateBatch` is also the path of `/api/generate`, chat and MCP, so a `can()` check added inside it would refuse every scheduled run as well as gate those.
32. **Plan changes outside the console.** Only `POST /api/admin/plan` changes a plan or seat count and it runs `enforceSeats`; any future billing webhook or checkout must call it too, or members above the new limit keep working until the next console change.
33. **The chat still offers refused tools.** A plain member's chat shows the model `update_brand`, `publish_item` and `import_catalog`; `runTool` refuses them in code and the model relays the sentence. An admin or owner's `update_brand` also only proposes (rule 568). Filtering the tool list by role would change the cached prompt prefix per role.
34. **Hand-passed invite links prove nothing about the address.** When mail is not set up (`mailReady()` false, the case in production until `MAIL_FROM` is set), the invite link is returned to the inviter, who can accept it themselves and hold a member login at an address they do not control; signup at that address then answers `exists`. With mail set up the link reaches only the invited address (a failed send withdraws the invite). Signup itself does not verify an address either, so an unregistered address can be taken either way; the founder console can now look a member login up by address and remove it (`removeMember`), as it can delete an owner account.
35. **A paused member comes back only on a limit change.** `enforceSeats` brings paused members back oldest first when the limit grows; removing someone frees a seat for a new invite but does not resume a paused member, and the owner has no resume action. Removing the paused member and inviting the address again works.
36. **Media webhooks are hints, and only kie's can be signed.** Higgsfield does not sign its webhooks, and kie's callbacks are checked (`verifyKieSignature`, `X-Webhook-Timestamp` within ±300 s, `X-Webhook-Signature` compared with `sameSecret`) only when `KIE_WEBHOOK_HMAC_KEY` is set; without it a kie callback is accepted unsigned. Both are therefore treated as hints: the body's only use is the provider task id, which selects a job through a pointer the server wrote, and the job then asks the provider itself, with our key, under the same 4 s throttle and job lock as a poll. A forged hint costs at most that one throttled status call per job per 4 s and cannot write outside the job's own scope; it cannot deliver a result, because result URLs in the body are never read. Anyone may still spend the per-IP allowance (240 per minute per provider and IP). Whether the founder will set `KIE_WEBHOOK_HMAC_KEY` is an owner step (`docs/Owner_Tasks_AR.md`, section ج2).
37. **Concurrent writes to one task.** Fixed 2026-09-29. `saveTask` replaces the whole task, and the approve and edit route, the publish route, the chat tool `publish_item`, the judge and the media engine each used to save a copy read before their slow work, so one could erase another's change: a publish or an approval undid a paid render that finished meanwhile, and a finalize undid an approval. Each now changes the task through `updateTask` (`src/lib/brand.ts:357`): re-read under `tasklock:{scope}:{id}` (10 s), apply only its own fields, save; a deleted task is not recreated. `tasklock` bounds a writer's wait to 3 s (`task_lock_timeout`) and, like the ledger lock, is released with an unconditional DEL. Whole-record USER saves outside the credit lock (item 18) are unchanged.
38. **Who changed governance is kept until the account is wiped.** Each revision in `govhist:{scope}` stores `by` with an email (the owner's, or a member's own login email; section 4 item 10) and whole before and after settings. Removing a member, or a member leaving, does not remove their email from past revisions or decisions; `wipeAccount` deletes `govhist:{s}` and `govlock:{s}` for every brand. Whether a member's email should be dropped from history when they leave is not decided.
39. **Support access can change and restore governance.** `governance.write` is not in `IMPERSONATION_BLOCKED` (section 0.1 item 4), so a founder impersonating an account can save or restore its governance (rows 18 and 104), including a restore that raises autonomy (turns auto-approve back on, removes caps). It is recorded as the founder, with the founder's email or `"key"` in `imp` (section 4 item 10), not refused; the account's logins see it as "Thakeen support", without `imp`. Whether restores or autonomy-raising changes belong in `IMPERSONATION_BLOCKED` is not decided.
40. **The activity log keeps member emails and free text until the account is wiped.** Each row of `activity:{scope}` stores `by` with an email (the owner's, or a member's own login email) and a note of up to 200 characters: an item's title, a file or brand field name, the changed governance settings, or the title an outside app gave a draft. Removing a member will not remove their email from past rows; `wipeAccount` deletes `activity:{s}` and the routines' `actskip:` markers for every brand, and the log is trimmed to its newest 2000 rows per brand. The notes are rendered as text and escaped in the CSV, never fed back into a prompt. The log is not an MCP tool: a bearer token cannot read or export it. Reading and exporting it is `read` (every login, rows 105 and 106), so a plain member sees which login did what, the other logins' addresses included (never the founder's: `imp` is dropped on the way out, section 4 item 10); whether a plain member should see them is not decided.

Approving and publishing are bound to the content the approver saw (2026-10-05, rules 560-564): the request carries `version` (`taskVersion`, `src/lib/task-version.ts`), checked under the task lock and the publish lock. A member's edit made after an admin opened the card can no longer be approved or published under the admin's name. New media (image or video) delivered to an approved item that no person has published clears its `decidedAt`, so chat `publish_item` refuses it until a person approves again (rule 576).

## Brand rules (2026-09-28)

The owner's banned words are enforced in code, not only in prompts (`src/lib/brand-rules.ts`). A customer-facing item whose text contains one cannot become `approved` through `PATCH /api/tasks/{id}` (409 `brand_violation`), cannot be published through `POST /api/publish/{id}` (409) or the chat tool `publish_item` (refused), and is created `pending` even when its type is auto-approved. Any login of the account can still edit it or save it as pending (`drafts`), and the owner or an admin can remove the word from the brand file (`brand.write`); nothing else lifts the rule.

## Drafts from the owner's own assistant (2026-09-29)

MCP `save_draft` lets a bearer (the owner's Claude, ChatGPT or any MCP client, through a personal token or an OAuth grant; tokens resolve to owner accounts only, section 0) put text into the approval inbox of its own active brand. Its role in `MCP_TOOL_ACTION`: see `tests/role-gates.test.ts`. It can create, never decide: the item is always `pending` whatever `gov.autoApprove` says, the brand gate applies to it like to any agent's draft, and deciding stays with signed-in logins in the app: approving and publishing with the owner or an admin (`publish`), editing and rejecting with any login of the account (`drafts`). It spends no credits, so its only bound is `draftAllowed` (30 an hour, 150 a day per account). The text is untrusted input: typed and bounded by `parseDraft`, rendered in the inbox like model output (`render`), never executed or followed.

## Reem, the secretary (2026-09-29)

Role (founder, 2026-10-05): Reem works for one Thakeen customer, the account owner, within the Thakeen contract and only on the marketing work that serves the brand; she follows the team without assigning it work on her own (work for the team is the owner's decision) or planning its time, and declines what is outside that scope. That scope is held by her persona (`src/lib/agents.ts`) and playbook (`content/skills/secretary.md`, the Role and scope principle), which is a prompt, not code; what code enforces is the data boundary below (rules 609-611).

Phase 1 of the secretary agent keeps the owner's own to-dos, follow-ups and "who owes what" per brand scope (`src/lib/secretary.ts`, spec `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-29-secretary-reem-phase1.md`). What code enforces, not prompts:

1. Scope. Every key is built from `sc(u)` (the session's or the bearer's active brand); an item id from a request is format-checked and resolved under that scope only (rows 101-103, section 3.2).
2. Only the owner closes. `complete` (a written result is required), `confirm_done`, `reject_done`, `delete` and `restore` are `OWNER_ONLY`: `changeItem` and `mutateItem` refuse them unless the actor is the owner through the web route. Chat and MCP tools can create, update, snooze, add notes and propose "done" with evidence; a proposal ends any snooze and waits under "needs your confirmation". The one other close is deterministic: an item linked to an inbox task of the same scope closes with the result "linked item approved" when that task is approved (`closeLinked`, re-checked under the item lock). Because a link can close an item, only the owner changes a link on an existing item; chat and MCP set one only at creation and only to a `pending` task, so the close always follows an approval the owner made after the link (tests rule 192).
3. Concurrency. Writes to one item go through `sec:lock:{scope}:{id}` and bump `rev`; a request that sends an older `rev` gets 409.
4. Limits. 200 new items a day and 600 changes an hour per account (across brands), 600 reads an hour, 500 open items per brand, counted on create and on a restore that reopens an item.
5. Untrusted text. Titles, notes and evidence are cleaned and bounded; the chat tool answers quote them as JSON under a line that says they are data; private notes never reach a model (`itemLine` leaves them out of `agenda_get`, in chat and over MCP), and only the owner on the web can write one (a note from chat or MCP is stored shared). An agent's "done" (`proposedDone.evidence`) is the agent's claim: the page shows it attributed and marked as not checked, next to the fact code read from the stored linked task (`linkFactText` over `facts`: status, `decidedAt`, publish receipt) or "Nothing in the app confirms this"; the evidence text never becomes the checked fact.
6. Deletion. A delete is soft for 30 days; the daily cron purges after that in every brand scope, archived brands and suspended accounts included (`purgeIdle` over `dailyRoster().idle`); `wipeAccount` deletes every `sec:*` key of every brand scope (`secretaryKeys`).
7. No new vendor, no new environment variable, no WhatsApp or Telegram delivery, no time-based reminder job: reminders reach the owner through the page, the 8 AM brief (and its email, per the owner's preference) and the in-app overdue alerts (item 8). Nothing fires at an item's due time, and the copy (Reem's prompt and playbook, the chat chips, help and the site) says so instead of promising it.
8. Overdue alerts. One per item that fell behind on the local day it did and 1, 3 and 7 days later, then only the brief; each step's marker (`notifseen:{email}:todo-due:{scope}:{id}:{anchor ms}:{step}`, the due time for the owner's own item, the chase moment's anchor for what someone else owes since 2026-10-03) is claimed with SET NX before anything is pushed, at most 5 a brand a local day (`sec:alerts:{scope}:{day}`), claims past the allowance handed back. Since 2026-10-03 a contact's steps count working days and none is sent on a quiet day (item 21). Every other follow-up's marker is claimed the same way (`pushNotif`), so two overlapping cron runs notify once.
9. Time zone. A brand's zone is one of six allow-listed Gulf zones (`zones.ts`), stored only through `PUT /api/brand` (row 9) and re-checked on every read (`zoneOf`); `update_brand` cannot set it. It decides local days, the reading and writing of dates, the snooze presets, the country whose holidays apply (item 20) and the default weekend the chase clock skips.
10. Chase clock. A they-owe item is due for a follow-up at its due time, else at `expectBy` (two working days after creation by default), else two working days after the last recorded follow-up; recording one (`chased`, the owner's action or `todo_update {chased: true}`) never closes the item. Since 2026-10-03 the working days are the brand's, and the chase waits out quiet days, Ramadan's off-hours and a contact's absence (item 21).
11. Team roles (after main's teams change was merged, 2026-09-29). The list is the account's, per brand, shared by its logins. Any member adds, updates, snoozes, reschedules, notes and records follow-ups (`todo.write`, member); completing, confirming or sending back an agent's claim, deleting and restoring need the owner or an admin (`todo.close`, admin+; the route refuses with 403, the page hides the buttons). "Owner" in Reem's audit and in `OWNER_ONLY` means a person in the app rather than an agent; `login` in each audit line says which person. MCP tools map to `read` and `todo.write` (`MCP_TOOL_ACTION`); the chat tools are member-level, and none of them closes an item whatever the role.

Phase 2 (2026-09-30, spec `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-30-secretary-reem-phase2.md`; item 7 above no longer holds for reminders: there is now a time-based job, and still no WhatsApp or Telegram delivery):

12. Reminders. `remindAt` is set by the owner in the app or by an agent (`remind_at`), in the future, in the brand's zone (a date alone is 08:00 local). Only `indexWrites` writes `sec:due:{scope}` and `sec:duescopes`; the sweep never removes a due id: it leases it (`SET NX sec:firing:{scope}:{id}`, 330 s), so one run at a time works on it, and fires only an open item whose time came and is still the instant it read, re-read under its lock. Delivery comes before the item's write: the notification (fingerprint `todo-remind:{scope}:{id}:{remindAt ms}`), then the email only when that push was new and the preference is not `none`, then the item; a failure or a killed run leaves the id due for the next sweep, and neither the notification nor the email repeats (late, never lost). One scope's or account's failure never stops another's delivery, and no account starts after the sweep's deadline. A suspended account's reminders, and an archived brand's, wait. Every read-then-write of the bell holds the account's bell lock, so concurrent writers never drop each other's entries.
13. The tick. `POST /api/cron/secretary` refuses oversized input first, then runs for a signed QStash call or the CRON_SECRET bearer; only failed calls count against the per-IP limit, so a verified call is never refused by it. The scopes it serves come from the store, never from the request. The two QStash keys are secrets (`docs/system/variables.md`), compared in constant time, never logged. Tool answers and the page promise a reminder "at" its time only while a QStash call is under an hour old (`timedReminders`); otherwise with the next morning brief, at its local time in the brand's zone (08:00 or 09:00, `briefClock`), when the daily cron's fallback sweep fires it.
14. Reopen is `OWNER_ONLY` (admin+, `todo.close`) and counts against the open cap; chat and MCP cannot reopen. A reopen drops the item's inbox link, so the linked close cannot undo it.
15. A contact's phone number is written by the owner in the app only (`owner_only` from chat, MCP or an agent; the tools have no argument for it), only on a they-owe item, in the international form (8 to 15 digits, no leading zero, after Arabic digits, punctuation and a leading 00 are cleaned); a change of who owes the item drops it, whoever makes the change, unless the owner gives the new party's number in the same change; it never reaches a tool answer, a prompt, the walk card or the audit line. The page's wa.me link opens a fixed message the owner sends; the app sends nothing.
16. Walk. A card lists only the actions the viewer may take, and never Approve on a draft the brand guard holds; its buttons call the existing routes, which keep their gates (a member's approve would still be 403 at the task PATCH, a held draft 409 `brand_violation`). A confirm card shows the agent's claim as a claim next to what the app checked, and an approval card the draft's opening, so nothing is confirmed or approved unseen.
17. Evening wrap. Built from stored facts after the agenda closed approved linked items, sent at most once per scope and local day (SET NX marker, released only when the in-app notification could not be written), only when something happened or is due; email only with preference `all`; off when follow-ups are off (the switch's text says so).
18. Agents' handoff notes («ملاحظات التسليم», the chat tool `handoff_note`; not Majed's handoffs, section "Handoffs"). Chat only (refused over MCP); the sender is the turn's agent; bodies are bounded (500), cleaned, stored per scope (30) and shown to the receiver as `asData` inside `<handoff_notes>`, labelled as data. A blocked note opens at most one item per text that says the same thing and agent (a second one becomes a note on it; different requests in the same frame, and one-word bodies that differ, stay apart), at most 20 a day per account; to the owner it is due today; its item is a Reem item like any other, which only the owner closes.
19. Account deletion also removes `sec:due`, `sec:walk`, `sec:wrap` markers and `notes:{scope}` for every brand scope, and the scopes' members of `sec:duescopes`.

Phase 3 A1 (2026-10-03, spec `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-02-secretary-reem-phase3a-design.md` sections 1-3, `flows.md` 39; rows 132-137 and section 3.2): the brand's work calendar, one chase rule and contacts who are away. What code enforces, not prompts:

20. Working days. Stored per brand in `sec:cal:{sc(u)}`, written only by `PUT /api/secretary/calendar` (admin+, `brand.write`; 30 saves an hour; under `withLock(scope, "cal")`; one `calendar` audit line naming the login) after `parseCalendar` checks every field by name (an unknown field such as `scope` or `email` is refused, not ignored). Members read them (`read`), on the page and through GET; a member's PUT is 403 with nothing written. No chat or MCP tool changes them (`update_brand` does not touch them). The country's holidays are code (`COUNTRY_CAL`), never a request's or a model's; a stored value the calendar cannot be built from gives the defaults, so a bad record never stops the agenda or a chat turn.
21. Quiet days pause only the chasing of contacts. The owner's own items, the reminders the owner set and what an agent owes keep their dates and their alerts (an agent's count calendar days, any day). For a contact's debt, the chase moment and the alert steps are computed in code from the brand's calendar and away map (`chaseMoment`, `overdueAlerts`): working days, nothing sent on a weekend, Eid, official or added holiday, nor while the contact is away; a step that only holds keeps its marker alive (7 days), so it is never sent twice.
22. Away contacts. `sec:away:{sc(u)}`, one field per contact (`partyKey`), written by any member with `todo.write` through `PUT` / `DELETE /api/secretary/away` (`sec:write`; under `withLock(scope, "away")`; one `away` or `away_end` audit line naming the login, never the note). Only a contact can be away: the owner, an owner alias or an agent id is `not_contact` however it is written. At most 200 fields with ended ones, a last day from today to today + 366 in the brand's zone, a note of 200 characters. Ending is idempotent and keeps the entry for the back alert; only `remove: true` deletes it; the daily clean-up removes entries ended over 30 days ago.
23. Reem only proposes an absence. `contact_away` writes nothing to the away list: it saves a 7-day proposal shown as a card, and the absence is saved only when a person with `todo.write` presses Save (`POST /api/secretary/away/proposals/{id}`), once, through every check of `PUT` / `DELETE` again, with an audit line naming that login. The tool is offered only in Reem's chat turns and refused in `runTool` for any other agent, a turn with no agent, MCP and a token; MCP never lists it. Text from a customer, a supplier, a connector or a file can at most lead Reem to propose a card, which a person must save.
24. Account deletion also removes `sec:cal`, `sec:away`, and every away proposal's record and outcome (found through `secawayprops:{scope}`, since their ids are random) for every brand scope.

Phase 3 A2 (2026-10-03, spec `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-02-secretary-reem-phase3a-design.md` sections 4-6, rulings R32-R54 of the plan):

25. Repeating items (rules 490-501). A rule is set by the owner in the app (`PATCH /api/secretary/{id}` with `repeat`), by Reem in chat or by the owner's own MCP assistant (`repeat` on `todo_create` and `todo_update`). `runSecretaryTool` refuses `repeat` and `stop_repeat` from any other agent (`ask_noor` included) and from a chat call with no agent, before `sec:write` is spent, with nothing saved; this is code, not the prompt. Every value is checked by `parseRepeat` (400 `repeat_invalid`); a repeating item keeps a due date (400 `repeat_needs_due`). A repeat never ends or deletes anything on its own authority: stopping one (`repeat: null`, `stop_repeat`), ending it with a `count` not above the copies made or an `until` before the next date, and skipping one copy (`skip`, admin+, `todo.close`) are the owner's in the app only (`owner_only`, 403, field `repeat`, also for Reem and MCP). Text Reem reads (a note, a handoff) is untrusted: a handoff note naming a `repeat` creates an item with none.
26. The next copy (rules 497-499). Every close (complete, confirm, skip, the linked close when its inbox item is approved) opens the next copy through `spawnNext` under the closed copy's item lock; a claim `sec:spawn:{sc(u)}:{id}` (SET NX, 2 days) makes a retried close open one copy, and the new copy is written only when no record is there. Every key is built from `sc(u)`; the copy carries no links, notes, result or proposal, and a spawn is not a create (no open-cap check, no create allowance). Reopening a copy deletes its next copy only while untouched (`deleteNextCopy`, audit `series_reopen`, actor `rule`), under that copy's own lock, and answers 503 `lock_timeout` with nothing written when it cannot decide; `OWNER_ONLY` is not widened.
27. Closed copies are deleted after a warning (rules 502-505, 508). A recurring series' closed copies are warned about by a notification and an email unless the owner's email preference is none, then deleted 90 days after their close and never sooner than 7 days after a warning that named their series. The daily step deletes only copies in `sec:spurge`, re-checking under the item's lock that the copy is still closed, its series is not kept, and 90 days have passed; a brand that is suspended, archived or missing runs nothing. The owner keeps a series (row 138) so none of its copies is ever warned about or deleted; releasing it is admin+ only. A kept series is limited to 500 per brand.
28. Account deletion also removes `sec:series`, `sec:serieslist`, `sec:sdone`, `sec:spurge`, `sec:serieskeep`, `sec:swarn` and every `sec:spawn` claim (`secretaryKeys`).

## Security gates from the Taskuary study (2026-09-29)

Found by comparing Taskuary's send path with ours (study: private artifact of 2026-09-29), each checked in code with no model in the loop and pinned in `tests/security-gates.test.ts` (rules 213-220 of `tests.md`):

1. Unfilled placeholders. `gateTask` (`brand-rules.ts`) holds a customer-facing item whose body (and, for an article, whose published title; no screen edits a title, and cart recovery drops an unoffered percentage from a title instead of writing `[رقم]` there) has an unfilled placeholder (`[رقم]`, `[date]`, `[دليل مطلوب / proof needed]`, also with a note in parentheses; brackets are read as placeholders, a markdown link to an address, a checkbox, a footnote and, at approval, a script's direction label such as `[VO]`, `[VISUAL: …]`, `[Scene 1: …]` or `[لقطة قريبة]` are not, unless the label names a value; at publish direction labels hold too) at approval, auto-approval (every creation site passes the item's channel) and publish. A personalisation slot (`{{1}}`, `[الاسم]`, `[name]`) passes at approval only on a WhatsApp, email, SMS or call-script draft; at publish (`gateTask(..., { publish: true })`) every placeholder holds the item, and the inbox's publish buttons follow that stricter check. The inbox, MCP `check_brand` (which also reports `publishable`) and `save_draft` run the same function with the channel.
2. Chat publishes only what a person approved (`decidedAt`, set by an approval, by an owner's rewrite of an approved item, or an earlier owner publish), and nothing over MCP. Since rule 3012 an image or clip that landed after that approval or publish (`mediaAt`) needs a newer one (`personSawCurrent`, src/lib/task-version.ts), and the copy sent must still be the checked `taskVersion`.
3. Brand gate inputs. Since 2026-10-05 (rule 568) an agent writes no brand field: adding banned words (up to the 400 characters the Brand page keeps), taking one out (`banned_remove`), changing the offers and every other editable field are proposals (`brandprop:{scope}:{id}`, 7 days) that only admin+ (`brand.write`) applies through `POST /api/brand/proposals/{id}`, once. An offers change applies only while the offers still hold the value it was made against; a removal takes its words out of the list as it is then, and an addition puts its words into it as it is then. Agent writes and applied proposals run under `brandlock:{scope}`; a reloaded chat shows each card's outcome (`brandprop-done:{scope}:{id}`); a plain member sees the card without Save; a card from MCP says so. Deleting the account deletes every proposal of every brand, its outcome and claim (found through `brandprops:{scope}`, since the ids are random), so the old and proposed offers or banned words do not outlive it (rule 240).
4. One publish per item and platform (`publishOnce`): a lock held a little longer than the caller may run (70 s from the publish route, 330 s from chat and MCP), the caller's checks again on the copy that is sent, an attempt marker kept unless the platform clearly refused (the connector's own leading 4xx status, a refused token, a check before sending; never a number inside the platform's message), a refusal of a repeat, and the owner's `again` to publish anyway. X and Telegram sends time out at 45 s, inside the route's limit, so a slow platform is answered as "result unknown" rather than killed. A duplicate refusal is its own answer (`duplicate_post`), never a disconnected account, and after an unknown attempt it records that attempt as live (`earlier_live`).
5. The judge's automatic fail withdraws an auto-approval, never an owner's approval, never a published item.
6. Memory files are fenced data in every generation and chat prompt; `preferences.md` shows its newest notes; core memory files are not written over MCP, and the catalog import (which rewrites `products.md`) is not run over MCP.

## Handoffs (2026-09-30)

Majed, the lead, proposes work for the agents who own it, and a login of the account runs it (`src/lib/handoffs.ts`, `flows.md` 34). These are Majed's handoffs (`propose_handoff`, rows 107-109), not the agents' handoff notes (`handoff_note`, item 18 of the Reem section), which run nothing. What code enforces, not prompts:

1. Who proposes. Only a chat turn with Majed: the tool is offered in his turns only (`toolsFor`) and `runTool` refuses it to any other agent, a turn with no agent, a token or a turn over MCP (`ctx.via`, `ask_noor`) and a second proposal in one reply. No MCP tool proposes, runs, declines or lists handoffs (`MCP_TOOL_ACTION` and `tools/list` name none, and `ask_noor` talks to Noor). Proposing is free.
2. What the model sets. Only each item's type (from `HANDOFF_TYPES`) and brief, and `why`. The agent (`agentForTask`), the cost (`TASK_COST`), the status, the scope (`sc(u)` of the turn), `requestedBy` (the turn's `by`) and the origin (`web_chat`) are set in code.
3. No bypass. In Majed's turns `generate_content` and `draft_items` are refused for a type he does not own (`roleRefusalText("majed", type)`, rule 798, in every mode of the founder's role layer), before any charge.
4. The brief is not a source. It reaches the child batch, its reviewer, the cross-reviewers and the judge as a labelled `<lead_brief>` data block, escaped with `asData`, never as owner notes; a figure, price or claim found only there is unsourced. A task made by a run keeps no `brief`.
5. Running. One item per request, by its position, under `handofflock:{scope}:{id}` for the whole run, with the item's status checked in code; the charge, the agent's cap, the refund, the brand gate, auto-approval and the cross-review hold are `generateBatch`'s, for the login that pressed Run (`generate`, member). Declining needs `drafts` (member). Both are refused to a login no role check accepts.
6. Depth 1. A run is a generation, which offers no tools: nothing a handoff makes can propose another handoff. `createHandoff` has one caller, the chat tool.
7. Retention. A record lives 180 days from its last write; the index keeps the newest 200 ids per brand, and the proposal that pushes an id past 200 removes it by value with its record, lock and auto-run attempt counters (two proposals at once each remove what they pushed off), while a run or decline rewrites a record only while it exists (SET XX), so no record outlives the index; `wipeAccount` deletes every record, lock, auto-run attempt counter and notification marker and index of every brand (`handoffKeys`), and the auto-run's hold (`handoffhold:{scope}`). A record keeps the owner's own message (500 characters) and the logins that proposed, ran or declined it until then; the card's view (`GET /api/handoffs/{id}`) shows neither.
8. Majed's auto-run (opt-in, `flows.md` 35). Off by default; only the owner switches it on (`governance.write`, row 18), only with a cap on an agent Majed hands work to and an explicit confirmation in the request, both checked in code (`govRefusal`). The routines cron (row 91) then starts at most one item a day per brand, and only an item `autoBlock` allows under the handoff's lock with the settings read again: Majed's own proposal, from the app's chat, requested by the account's OWNER (never a member, support or a connected app: `requestedBy.kind` is the chat turn's `by`, set in code), whose card the owner has (the reply carrying it was saved, `shownAt`, and its conversation was not deleted), still proposed, at most 7 days old, for an agent with an owner-set cap, of a type that keeps the 60% margin floor on a cold cache (`autoUnderFloor`: every handoff type since rule 901), in a proposal of at most 3 items and 12 credits. Paused, it retries without reading anything; without a key it skips. It is inside the weekly cap (`isAutonomous` work and the routines' holds), bounded by an attempt counter per item, charged to the item's agent under that cap, logged and notified as the system's `auto_handoff` job, and every task it makes waits for the owner (`forcePending`), even for an auto-approved type. What it may start next is listed on the Governance page with Decline (`autoQueue`, the same rule), so an item whose card scrolled out of its thread can still be declined. The model never decides that anything runs.

## Chat allowance (2026-10-01)

Pricing phase 2 (`src/lib/chat-allowance.ts`, `src/lib/chat-budget.ts`, `flows.md` 37). What code enforces, not prompts:

1. One allowance per account. Every message is counted on the account's counter (`u.email`, the owner's for every seat): the owner's, an admin's, a member's, every brand's and a connected app's (MCP `ask_noor`) alike, against `chatAllowanceOf(u)` from `src/lib/pricing.ts`. No request field names the counter, the plan or the allowance.
2. Counted before the turn. `reserveChatTurn` runs after the session, the text and the burst limit and before `chat()` saves the message or calls a model (rows 30 and 53); INCRBY, so parallel messages cannot pass it. A refused message is in no thread, reached no model and cost nothing. A failed or uncosted turn is given back, a message the workspace is too large for included (no model call is made).
3. Past the allowance only the owner decides (`billing`, row 114): paused (the default), or continued on credits on a paid plan. A member, an admin or a connected app cannot change it, and no chat tool or MCP call does; a founder impersonating the account can, since `billing` is not in `IMPERSONATION_BLOCKED` (section 0.1 item 4). A trial never continues on credits.
4. Bounded cost per message. Nothing is held or taken before an over-allowance message: the gate only checks the balance is at least `chatOverflowHold()` (2 credits since 2026-10-02; 5 before), so the turn's own tools can spend the whole balance. After the reply it is billed at most that: its metered model cost at the floor rate (`maxCostPerCreditUsd`), never more than the balance still holds, the remainder carried under one credit (`User.chatCarryMicro`), in one save under the user lock (`settleChatOverflow`), and the credits billed added to `usage:{email}:{YYYY-MM}:chat_cred`; a failed message is not billed. Every message, inside the allowance or not, has its chat model calls capped at $0.14 in code ($0.35 on Opus until 2026-10-02) (`nextMaxTokens`, with `fitFirstCall` for the first call) and its history trimmed (`trimHistory`); generation asked for in chat stays charged in credits under the agents' caps, which never count chat messages.
5. The founder may override an account's allowance (`POST /api/admin/plan` `chatAllowance`, row 79): a whole number 0..100,000, checked in code; 0 stops chat for the account, "continue on credits" included (`reserveChatTurn` refuses every message with `chat_allowance`).
6. The status every login sees (`ChatStatus`: used, clamped to the allowance; allowance; left; `counted`, the raw count; period; the switch; the messages on credits this month and the credits they were billed; `hold`, the least a balance needs for one and the most one is billed) is the account's, read on the server; the client components import its type only. Deleting the account deletes the counters (section 5 item 17).

## Top-ups (2026-10-01)

Credit top-ups (`src/lib/topups.ts`, `flows.md` 38, rules 440-464). What code enforces, not prompts:

1. Only the founder adds, voids or refunds a pack (row 131, `adminActor`). No owner, member, connected app, chat tool or model output can add credits to a pack: the billing page's buttons only open a WhatsApp or email message to support, and nothing in the app takes a payment.
2. Packs are sold to paid plans only: `addTopup` refuses a trial under the ledger lock (409), whatever the console shows.
3. The pack, the amount paid, the payment date and the reference are checked in code (`parseTopup`) before any read; the account is the body's email, and only its own record and lock are touched.
4. Every change to a pack's credits happens under the per-user ledger lock in one save with the balance: activation and void (`addTopup`, `voidTopup`), spending (`debit`, `settleChatOverflow`), refunds (`returnCredits` with the charge's receipt), renewal and plan changes (`setMonthly`), bonuses and "+ credits" (`addMonthly`, never a pack's credits), and expiry (`settleTopups` on read, `saveSettledTopups` by the daily cron).
5. What the account sees of a pack leaves out what was paid, the payment reference, the payment id and who activated it: the billing page shows `topupView` (`id`, `credits`, `left`, `at`, `expiresAt`) and `GET /api/profile` `topupBalance` (`credits`, `left`, `expiresAt`); the founder sees the rest in the console. The payment reference is never written to `admin:log`.
6. A pack's payment and the partner's commission are decided in code (rules 454-455): "Add top-up" records the payment through `recordPayment` and `settle`, the same commission decision as any payment (attribution, 30 days, 4 months, self and team referrals), under the console's payment id, so a retried request never records it or its commission twice; voiding the pack voids the payment and the commission (`voidPaidTopup`), and the Payments route cannot void a live pack's payment or record a top-up's. No owner, member, model output or request body can mark a payment as a top-up's or move a commission.
7. A refund of unused credits (rules 460-464, founder decision of 2026-10-02) is the founder's alone and is bounded in code: only a live, paid, not yet refunded pack with credits left, only those credits (taken under the ledger lock, so a racing charge never spends them and an in-flight charge's credits are never refunded), only the amount `unusedRefundSar` computes from the pack (never one from the request), and only when the credits left are the ones the founder was shown (`left_changed`); the commission cut is computed from the one refund record, so it cannot be taken twice, and a refund that stopped halfway is finished from the pack's own record ("Finish refund", or any later payment change through `settle`), so no commission stands on refunded money (rule 465). No owner, member, chat tool or model output can ask for or record a refund in the app: the owner asks support, and the billing page and the terms only say that it can be asked for. A refunded pack is not shown to the account (`openLots`), and the transfer's reference, like the payment's, is never logged.

## Calendar feed (2026-10-05)
