Reem phase 3 B1 (`src/lib/feed-token.ts`, `src/lib/secretary-feed.ts`, `flows.md` 41, rules 530-539, 577-585, spec `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-05-secretary-reem-b1-ics-feed-design.md`). What code enforces, not prompts:

1. Named exception to "secrets are never accepted in URLs". `GET /api/secretary/feed/{token}` (row 143) is the one route in the product whose credential is in the URL path, because a calendar app cannot send a header. The founder accepted it on 2026-10-05 for this route only, and it is bounded: (a) the link is a read-only capability for one login's view of dated item titles, one brand: no notes, no descriptions, no phone numbers, no credits, no write, nothing else of the account; (b) 32 random bytes (43 characters), stored only as a SHA-256, shown once, revocable by its login in one press, replaced by renewing; (c) our code never logs it and never echoes it in an error, and the route answers the same empty 404 for every failure without the path; (d) it ends with the login (item 5); (e) a request limit per address and per link (item 3); (f) the card tells the person to renew the link if it was shared by mistake.
2. Residual risk, said plainly. The platform's own request logs (Hostinger's, Vercel's before 2026-10-08) record request paths, so the token is in them. Anyone who can read those logs can read that login's dated titles until the link is renewed. No header-based alternative exists for calendar apps; renew the link if it was shared by mistake (spec B1-R8). Not fixed, and recorded here so it is a known risk and not an unknown one.
3. Limits. Management routes (rows 141 and 142): 20 an hour per login, one bucket (`sec:feed:{login}`); the status read (row 140) has its own, `sec:read:{login}`. The public route: 120 an hour per client IP (`feed:ip:{ip}`), counted before any store read, and 60 an hour per link (`feed:t:{hash}`); both answer 429 with `Retry-After: 3600`. Calendar apps poll about once an hour; fetchers that share addresses (Google's) could reach the address cap at scale. Tracked, accepted for B1 (B1-R6).
4. Not a pure 404. Every refusal of the public route is the same empty 404, with two named exceptions: the address limit answers 429 for any caller, and the per-link limit answers 429 only after a live record was found, so a holder of a live token can tell the record exists. A caller with an unknown token never gets a 429 from the per-link limit (it is only counted once `resolveFeed` has returned a record).
5. A link ends when its login's access ends. Stop and Renew delete the old record; removing a member, a member leaving, `wipeMemberships` and `wipeAccount` revoke the member's or the whole account's links; a member's password reset or change revokes that member's; an owner's password reset and the founder's reset of an owner revoke the owner's and every roster member's (they sign everyone out); an owner's password change revokes only the owner's. Suspension and a seat pause delete nothing, and `feedHolds` refuses the record on every fetch. A record that `sec:feedof:{login}` does not name is dead. A record older than the login's life (a member's `acceptedAt`, an owner's `createdAt`) is refused, so a failed revoke cannot revive a link after a re-invite (B1-R14). A record older than the login's last password event is refused too (B1-R15): the owner's `sessionsAfter` (set by a password change, reset or founder reset), or for a member the member's own `sessionsAfter` or the owner's `teamSessionsAfter` (set by an owner reset or a founder reset, not by an owner's password change), so a revoke that failed after the password was saved cannot leave a link working. A missing or unparsable stamp is not a reason to refuse. `revokeFeed` waits for the lock 20 times and then revokes anyway (B1-R10).
6. What the file may hold. Only open items with a due date that are not snoozed now (as the to-do page hides them), read with the record's own scope, as listed in `flows.md` 41; only title, due date, all-day flag, who owes it (`owedBy`, for the party's name), `id`, `rev` and `updatedAt` are read from an item. Text is escaped per RFC 5545 after control and direction-override characters are removed, so a title cannot add a line or a property; the file carries no price, count, promise or number of ours.
7. Impersonation. A founder impersonating an account cannot create, renew or stop its link (`feed.manage` is in `IMPERSONATION_BLOCKED`): a link handed out by an impersonator would outlive the session. The status read is allowed.
8. Not read-only. The public route's reads are not all pure: `listOpenItems` removes a ghost id (an id in `sec:open` whose item is gone or no longer open) from the open set with a `ZREM`, and the rate counters write. Those are the only writes; the route never changes an item, a record or a setting.
9. Per person and account (multi-workspace M1, 2026-10-19, branch `claude/wave3-m1-expand`, rules 2119, 2131, 2132, 2139; `flows.md` 91 item 6). The management routes pass `linkOwner(u)`: the session's person (`u.actor.pid`), the active account (`acct(u)`), the acting address and whether that account is the person's home, never a value from the request. A link is per `(pid, aid)`: `sec:feedof:{pid}:{aid}` names it, and for the home account the legacy `sec:feedof:{login}` names it too, so an R1 build still resolves and revokes it. The record adds `pid`, `aid` and `home`. A home record is live only while BOTH pointers name it, so a revoke by address (the password reset, the console, an R1 build) ends it even when it gave up waiting for the lock; a record made before M1 (no `pid`) is live while the legacy pointer names it and counts for the home account only. For a record with `pid`, `feedHolds` reads the person, the account, the membership and the account's brands in one round and holds only when: the scope belongs to `aid` (`accountOfScope`), checked before any read; the person is not suspended (a person suspension ends every link, an account suspension only that account's, rule 2131); the account is not suspended and is verified; `roleIn` gives the person a role there; the record is not older than the person's `sessionsAfter`, the membership's `acceptedAt` (the account's `createdAt` for the owner) or the account's `teamSessionsAfter` for a member; and the brand is active. Removing a membership (`deleteMembership`) revokes that membership's links, and a link minted in a race after the removal is refused at read time. A record without `pid` keeps the check of item 5, plus the person suspension (rule 2131: every link of a suspended person ends, a link made before M1 too). The address-based revokes of item 5 (password events, console, account deletion) still name the login and so end the home link only; a link in another account ends at read time with the person's `sessionsAfter` or suspension, or with that account.

## Booking webhook (2026-10-05)

Reem phase 3 B2a (`src/lib/booking-token.ts`, `src/lib/secretary-booking.ts`, `src/app/api/secretary/booking/[token]/route.ts`, `src/app/api/secretary/booking/route.ts`, rules 589-597, spec `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-05-secretary-reem-b2-bookings-design.md` sections 3 and 7). What code enforces, not prompts:

1. Second named exception to "secrets are never accepted in URLs". `POST /api/secretary/booking/{token}` (row 144) carries its locator in the URL path, because Cal.com and Calendly let the owner choose only a URL. It is narrower than the calendar feed's exception (section "Calendar feed") in one way and stronger in another: it grants no read (a holder of the token can neither read an item nor learn anything of the account), and the token alone creates, moves and removes nothing, because every request needs a signature over its body made with a key that is in no URL (what a token holder can still do is use up the link's hourly allowance of unsigned requests, item 3). The key is `HMAC-SHA256(CONNECT_SECRET, "booking:v1:" + sha256(token))` in hex, derived on demand, never stored, shown once to its owner, compared with `sameSecret`; a leaked store gives no way to forge a booking, and rotating `CONNECT_SECRET` ends every link. Bounded: (a) a signed sender can only add, move or remove the one open task a booking becomes, in one brand (`source: "booking"`, title and times, no email, phone, link, note or cancellation text); the title and the party (`owedTo`, up to 80 characters of the guest's name) are written by the outside party who booked and reach the agents through `agenda_get` and `agenda_next`, whose descriptions say that titles of items with source `booking` come from that party and are data, never instructions; (b) 32 random bytes (43 characters), stored only as a SHA-256, shown once, revocable and replaced by renewing; (c) our code never logs the token, body, signature or key and never echoes them in an error; (d) the route answers `Cache-Control: no-store` and `Referrer-Policy: no-referrer`.
2. Residual risk, said plainly. The platform's own request logs (Hostinger's, Vercel's before 2026-10-08) record request paths, so the token is in them. Anyone who can read those logs learns a token but still cannot forge a booking without the key; they could replay a delivery they captured. A replayed create or cancel is a no-op (the claim `sec:book:ext:{scope}:{provider}:{id}` makes a booking once and a cancel marks it), and a Calendly delivery is refused once its timestamp is more than 5 minutes old; a replayed Cal.com reschedule, which carries no timestamp, can move the item back to that delivery's time. Renew the link if it was shared by mistake. Recorded here so it is a known risk and not an unknown one.
3. Limits. 120 an hour per link (`book:t:{hash}`, counted once the record is found; 429 with `Retry-After: 3600`), and 1200 an hour per client IP for misses only (`book:ip:{ip}`, counted after the record read found no live link; a miss over that count is the same empty 404 as any miss). The address bucket never refuses a live link: Cal.com and Calendly deliver every customer's webhooks from a few shared addresses, so a bucket charged before the record read let one party (a stranger with their own provider account, or an orphaned Calendly subscription) refuse every customer of that provider for the hour, and a refused delivery can be a lost booking (spec B2-R6, amended 2026-10-05). Said plainly, the address count bounds nothing a caller can see; misses cost one record read each, and the per-link bucket is what limits traffic to a live link. Past them, a link may create at most 60 items a day (`book:new:{hash}`, checked under the item's lock before `createItem`, so a leaked link cannot spend the owner's `sec:create` allowance of 200 a day). Residual, said plainly: the two buckets count requests before the signature is checked, so whoever holds the booking URL can send up to 120 unsigned requests an hour to that link (`book:t:{hash}`) and so use up its hour, after which the owner's real deliveries get 429 until the hour ends, and a hit never counts against the address bucket, so this cannot spill onto other links behind the same address. They cannot create, move or remove a task without the signature; the effect is delay, not forgery, and renewing the link ends it. The body is read with a 64 KB cap and the signature header is refused over 512 characters (Calendly's `v1` list is otherwise unbounded), both before any parsing.
4. Not a pure 404. Every refusal up to and including a bad or missing signature is the same empty 404 with the same headers (an oversized or unreadable body, a malformed token, no record, a record `sec:bookof:{login}:{provider}` does not name, a failed validity check, a missing, over-long or wrong signature). The named exceptions: the per-link limit answers 429 only after a live record was found (the address count of misses never answers anything but the same empty 404), so a holder of a live token can tell the record exists; a store failure before the signature (the counters, `resolveBook`, `loginHolds`) answers 503; a record that is not well formed answers 500 before the validity check and the signature (it exists only if a token was minted); an error deriving the signing key or running the check answers 500 (`CONNECT_SECRET` unset in production). After a good signature: malformed JSON is 400; handled, ignored or replayed is 200; a held item lock or a store failure inside `applyBooking` is 503 (the provider retries).
5. Validity. `loginHolds(rec)` is the calendar feed's check (`feedHolds`) under a shared name: the login exists and is active, is still of that account, the scope is one of the owner's active brands, and the record is not older than the login's life nor than its last password event. A record that `sec:bookof:{login}:{provider}` does not name is dead. The management routes (rows 145-147, rules 596-597) use the `booking.manage` gate (member level; blocked while a founder impersonates, `IMPERSONATION_BLOCKED`); the Calendly personal access token the owner pastes there is the one secret they handle: it is used for two calls and never stored, logged, put in a URL or echoed (flows section 42, item 10). Where a link ends is item 6.
6. A booking link ends where the calendar feed's does (rule 598), for every provider of the login: removing a member, a member leaving, `wipeMemberships` and `wipeAccount` delete the member's or the account's link records (`revokeBook`/`revokeBooks` beside `revokeFeed`/`revokeFeeds` at every call site); a member's own password reset or change revokes that member's only; an owner's password reset and the founder's reset of an owner revoke the owner's and every roster member's; an owner's password change revokes only the owner's. Booking claims belong to the brand scope and go with the account (`secretaryKeys`), not with a member.
7. Per person and account (multi-workspace M1, 2026-10-19, rules 2119, 2131, 2132, 2139), as the calendar feed's item 9, per provider: `sec:bookof:{pid}:{aid}:{provider}` beside the home account's legacy `sec:bookof:{login}:{provider}`, `lock:book:{pid}:{aid}:{provider}` before the legacy `lock:book:{login}:{provider}`, the same both-pointer rule for a home record, and `loginHolds` with the same checks for a record with `pid`. The management route passes `linkOwner(u)` to `getBookStatus`, `createBook` and `revokeBook`; a Calendly subscription that fails after the link was made revokes that `(pid, aid)` link only.

## Paymob billing (2026-10-08)

Online card payments through Paymob KSA (`src/lib/paymob.ts`, `src/lib/billing.ts`, `src/lib/billing-state.ts`, rows 149-151 and 130, `flows.md` 47, rules 700-722, spec `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-08-paymob-billing-design.md`). On a branch, test mode only: nothing is live until the founder sets the variables (`variables.md` 4a). What code enforces, not prompts:

1. Off unless configured. `paymobConfig()` needs the secret key, public key, HMAC secret and card integration; live keys also need `PAYMOB_LIVE=1`. Off, the three routes answer 404, the cron step does nothing and the billing page keeps the pay links and WhatsApp exactly as before.
2. Who pays. Only the owner, never a member and never the founder impersonating (`billing.pay` is owner-only and in `IMPERSONATION_BLOCKED`). The page shows card buttons only when `can(u, "billing.pay")`.
3. The amount is ours. Every price comes from `src/lib/pricing.ts` (`priceFor`: plan or offer month or pack, plus 15% VAT in halalas); the browser sends only the plan or the pack. The callback's amount, currency, order and live flag must equal our stored intent or nothing is applied.
4. The account is ours. A callback names no account we trust: the account comes from `paymob:intent:{ref}`, found by the random reference we generated, after the HMAC matches. A card token is kept only through our own order pointer and only when its email is the intent's account.
5. Exactly once. A transaction id is applied once (`paymob:done:{id}`, a 60 s lock, `billing.txns`, and a payment id derived from the transaction id so `recordPayment` and `addPaidTopup` record nothing twice). Every write to the account is under `withUserLock`.
6. The card token never leaves `paymob:card:{email}`: no page, API answer or log carries it; the account record keeps only the brand and last four digits. `wipeAccount` and `remove_card` delete it.
7. Renewals are code, not a model: the daily cron charges a due month at most once a day per account on the Moto integration, marks it past due on a decline, and locks it after 7 days of grace; a locked subscription is `trialOver` (every spending gate refuses) and gets no new month of credits. Paying again unlocks it.
8. The founder's plan control (row 79) ends the online subscription when it sets a plan (`billing:manual` in `admin:log`), so a hand-managed account is never locked or charged by the cron. Refunds are the founder's alone (row 130), full transaction only in v1.

## Geidea billing (2026-10-24)

Geidea KSA replaces Paymob as the card provider (`src/lib/geidea.ts`, `src/lib/billing.ts`, row 151b, `flows.md` 109, rules 3900-3917). Everything in "Paymob billing" above holds for Geidea too; the differences:

1. Off unless configured. `geideaConfig()` needs `GEIDEA_PUBLIC_KEY` and `GEIDEA_API_PASSWORD`; live keys also need `GEIDEA_LIVE=1`, which is refused (logged once, gate closed) anywhere but the production deploy and always on staging. With Geidea on, the Paymob webhook answers 404 and only the Geidea one works.
2. The callback signature is the only proof, and it is checked before the body is used (`sameSecret`). A callback can never name an account or an amount: both come from our intent, found by the UUID reference we generated.
3. A second paid order for a reference already paid is not applied twice: it is logged (`geidea_second_order`) for the founder to refund by hand.
4. Saved-card renewals (MIT) run only with `GEIDEA_MIT=1` and only for a card saved under the same provider and agreement; an agreement mismatch (750.005) deletes the card and turns renewal off.
5. Refunds stay the founder's alone (row 130). A refund made in Geidea's own dashboard is only logged (`geidea_external_refund`); it does not end the plan.

## What a plan includes (2026-10-08)

One pure function answers it (`entitlements(u)` in `src/lib/entitlements.ts`, rule 780, spec `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-08-entitlements-agent-scope-design.md` 3.1 and 10). It reads the account's own record and constants; no request supplies any input to it. The video start (rule 781) and the chat route and MCP `ask_noor` (rule 782) ask it for the video models and the agents a plan includes; the other numbers are still a read model beside the old helpers that give the gates theirs. The refusal codes (`plan_required`, `trial_over`, `trial_video_used`) are unchanged; the one new code is `agent_not_included` (403 on the chat route, a tool error on MCP), which no plan triggers yet.

1. `active` and `inactive`. An account is inactive for the first of: `suspended` (the founder's flag); `billing_locked` (a Paymob subscription the cron locked after the grace days, `billingLocked`); `trial_over` (a trial whose `trialEnds` has passed). `inactive` is for display. Today's gates still refuse a locked subscription as `trial_over`, as in `trialOver` (Paymob billing, item 7). An inactive account still reports its plan's own credits, seats and chat numbers.
2. `endsAt`. A trial reads `trialEnds` (ISO). Any other account reads its Paymob subscription's `paidThrough` day (`YYYY-MM-DD`, Riyadh) when it has one, and null when the founder activated it by hand (`billing` is cleared by the plan control, Paymob billing item 8). A locked subscription keeps its paid-through day, the day it ran out.
3. The video list. A trial gets the trial models (`TRIAL_VIDEO_MODELS`) and a paid plan every video model, as the gates did. A dated founder grant on the record (`User.grants.video`) opens every model to a trial; it holds through the end of its UTC `until` day, which must be a day that exists (`isUtcDay`: `2026-02-31` reads as no grant), and counts only with `by` and `at` set. An expired grant is ignored and never deleted. The video start (`renderVideo`) and the inbox's model cards read this list and nothing else (`TRIAL_VIDEO_MODELS` is read only by the catalog and `entitlements.ts`, rule 781). The trial's one-clip limit and the switch that turns a model on or off are separate checks and do not change.
4. Who touches a grant. `entitlements()` is the only reader. The only intended writer is the founder console's plan route (`POST /api/admin/plan` `videoGrant`, rule 783: founder session or `x-admin-key`, under `withUserLock`, the body checked by `parseVideoGrant` and `by` and `at` set by the route, logged before and after in `admin:log`); no other route reads a grant from a request. The founder console can open every video model to one account until a date, and only that. `User.grants` is an optional field, an expand-only change (rule 679; the rollback caveat is in `flows.md` section 52 item 9). The founder sees the grant in the entitlements table of the account's detail (`entitlementView`, rule 784), greyed and marked expired once its day has passed; the console's "Video grant" button only asks for the day and a note and sends them, the server is the one check. The owner sees only the effect of a grant, the video models listed on the billing page's "What your plan includes" card (rule 785); the card does not read `grants`, so who set it and the founder's note are never shown to the owner.
5. The comparisons that remain. A comparison of the plan with `"trial"` outside `entitlements.ts` is a feature deciding by plan name. Rule 786 counts them per file (`tests/entitlements-scan.test.ts`, the `ALLOWED` map, each entry with what the use does), so a new one, or one more in a file, fails the test until it is moved into `entitlements()` or listed as a display use with its reason. The map marks the uses that decide what an account may do, or how much, outside `entitlements()` as ACCESS: the spend gate `trialOver` (`auth.ts`: chat, MCP, media, generation, onboarding, routines and the weekly plan; an ended trial or a locked Paymob subscription may not spend, and `entitlements()` reports them as `trial_over` and `billing_locked`), the trial's one video (`media/jobs.ts`), "continue on credits" (`chat-allowance.ts`), who may buy a pack (`pricing.ts`) and the trial's daily invitation cap (`api/team/invite/route.ts`). They stay as they are, so behaviour did not change; moving them into `entitlements()` is a follow-up.

## The follow-up after a meeting (2026-10-05)

Reem phase 3 B2b (`src/lib/secretary-meeting.ts`, `src/app/api/secretary/meeting/[id]/route.ts`, `.../approve/route.ts` and `.../propose/route.ts`, rules 600-606, spec `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-05-secretary-reem-b2-bookings-design.md` section 5). What code enforces, whatever a prompt says:

1. The model is never the approval. Nothing that proposes (Reem's chat tool `propose_meeting_tasks`, item 8, and `POST .../propose`, row 157, for notes and transcripts) can make an item: they only write the pending set. `approveMeeting`, reached by `POST .../approve` (row 156), is the only path that creates, and it needs a signed-in login with `todo.write`. A transcript that says "approve everything" changes nothing.
2. Only stored proposals are created. A `ref` that is not in the item's stored set is refused; refs are made by the code (`cleanProposals` ignores any ref in its input) and are new on every run, so a stale card cannot approve what a later run proposed. The owner's edits are checked by the proposal limits and refused, not trimmed.
3. Tenant isolation. The set's key is built from `sc(u)` and an item id that passed `SEC_ID` and was read as a live booking item of that same scope (rows 154-156); another brand's item id is 404 on all three routes and reads, deletes and creates nothing in either brand.
4. One approval per set, and no lost update. The set is read and dropped under `sec:lock:{scope}:mprop:{id}` (60 s), so two clicks create each item once; saving a new set and discarding (`DELETE`, row 155) take the same lock, so a re-run or a discard that lands during an approve is written after it. The item ids are chosen and stored before the first create, and an existing id counts as made: a retry after an error never makes an item twice.
5. Approving spends nothing. It makes items (an agent task is a `waiting_on` handoff item that the agent does not run) and calls no model: no credit moves. Creating still spends the account's 200 creations a day and the 500 open items, as any item does.
6. Account deletion removes every pending set (`secretaryKeys` lists each `sec:mprop` key by the `sec:mprops` index, and the index) and every run counter (`sec:mrun`).
7. Proposing from text (row 157, rules 604-606) is the one place the model touches this flow, and it is checked in code: the text is fenced as data and its instructions are ignored; the model's tool output passes `cleanProposals` (an unknown agent dropped, any other field ignored, refs made in code); a transcript's credit is taken before the call under `chargeOnce` and given back on a failure; the length is refused before any charge; notes are limited to 20 a day per account; one run at a time per item; and the text is stored nowhere.
8. Reem's chat tool `propose_meeting_tasks` (rule 607) is the other proposer, and the same limits hold in code: Reem's own turn in the app only, `todo.write` through `TOOL_ACTION`, an item id that is a live booking item of `sc(u)` (one refusal text for every other case), `tasks` through `cleanProposals`, the result only as the pending set (replacing the one that was there: a call after a transcript run replaces the transcript's proposals, and a run after the tool replaces the tool's; the later write wins and the transcript's credit is not given back for it), no model call and no credit. A failed save is an error answer, never "stored". Approval stays the click on the item (`POST .../approve`).

## Agent scope (2026-10-12)

Each agent works only on the active brand's marketing, sales, growth, finance and analytics (spec `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-08-entitlements-agent-scope-design.md` section 6; `flows.md` 54). The checks are in code; the prompt paragraph is only the first layer.

1. Four layers, each with the founder's switch in `agent-scope:modes` (default `prompt` on, the other three `shadow`): the scope paragraph in every prompt (rule 797); the input gate on the owner's words in chat and generation notes (rules 794-796); roles and the offered tools (rules 798-799); the output gate on chat replies, generated items, MCP drafts and, since 2026-10-12, both publish doors (rules 800-802, 1410). Shadow logs and changes nothing; enforce refuses.
2. Who switches. Only the founder, through `POST /api/admin/agent-scope/modes` (route row 182; the only writer of the key, pinned by `tests/agent-scope-scan.test.ts`), with a reason the audit row keeps (rule 1500). `input` and `output` reach `enforce` only after a passed test set on the classifier's current model (rule 1412). Nothing in the owner's app, the chat tools or MCP reads or writes the modes beyond reading them.
3. The test set costs the founder's money: `POST /api/admin/agent-scope/eval` (row 183) runs about 200 classifier calls on the global meter, only when the founder presses Run; no cron and no test calls it.
4. Release. A held publish is released only by the founder (`POST /api/admin/agent-scope/release`, row 180), for that exact text (`itemHash`); an edit is checked again, and the owner still presses Publish. The brand must be one of the account's own; the task key is built from `scopeFor(email, brandId)` after both are checked.
5. The refusal log (`agent-scope:log:{day}`): one line per check the classifier ran or failed, with the account's email, brand id, agent, layer, place, verdict, mode, item id and the first 200 characters of the text. Founder only (`GET /api/admin/agent-scope`, row 181); 31 days from the day's first line, newest 5000 a day. The privacy policy names it in both languages (rule 1415).


## Account security (2026-10-16, branch `claude/beautiful-blackburn-50e6d7`)

Rules 1930-1956; routes rows 242-248; flows in docs/system/flows.md "Customer two-step sign-in", "Signed-in devices", "Changing the sign-in address" and "Sign in with Google".

1. Every security record is keyed by the ACTING login the session resolves (`actingLogin` in src/lib/own-password.ts: the member's own email for a member, the account email for the owner), never by an address, id or scope from the request. A member manages its own login only, never the owner's or another member's.
2. `mfa.manage`, `email.change` and `google.manage` are member-level and in `IMPERSONATION_BLOCKED`. Every change needs the acting login's own password (wrong ones counted per login and hour, `allow` before the compare).
2a. Passkeys (2026-10-20, rules 2400-2429, branch `claude/wave3c-passkeys`, rows 254-257, the console off switch row 257; flows in docs/system/flows.md section 100). `passkeys.manage` is member-level and in `IMPERSONATION_BLOCKED`; GET checks it too, so a founder viewing an account never reads its passkeys. A passkey set is keyed by the pid `actingLogin` resolves, never by a request value. Adding and removing need a fresh proof in the same request (the password, or a TOTP or recovery code when two-step is on), checked before any person lock; renaming needs only the session. A login with no password and two-step off cannot add or remove (403 `reauth_unavailable`) until it sets a password through "Forgot password". The list never carries a credential id or public key.
3. Password sign-in with two-step on stops at a challenge (POST /api/auth/login answers 401 `mfa_required` and sets `thakeen_mfa`); the session exists only after the code. Google sign-in goes through the same `finishLogin`, so it can not skip the code or a refusal.
4. Google links only to an owner whose address is already proved, or to the signed-in login from settings. It never takes over an unproved password account or a member login by address.
5. Account deletion (`wipeAccount`) first turns renewal off and deletes the saved Paymob card under the account lock (`stopRenewals`, rule 1955); a renewal already past its card read re-checks both under that lock before charging (rule 1956). It then removes every login's two-step, address-change, Google and device records and ends every session (rule 1954).

## Admin workspace view and suspension end dates (2026-10-17, branch `claude/wave2-admin-team-suspension-853778`, rules 2070-2083)

- Console permissions by staff role live in `STAFF_MAY` (`src/lib/roles.ts`): `workspace.view` is `owner` and `support`; `passkeys.switch` (row 257, 2026-10-20, rule 2426) is `owner` only; `data.convert` (row 265, 2026-10-24, rule 4101) is `owner` only. `staffMay(role, action)` is false for an action not in the table, a role that is not a string, or a role not listed. `consoleRole(req)` (`src/lib/auth.ts`) gives the stored staff role for a staff session and `owner` for the key session, the `x-admin-key` header and the founder's customer session; no console actor is `null`, which `staffMay` refuses.
- The workspace view (row 249) is read only and has no write action anywhere. Each open is one audit row. It never shows password hashes, session or device details, invite ids or tokens, brand files, tasks or conversations. Since multi-workspace M1 a member's other workspaces show only as a count (`alsoIn`), never by name or id.
- Suspension (`POST /api/admin/users/{email}` `suspend`, row 81) takes `for` (`indefinite`, `7`, `30` or `date`) and `date` (`YYYY-MM-DD`, the account opens at the start of that day in Riyadh, at most 365 days away); anything else is 400 `bad_until` and nothing changes. Without `for` it is indefinite as before. The audit rows of `suspend` and `unsuspend` carry `before` and `after` as `{suspended, until}`.
- An ended suspension stops blocking at read time (`isSuspended`): `currentUser`, sign-in, password reset, MCP, team, entitlements and every cron that skips suspended accounts read it through that one function. The first sign-in of the account (or a member) or staff opening the account after the end clears the field and writes `suspension.expired` (actor `system`).

## Agent tool authority (2026-10-20, branch `claude/lucid-faraday-kq261a`, rules 2430-2436)

What each agent may do from a conversation, set by the owner and checked in code (flows.md section 94, `src/lib/tool-authority.ts`).

1. Who sets it. Only `governance.write` (the owner), through `PUT /api/governance` and `POST /api/governance/rollback` (rows 18 and 104), into `gov:{sc(u)}` like every governance field, with a history row per changed capability (`toolPolicy.{agent}.{capability}`). Members and admins read it on the Governance page with the controls disabled. No chat tool, MCP tool, instruction or prompt reads or writes it except through those routes.
2. What it keys on. The talking agent of the turn (`ctx.agent`, set by chat() from the route's checked agent id), never a request field. A call with no agent (tests, onboarding) is not governed.
3. Off. The tool is not offered in that agent's turn (`toolsFor(agent, policy)`), and `runTool` refuses a call that names it anyway from the setting read at the moment of the call, after the role gate and before any read, charge or write (rule 2432). Over MCP (`ask_noor`, Noor's turn) the same.
4. Ask first (only `produce` and `images`). The model's call never runs: in the app a spend card is stored with `reason: "ask"` whatever the owner's message said; over MCP and outside a chat turn it is refused (rule 2433). The owner's Run (`POST /api/chat/spend/{id}`, `generate`) is the approval.
5. Revalidated at Run (rule 2434). `decideSpend` reads the setting again for the card's agent before any charge; if the capability is now off the answer is 409 `revoked`, nothing is charged and the card stays open.
6. Not governed here: reading tools, the secretary's tools, and proposals that already wait for a person (brand, instruction, away, handoff, core-file cards). Scheduled work (weekly plan, routines) and the Generate page are governed by the caps and switches above them, not by this policy. MCP's own `generate_content` and `save_draft` (not a chat turn) are not governed by it either: the bearer is always the owner, and the call is the owner's own request, as on the Generate page (rule 3015, re-checked 2026-10-22).
7. Majed's auto-run (rule 3014, 2026-10-22). The one spend here with no person at all: `autoBlock` starts an item only while its agent's `produce` is on; `off` or `ask` leaves it for a login's Run (`authority`). A login's Run of a handoff card is that person's request (it needs `generate`, the Generate page's action) and is not refused by the policy.

## Reference files: on-demand reading and provenance (2026-10-20, branch `claude/skills-stub-provenance-79a1f1`, rules 2523-2532)

1. Reading a reference in the chat. `read_file` stays a free reading tool for every role that can chat (owner, admin, member): it is not in `TOOL_ACTION`, charges nothing and writes nothing, and reads only the workspace of the turn (`sc(u)`). The agent reads a file only inside a chat turn the person started, after the input gate.
2. Provenance on the instructions page. Every signed-in role that opens the page sees each attached file's source and age and the out-of-date badge; it is read from `sc(u)` only, and the write controls stay behind `instructions.write`.
3. Who sets the source. Only code: each writer passes its own kind. No request field, instruction, tool or prompt sets `WFile.src`; the files route always stores `owner`.

## Customers as workspaces (2026-10-23, branch `claude/m2-console-workspaces-e22a0c`, rules 3720-3749)

- Both new routes (rows 249b, 249c) reuse the console permission `workspace.view` (`owner` and `support`); `src/lib/roles.ts` is not edited. A role this code has never heard of gets 403 from both.
- The pages `/{lang}/admin/users`, `/users/accounts` and `/users/{email}` sit behind `requireAdmin` like every other console page. Only the account table has write controls, and they are the ones that existed before (`/api/admin/users/{email}` and `/api/admin/plan`).
- A staff member who searches a member's address learns which workspaces that person belongs to. That is the reason for the `workspace.search` audit row, written before the answer, naming the staff actor and the person.
- The per-member numbers on the workspace page are computed from the account's own activity rows and only for logins on its roster; the same person's work in another workspace is never read into this one.
